# CRIMINAL CODE

CRIMINAL CODE
Codification 0
Official Registry Supplement 147 of 22-Jan-1971 Last modification: 10-Feb-2014
Status: In force

GENERAL NOTE:
The Comprehensive Organic Criminal Code shall enter into force one hundred eighty days from its publication in the Official Registry, given by Law No. 00, published in Official Registry Supplement 180 of February 10, 2014, which shall repeal this Code.

THE LEGAL COMMISSION

Considering:

That subsequent to the year 1959 in which the codification of Common Criminal Law was carried out by the Permanent Legislative Commission, numerous and important reforms have been issued that need to be adequately incorporated into the Criminal Code; and,

In exercise of the authority conferred upon it by Supreme Decree No. 55, of July 8, 1970, issued by the President of the Republic, Dr. José María Velasco Ibarra.

Resolves:

First.- To proceed with the codification of the Criminal Code and order its publication in the Official Registry so that it shall have binding force.

Second.- To order that henceforth the new numbering of its articles be cited; and,
Third.- To order that this Resolution be inserted in all editions of the Criminal Code codified by the Legal Commission.

## FIRST BOOK

ON OFFENSES, ON PERSONS RESPONSIBLE FOR OFFENSES AND ON PENALTIES IN GENERAL

### TITLE I

ON CRIMINAL LAW

SOLE CHAPTER

###### Art. 1

Criminal laws are all those that contain any precept sanctioned with the threat of a penalty.

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 201
•	LAW ON NARCOTIC AND PSYCHOTROPIC SUBSTANCES, Arts. 124
•	CODE OF EXECUTION OF PENALTIES AND SOCIAL REHABILITATION, Arts. 11
•	ORGANIC LAW ON LAND TRANSPORT, TRAFFIC AND ROAD SAFETY, Arts. 1

###### Art. 2

No one may be punished for an act that is not expressly declared an offense by criminal law, nor suffer a penalty that is not established therein.

The offense must be declared, and the penalty established, prior to the act.

An act ceases to be punishable if a law subsequent to its commission removes it from the number of offenses; and, if a conviction has already been handed down, the penalty shall be extinguished, whether or not it has begun to be served.

If the penalty established at the time of sentencing differs from that which was in force when the offense was committed, the less severe one shall be applied.

In general, all subsequent laws concerning the effects and extinction of actions and penalties shall be applied insofar as they are favorable to offenders, even if there is a final judgment.

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 76
•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 2
•	CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 8
•	ORGANIC CODE OF THE JUDICIAL FUNCTION, Arts. 7, 157

###### Art. 3

It is presumed by law that criminal laws are known to all those over whom they govern. Consequently, no one may invoke ignorance thereof as an excuse.

Concordances:
•	CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 6, 13
•	TAX CODE, Arts. 312

###### Art. 4

Extensive interpretation is prohibited in criminal matters. The judge must adhere strictly to the letter of the Law. In cases of doubt it shall be interpreted in the sense most favorable to the accused.

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 76
•	CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 3, 18, 19
•	LABOR CODE, Arts. 7

Case law:

•	Judicial Gazette, JURISDICTIONAL DISPUTE, 21-Jun-1973
•	Judicial Gazette, JURISDICTION IN CRIMINAL MATTERS, 21-Mar-1984
•	Judicial Gazette, EQUITY CRITERION, 29-Aug-1996

###### Art. 5

Any offense committed within the territory of the Republic, by Ecuadorians or foreigners, shall be tried and punished in accordance with Ecuadorian laws, except as otherwise provided by Law.

The following are deemed offenses committed in the territory of the Republic:

Those committed aboard Ecuadorian naval vessels or aircraft, whether military or merchant, except in cases where merchant vessels are subject to foreign criminal law, in accordance with International Law; and those committed within the premises of an Ecuadorian Legation in a foreign country.

The offense is understood to have been committed in the territory of the State when the effects of the

action or omission that constitutes it are to occur in Ecuador or in places subject to its jurisdiction.

A national or foreigner who commits any of these offenses outside the national territory shall be punished in accordance with Ecuadorian law:

1a.- Crimes against the personality of the State;
2a.- Crimes of forgery of State seals, or use of forged seals;
3a.- Crimes of counterfeiting currency or bank notes of legal tender in the State, or stamped securities, or Ecuadorian public credit instruments;
4a.- Crimes committed by public officials in the service of the State, abusing their powers or violating the duties inherent to their functions;
5a.- Offenses against International Law; and,
6a.- Any other offense for which special provisions of the Law or international conventions establish the application of Ecuadorian law.

Foreigners who commit any of the offenses detailed above shall be tried and punished in accordance with Ecuadorian laws, provided they are apprehended in Ecuador, or their extradition is obtained.

###### Art. 6

Extradition shall be carried out in the cases and in the manner determined by the Constitution, the Law on the matter and the Code of Criminal Procedure.

Concordances:

•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 7

###### Art. 7

An Ecuadorian who, outside the cases contemplated in the preceding article, commits in a foreign country a crime for which Ecuadorian law has established a penalty of deprivation of liberty greater than one year, shall be punished according to Ecuadorian criminal law, provided he or she is found in Ecuadorian territory.

Note: Article Amended by First Final Provision of Law No. 24 published in Official Registry Supplement 144 of August 18, 2000.
Note: Article Amended by First Final Provision of Law No. 24, republished in Official Registry 152 of August 30, 2000.

###### Art. 8

When criminal law makes any legal effect dependent on the passage of time, all days shall be counted for the computation of the legal period.  

Concordances:

•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 6
•	CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 33, 34, 35

###### Art. 9

When two criminal provisions are in opposition, the special one shall prevail.  

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 76
•	CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 12
•	ORGANIC LAW ON LAND TRANSPORT, TRAFFIC AND ROAD SAFETY, Arts. 12

### TITLE II

ON OFFENSES IN GENERAL

#### CHAPTER I

On Consummated Offenses and Attempt

###### Art. 10

Offenses are imputable acts sanctioned by criminal laws, and are

divided into crimes and contraventions, according to the nature of the particular penalty.  

Concordances:

•	LAW ON NARCOTIC AND PSYCHOTROPIC SUBSTANCES, Arts. 56
•	TAX CODE, Arts. 314, 315
•	ORGANIC LAW ON LAND TRANSPORT, TRAFFIC AND ROAD SAFETY, Arts. 106, 107

Jurisprudence:
•	Judicial Gazette, CLASSIFICATION OF OFFENSES, 31-Aug-1929

###### Art. 11

No one may be punished for an act provided by law as an offense, if the harmful or dangerous event on which the existence of the offense depends is not a consequence of his action or omission.  

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 76, 88
•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 2, 88
•	ORGANIC LAW ON LAND TRANSPORT, TRAFFIC AND ROAD SAFETY, Arts. 106

Jurisprudence:

•	Judicial Gazette, ABOLITION OF THE DEATH PENALTY, 31-Mar-1884
•	Judicial Gazette, CASSATION FOR MURDER, 11-Jun-2009

###### Art. 12

Failing to prevent an event, when one has a legal obligation to prevent it, is equivalent to causing it.  

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK III)

, Arts. 1010
•	ORGANIC LAW ON LAND TRANSPORT, TRAFFIC AND ROAD SAFETY, Arts. 106
•	ORGANIC LAW ON PUBLIC ENTERPRISES, LOEP, Arts. 60

###### Art. 13

Whoever voluntarily executes a punishable act shall be responsible for it, and shall incur the penalty established for the resulting offense, even if the harm that the offender intended to cause varies, or falls upon a different person from the one he intended to harm.  

In case preexisting, simultaneous or supervening causes, independent of the will of the perpetrator, concur with the punishable act, the following rules shall be observed:

If the event, which was not in the intention of the perpetrator, is realized as a consequence of the sum of one or more of these causes with the punishable act, the offender shall be liable for a preterintentional crime.  

If the event occurs as a result of one or more of said causes, without being added to the punishable act, the perpetrator shall be liable only for the offense constituted by the act itself.  

Jurisprudence:
•	Judicial Gazette, PRETERINTENTIONAL HOMICIDE, 24-Apr-1974
•	Judicial Gazette, PREINTENTIONAL CRIME, 08-Mar-1976
•	Judicial Gazette, PRETERINTENTIONAL CRIME, 11-May-1979
•	Judicial Gazette, PRETERINTENTIONAL CRIME, 30-Jul-1981
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIMINAL LIABILITY, 19-Jul-1993
•	Judicial Gazette, ILLICIT POSSESSION OF NARCOTICS, 16-May-1997
•	Judicial Gazette, SIMPLE AND PRETERINTENTIONAL HOMICIDE, 07-Jan-2003

###### Art. 14.- The offense is intentional or negligent

The intentional offense, which is one in which there is intent to cause harm, is:
Intentional, when the harmful or dangerous event, which is the result of the action or omission on which the law makes the existence of the offense depend, was foreseen and intended by the agent as a consequence of his own action or omission; and;  
Preterintentional, when from the action or omission there derives a harmful or dangerous event more serious than that intended by the agent.  

The offense is negligent when the event, being foreseeable but not intended by the agent, occurs by reason of negligence, recklessness, lack of skill, or failure to observe law, regulations or orders.  

Concordances:
•	CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 29
•	TAX CODE, Arts. 316
•	ORGANIC LAW ON LAND TRANSPORT, TRAFFIC AND ROAD SAFETY, Arts. 108, 109
•	ORGANIC LAW ON PUBLIC ENTERPRISES, LOEP, Arts. 60

Jurisprudence:

•	Judicial Gazette, CORPUS DELICTI, 23-Mar-1955

###### Art. 15

The action or omission provided by law as an offense shall not be punishable when it is the result of fortuitous event or force majeure.  

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 76
•	CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 30
•	ORGANIC LAW ON LAND TRANSPORT, TRAFFIC AND ROAD SAFETY, Arts. 110

###### Art. 16

Whoever performs suitable acts unequivocally leading to the commission of a crime is liable for attempt if the action is not consummated or the event does not occur.  

If the perpetrator voluntarily desists from the action, he is subject only to the penalty for the acts executed, provided that these constitute a different offense, except when the law, in special cases, qualifies mere attempt as a crime.  
If he voluntarily prevents the event, he is subject to the penalty established for attempt, reduced by one-third or one-half.  

Contraventions are punishable only when they have been consummated.  

Concordances:

•	TAX CODE, Arts. 326

###### Art. 17

Conspiracy and proposal to commit a crime shall be punished only in the cases determined by law.  

Conspiracy is understood to exist when two or more persons agree on the execution of a crime; and proposal exists when one who has resolved to commit it proposes its commission to one or more other persons.  

If the conspiracy or proposal, even in the case of being punished by law, ceases to produce effects because its perpetrators voluntarily desisted from execution before judicial proceedings were initiated against them, no penalty shall be imposed on them.  

Jurisprudence:
•	Judicial Gazette, EXCULPATORY CIRCUMSTANCES, 13-May-1993

#### CHAPTER II

On the Circumstances of the Offense

###### Art. 18

There is no offense when the act is ordered by law, or determined by final resolution of competent authority, or when the accused was impelled to commit it by a force he could not resist.  

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

, Arts. 1472
•	CODE OF EXECUTION OF PENALTIES AND SOCIAL REHABILITATION, Arts. 46

Jurisprudence:

•	Judicial Gazette, MILITARY CRIMINAL JURISDICTION, 12-Apr-1916
•	Judicial Gazette, MILITARY CRIMINAL JURISDICTION, 09-Jun-1916
•	Judicial Gazette, NONEXISTENCE OF OFFENSE, 30-Aug-2004

###### Art. 19

No person commits an infraction of any kind who acts in necessary defense of his person, provided that the following circumstances concur: actual unlawful aggression; rational necessity of the means employed to repel said aggression, and lack of

sufficient provocation on the part of the person defending himself.  

Jurisprudencia:

•	Gaceta Judicial, LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 09-feb-1884
•	Gaceta Judicial, MUERTE POR LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 18-abr-1977
•	Gaceta Judicial, LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 17-nov-1978
•	Gaceta Judicial, LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 19-mar-1980
•	Gaceta Judicial, LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 29-jul-1981
•	Gaceta Judicial, LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 18-dic-1981
•	Gaceta Judicial, LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 04-feb-1987

###### Art. 20

It shall be understood that the circumstances enumerated in the preceding article concur, if the act has taken place in defense against the perpetrators of robbery or pillage executed with violence; or attacking an arsonist, or one who robs or steals in a fire, when they are apprehended in flagrante delicto; or repelling during the night the scaling or breaking of fences, walls or entrances to an inhabited house or apartment or their dependencies, unless it is established that the perpetrator could not believe in an attack against persons, whether considering the direct purpose of the individual who was scaling or breaking, or the resistance that the intentions of the latter should have encountered.  

Concordancias:

•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 162

Jurisprudencia:
•	Gaceta Judicial, ROBO. REPARACIONES CIVILES, 27-ene-1887
•	Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE ROBO AGRAVADO, 22-oct-2003

###### Art. 21

No person commits an infraction who acts in defense of another person, provided that the first two circumstances of Art. 19 concur and that, in case provocation of the aggressor has preceded, the person defending has not taken part in it.  

Nota: Incluida Fe de Erratas, publicada en Registro Oficial 165 de 17 de Febrero de 1971
, y nuevamente publicada en Registro Oficial 173 de 3 de Marzo de 1971 .

###### Art. 22

There is also no infraction when a person kills or causes injuries to another at the moment of being a victim of a crime of sexual abuse or rape.  
Nota: El adulterio como delito fue suprimido por el artículo final del Código de Procedimiento Penal, Ley No. 143, publicada en Registro Oficial 511 de 10 de Junio de 1983 .  

Nota: Artículo sustituido por Ley No. 2, publicada en Registro Oficial 45 de 23 de Junio del 2005 .

###### Art. 23

There is no infraction in blows that are given without causing wounds or serious injuries, to those guilty of theft or robbery, when they are surprised in flagrante delicto, or with the stolen or robbed things.  

Concordancias:
•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 162

###### Art. 24

No penalty shall be imposed on one who, in the necessity of avoiding a harm, executes an act that produces damage to another's property, provided that the harm that was sought to be avoided is real, that it is greater than that caused to prevent it, and that there has been no other practicable and less harmful means to prevent it.  

Concordancias:

•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 194

###### Art. 25

Homicide, wounds and blows are excusable, when they are provoked by blows, wounds or other serious physical mistreatment, or strong attacks on the honor or dignity, inflicted in the same act upon the perpetrator of the act, or upon his spouse, ascendants, descendants, siblings, or relatives by affinity within the second degree.  

The infractions determined in the preceding paragraph are also excusable, when they are the result of an excess of legitimate defense.  

Concordancias:

•	CODIGO CIVIL (TITULO PRELIMINAR), Arts. 22, 23

Jurisprudencia:

•	Gaceta Judicial, CIRCUNSTANCIAS EXCUSANTES, 24-feb-1981
•	Gaceta Judicial, EXCESO DE LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 28-ago-1985
•	Gaceta Judicial, MUERTE POR LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 27-ago-1999
•	Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE ASESINATO Y NO LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 16-abr-2001
•	Gaceta Judicial, EXCESO DE LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 10-ene-2002

###### Art. 26

Said infractions are equally excusable when they have been committed repelling during the day the scaling or breaking of fences, walls, or entrances

of an inhabited house, or its dependencies; except when it is established that the perpetrator of the act could not believe in an attack against persons, whether considering the direct purpose of the individual who attempted the scaling or breaking, or the effect of the resistance that the intentions of the latter would encounter.

###### Art. 27

Nota: Suspendido el efecto de este artículo. Dado por Resolución del Tribunal de Garantías Constitucionales, publicado en Registro Oficial 224 de 3 de Julio de 1989 .  

Nota: Artículo derogado por Ley No. 105, publicada en Registro Oficial 365 de 21 de Julio de 1998 .

###### Art. 28

The grounds for excuse enumerated in Arts. 25 and 26, are not admissible if the guilty party commits the infraction upon the person of his ascendants.

###### Art. 29

Mitigating circumstances are all those which, referring to the impelling causes of the infraction, to the physical and intellectual state and capacity of the offender, to his conduct with respect to the act and its consequences, diminish the gravity of the infraction, or the alarm occasioned in society, or make known the little or no dangerousness of the perpetrator, as in the following cases:

1o.- Provocations, threats or insults preceding on the part of the assailed, these not being among those qualified as circumstances of excuse;  
2o.- The guilty party being over sixty years of age;  
3o.- The offender having sought to repair the harm he caused, or to prevent the pernicious consequences of the event, with spontaneity and zeal; 4o.- Having committed the offense out of fear or under superable violence;  
5o.- Presenting himself voluntarily to justice, being able to have eluded its action through flight or concealment;  
6o.- Exemplary conduct observed by the guilty party subsequent to the infraction;  
7o.- Prior conduct of the offender that clearly reveals that he is not a dangerous individual;  
8o.- Rusticity of the offender, of such nature that it clearly reveals that he committed the punishable act out of ignorance;  
9o.- Acting impelled by motives of particular moral or social value; 10o.- Spontaneous confession, when it is true;  
11o.- In crimes against property, when indigence, the numerous family, or lack of work have placed the offender in an exceptional situation; or when a public calamity made it very difficult for him to obtain means of subsistence honestly, at the time he committed the infraction; and,
12o.- In crimes against property, the small value of the damage caused, relative to the possibilities of the offended party.  

Concordancias:

•	LEY DE SUSTANCIAS ESTUPEFACIENTES Y PSICOTROPICAS, Arts. 85, 86

###### Art. 29

A.- For the crimes of human trafficking and sexual crimes, mitigating circumstances shall not be considered, except for the following:

l. That contemplated in numeral 5 of article 29; and,
2. That the suspect, accused or defendant effectively collaborates with the authorities in the investigation of the crime.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 2, published in Official Register 45 of June 23, 2005.  

Art. ....- Mitigating circumstances in crimes of function of military or police servants.- For the imposition of penalties for crimes of function of military or police servants, the mitigating circumstances contemplated in this code for common crimes shall be applied, when these circumstances concur in the act.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

###### Art. 30

Aggravating circumstances are, when they are not constitutive or modifying of the offense, all those that increase the malice of the act, or the alarm that the offense produces in society, or establish the dangerousness of its perpetrators, as in the following cases:

1o.- To execute the offense with treachery, treason, snares or with assurance; or for price, reward or promise; or by means of flood, shipwreck, fire, poison, mines, train derailment, prohibited weapons, or other means that endanger other persons in addition to the victim; or employing cunning, disguise, fraud; or with cruelty or brutality, making use of any torture or other means to increase and prolong the victim's pain; or making it impossible for the victim to defend himself, whether by depriving him of the use of reason, or by employing accomplices in the commission of the crime; or having committed it as a means to commit another; or perpetrating the act with the author taking advantage of his condition of authority, or deliberately entering the victim's house, or after having received some benefit from the victim;  
2o.- To take advantage of fire, shipwreck, sedition, tumult or popular commotion or other calamity or public or private misfortune, to execute the offense;  
3o.- To carry it out with the aid of armed people, or of persons who ensure impunity; or falsely taking the title, insignia or name of authority; or by means of a false order from it; or with contempt or offense to the depositaries of public power; or in the very place where they are exercising their functions; or where a religious ceremony of any cult permitted or tolerated in the Republic is being celebrated; 4o.- To execute the punishable act deliberately seeking an uninhabited place or nighttime; or in a gang; or abusing the friendship or trust granted to the author; or with climbing or breaking; with lock picks or false and master keys; or with violence;  
5o.- That the author is being pursued or is a fugitive for a previous crime; having increased or attempted to increase the harmful consequences of the offense; committing the act against a foreign consular or diplomatic agent; and, in crimes against property, causing damage of relevant gravity, in consideration of the victim's conditions.  
6o. To execute the offense for reasons of discrimination, referring to place of birth, age, sex, ethnicity, color, social origin, language, religion, political affiliation, economic position, sexual orientation, health status, disability or difference of any other nature.  
Note: Literal 6o. added by Law No. 2, published in Official Register 45 of June 23, 2005.

###### Art. 30

A.- In the case of sexual crimes and human trafficking, the following shall be considered as aggravating circumstances, when they are not constitutive or modifying of the offense and shall be applied without prejudice to the general aggravating circumstances indicated in the preceding article:

1.	If the victim is a person over sixty years of age or under eighteen years of age, a person with a disability or one of those whom the Civil Code considers incapable;  
2.	That the victim is, at the time of the commission of the crime, in public or private establishments, such as those of health, education, worship, research, assistance or refuge, in social rehabilitation centers or in police or military precincts, or other similar places;  
3.	To take advantage of the fact that the victim is going through a situation of vulnerability, extreme economic need or abandonment;  
4.	Having infected the victim with a serious, incurable or fatal disease, or having produced injuries that cause permanent incapacity, mutilations, loss or disablement of organs, physical disability, emotional disturbance, psychological or mental disorder;  
5.	If the victim was or became pregnant, or if she was in puerperium, or if she miscarried as a consequence of the commission of the crime;  
6.	If the victim was physically or mentally incapacitated;  
7.	That the offender has some type of power and/or authority relationship over the victim, or if he is an adopter, guardian, curator or if he has under his care, for any reason, the victim;  
8.	To share the family environment with the victim;  
9.	To know the victim prior to the commission of the crime;  
10.	If the sexual crime has been committed as a form of torture, or for purposes of intimidation, degradation, humiliation, discrimination, revenge or punishment;  
11.	If the crime has been committed by public officials, teachers or ministers of some cult, who have abused their position to commit it, by health professionals and persons responsible for patient care; or any other class of professional or person who has abused his function or position to commit the crime; and,
12.	Having used to commit the crime, some substance that alters the knowledge or will of the victim.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 2, published in Official Register 45 of June 23, 2005.  

Art. ...- Aggravating circumstances of crimes of function of police servants are, in addition to the circumstances indicated in article 30, the following:

1.	If the crime is committed with the use of weapons, or in serious internal commotion; or,
2.	If the action produces the failure of a police operation.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art. ...- Aggravating circumstances of crimes of function of military servants are, in addition to the circumstances indicated in article 30, any of the following:

1.	If the crime is committed with the use of weapons or in armed conflict; or,
2.	If the action produces the failure of a military operation.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

###### Art. 31

It shall be deemed an aggravating circumstance of the offense if the victim is the spouse, cohabitant, relative up to the fourth degree of consanguinity and second degree of affinity, or is an ascendant or descendant of the offender.

Note: Article substituted by Law No. 2, published in Official Register 45 of June 23, 2005.

### TITLE III

ON IMPUTABILITY AND PERSONS RESPONSIBLE FOR OFFENSES

#### CHAPTER I

On responsibility

###### Art. 32

No one may be punished for an act provided for in the Law as an offense, if he or she has not committed it with will and consciousness.

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

, Arts. 1467

Case law:
•	Judicial Gazette, ILLICIT TRAFFICKING OF NARCOTIC SUBSTANCES, 19-Aug-2009

###### Art. 33

All offenses are deemed conscious and voluntary acts, unless

proven otherwise; except when from the circumstances that preceded or accompanied the act, it may be deduced that there was no harmful intent in committing it.

Concordances:
•	CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 32
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

, Arts. 1467

Case law:
•	Judicial Gazette, FALSE DOCUMENT, 19-Feb-2009

###### Art. 34

A person is not responsible who, at the moment the action or omission was carried out, was, by reason of illness, in such a mental state that he or she was unable to understand or to will.
If the act has been committed by a mentally insane person, the judge hearing the case shall order his or her confinement in a psychiatric hospital; and he or she may not be released except with a hearing of the public prosecutor and prior satisfactory report of two physicians appointed by the judge and who shall preferably be psychiatrists, regarding the full restoration of the intellectual faculties of the confined person.

Concordances:

•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 219

Case law:
•	Judicial Gazette, NON-IMPUTABILITY, 24-Nov-1998
•	Judicial Gazette, NON-IMPUTABILITY OF PARANOID, 13-Mar-2002

###### Art. 35

A person who, at the moment of carrying out the criminal act was, by reason of illness, in such a mental state that, although his or her capacity to understand or to will was diminished, it did not absolutely prevent him or her from doing so, shall be liable for the offense committed, but the penalty shall be reduced as established by this Code.

###### Art. 36

When the action or omission that the Law has provided for as an offense is, as to the fact and not the law, the result of deception by another person, the person who induced the deceived person to commit it shall be liable for the act of the deceived person.

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

, Arts. 1467

###### Art. 37

In the case of intoxication of the active subject of the offense, or intoxication by narcotic substances, the following rules shall be observed:
1a.- If the intoxication derived from fortuitous event or force majeure deprived the perpetrator of knowledge at the moment he or she committed the act, there shall be no liability;
2a.- If the intoxication was not complete, but greatly diminished knowledge, there shall be mitigated liability;
3a.- Intoxication not derived from fortuitous event or force majeure neither excludes, nor mitigates, nor aggravates liability;
4a.- Premeditated intoxication, for the purpose of committing the offense, or of preparing an excuse, is an aggravating circumstance; and,
5a.- Habitual intoxication is an aggravating circumstance. A person who engages in the use of alcoholic beverages, or frequently goes about in a state of intoxication, is considered a habitual drunkard.

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 30
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

, Arts. 2218
•	ORGANIC LAW ON LAND TRANSPORT TRAFFIC AND ROAD SAFETY, Arts. 126

Case law:

•	Judicial Gazette, EXEMPTION FROM CRIMINAL LIABILITY, 04-Dec-1889
•	Judicial Gazette, INTOXICATION, 10-Jun-1950
•	Judicial Gazette, NON-IMPUTABILITY IN MURDER, 30-Sep-1999

###### Art. 38

The rules of the preceding article shall be observed, respectively, in cases of intoxication by narcotic substances.

Concordances:
•	ORGANIC LAW ON LAND TRANSPORT TRAFFIC AND ROAD SAFETY, Arts. 126

###### Art. 39

When a deaf-mute commits a crime, he or she shall not be punished if it is fully established that he or she acted without consciousness and will; but he or she may be placed in a suitable educational institution, for up to ten years; and if it is established that he or she acted with consciousness and will, a penalty not exceeding half nor less than one-quarter of that established for the crime shall be applied.

###### Art. 40

Persons who have not reached 18 years of age shall be subject to the

Code on Children and Adolescents.

Note: Article substituted by Law No. 2, published in Official Register 45 of June 23, 2005.

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 175
•	CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 21
•	ORGANIC LAW ON LAND TRANSPORT TRAFFIC AND ROAD SAFETY, Arts. 114

#### CHAPTER II

On persons responsible for offenses

###### Art. 41

Perpetrators, accomplices and accessories are responsible for offenses.

Concordances:
•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 310

###### Art. 42

Perpetrators are deemed to be those who have committed the offense, whether in a direct and immediate manner, or by advising or instigating another to commit it, when the advice has determined the perpetration of the crime; those who have prevented or attempted to prevent its execution from being avoided; those who have determined the perpetration of the crime and carried it out by using other persons, imputable or not imputable, by means of price, gift, promise, order or any other fraudulent and direct means; those who have contributed to the execution, in a principal manner, by deliberately and intentionally performing some act without which the offense could not have been committed; and those who, by physical violence, abuse of authority, threat or other coercive means, compel another to commit the punishable act, even if the force employed for that purpose cannot be qualified as irresistible.

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK I)

, Arts. 110
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK III)

, Arts. 1010, 1011
•	LAW ON NARCOTIC AND PSYCHOTROPIC SUBSTANCES, Arts. 79, 80, 81

Case law:

•	Judicial Gazette, PERPETRATORS OF CRIME, 01-Dec-1943
•	Judicial Gazette, PERPETRATOR OF CRIME, 15-Feb-1995

###### Art. 43

Accomplices are those who indirectly and secondarily cooperate in the execution of the punishable act, by means of prior or simultaneous acts.

If from the particular circumstances of the case it results that the person accused of complicity only wished to cooperate in an act less serious than that committed by the perpetrator, the penalty shall be applied to the accomplice only in relation to the act he or she intended to execute.

Concordances:
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK I)

, Arts. 110
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK III)

, Arts. 1010, 1011
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

, Arts. 2216
•	ORGANIC LAW ON LAND TRANSPORT TRAFFIC AND ROAD SAFETY, Arts. 136

Case law:

•	Judicial Gazette, ACCOMPLICE TO HOMICIDE, 29-Jun-1979
•	Judicial Gazette, PERPETRATOR OF CRIME AND ACCOMPLICE, 07-Dec-1993
•	Judicial Gazette, DISTINCTION BETWEEN PERPETRATOR OR ACCOMPLICE OF CRIME, 27-Jan-1994

###### Art. 44

Accessories are those who, knowing the criminal conduct of the wrongdoers, habitually provide them with lodging, hiding place, or meeting place;
or provide them with the means to take advantage of the effects of the crime committed;
or favor them, by concealing the instruments or material evidence of the offense, or by rendering useless the signs or traces of the crime, in order to avoid its punishment and those who, being obligated by reason of their profession, employment, art or trade, to carry out the examination of the signs or traces of the crime, or the clarification of the punishable act, conceal or alter the truth, with the purpose of favoring the offender.

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK III)

, Arts. 1011
• ORGANIC LAW ON LAND TRANSPORT, TRAFFIC AND ROAD SAFETY, Arts. 136

Case Law:

• Judicial Gazette, CONCEALMENT IN THE CRIME OF ROBBERY, 28-Feb-1994
• Judicial Gazette, CULPABLE OMISSION AND CONCEALMENT, 06-Sep-1994

###### Art. 45

Concealment for the benefit of the accused's spouse is exempt from punishment; or of their ascendants, descendants and siblings, or of their relatives by affinity up to

the second degree.  

Concordances:

• CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 22, 23

###### Art. 46

The authors of an attempt shall suffer a penalty of one to two thirds of that which would have been imposed on them if the crime had been consummated. For the application of the penalty, the danger run by the passive subject of the offense and the background of the accused shall necessarily be taken into consideration.

###### Art. 47

Accomplices shall be punished with half the penalty that would have been imposed on them had they been authors of the crime.

###### Art. 48

Concealers shall be punished with one quarter of the penalty applicable to the authors of the crime; but in no case shall it exceed two years, nor shall it be imprisonment.

###### Art. 49

In cases of crimes against persons, intimate friends and those who have received great benefits from the person responsible for the crime, prior to its execution, shall be exempt from liability for concealment.

###### Art. 50

In the case of limited awareness due to illness, contemplated in Art. 35, the penalty applicable to the offender shall be from one quarter to half of that indicated for the offense, in accordance with the circumstances that shall be duly assessed by the judge.  

Case Law:

• Judicial Gazette, ACTING WITH DIMINISHED CONSCIOUSNESS AND WILL, 07-May-2002

### TITLE IV

ON PENALTIES

#### CHAPTER I

On penalties in general

###### Art. 51

The penalties applicable to offenses are the following: Penalties peculiar to felonies:
1.- Major imprisonment;  
2.- Minor imprisonment;  
3.- Prison from eight days to five years;  
4.- Interdiction of certain political and civil rights;  

5.- Subjection to the surveillance of the authority;  
6.- Deprivation of the exercise of professions, arts or trades; and,
7.- Perpetual incapacity for the performance of any public employment or office. Penalties peculiar to misdemeanors:
1.- Prison from one to thirty days. 2.- Fine.  

Penalties common to all offenses:

1.- Fines.  
2.- Special Confiscation.  

Note: Article substituted by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.  
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article substituted by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.  
Note: Article amended by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 160 of March 29, 2010.  

Concordances:

• CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66, 76
• CODE OF EXECUTION OF PENALTIES AND SOCIAL REHABILITATION, Arts. 1, 57
• ORGANIC LAW ON LAND TRANSPORT, TRAFFIC AND ROAD SAFETY, Arts. 123

Case Law:
• Judicial Gazette, THE OFFENSE IS QUALIFIED BY THE MAGNITUDE OF THE PENALTY, 31-Jul-1958

###### Art. 52

Every conviction carries with it the joint and several obligation to pay procedural costs by all those responsible for the crime. Damages and losses shall likewise be paid jointly and severally by all those responsible against whom a private prosecution has been brought with the object of obtaining such compensation.  

Concordances:
• CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

, Arts. 2214
• CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 309, 312
• ORGANIC LAW ON LAND TRANSPORT, TRAFFIC AND ROAD SAFETY, Arts. 108, 118, 175

Case Law:

• Judicial Gazette, LIABILITY FOR CRIMES, 02-Mar-1885
• Judicial Gazette, COMPENSATION FOR DAMAGES, 01-Mar-1887

###### Art. 53

Major imprisonment, which shall be served in the Social Rehabilitation Centers of the State, is divided into:

a) Ordinary from four to eight years and, from eight to twelve years;  
b) Extraordinary from twelve to sixteen years; and
c) Special from sixteen to twenty-five years.  
Note: Article substituted by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.  
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article substituted by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.  

Concordances:
• CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK I)

, Arts. 103, 110
• CODE OF EXECUTION OF PENALTIES AND SOCIAL REHABILITATION, Arts. 20

###### Art. 54

Minor imprisonment, which shall be served in the aforementioned establishments, is divided into ordinary from three to six years and from six to nine years, and into extraordinary from nine to twelve years.  

Those sentenced to minor imprisonment shall also be subject to re-education work or to work in common workshops; and they shall only be made to work outside the establishment when agricultural penal colonies are organized, and they shall not be isolated, except for regulatory punishments, which may not exceed eight days.  

Note: Article substituted by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.  
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text.

###### Art. 55

Correctional prison shall be served by the convicted in the jails of the respective canton, in those of the provincial capital or in appropriate sections of the penitentiaries, and they must engage in regulatory work, in common workshops.  

Note: Article substituted by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register

621 of July 4, 1978.  
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text.

###### Art. 56

Every sentence to ordinary or extraordinary major imprisonment, or extraordinary minor imprisonment, carries with it the interdiction of the convict, for the duration of the penalty. The interdiction takes effect from the time the judgment becomes final, and deprives the convicted person of the capacity to dispose of their property, except by testamentary act.  

Those sentenced to ordinary minor imprisonment, in the case of recidivism, or in that of concurrence of several crimes that merit a penalty of imprisonment, shall also be subject to interdiction.  

The appointment of the corresponding guardian shall be made in accordance with the rules of the Civil Code for the guardianship of the spendthrift.  

Concordances:

• CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 64
• CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK I)

, Arts. 367, 371, 383, 415, 416, 463, 468

###### Art. 57

No penalty of imprisonment shall be imposed on a person over sixty-five years of age. One who at such age commits a crime punished with imprisonment shall serve the time of the sentence in an establishment intended for correctional prison.  
If while already in imprisonment he reaches sixty-five years of age, he shall proceed to serve his sentence in a prison house, in accordance with the preceding paragraph.  

The same may be resolved by judges with respect to weak or sick persons.  

Note: Included Errata, published in Official Register 165 of February 17, 1971
, and published again in Official Register 173 of March 3, 1971.  
Note: Article substituted by Law No. 2, published in Official Register 45 of June 23, 2005.  
Note: Article substituted by Art. 5 of Law No. 1, published in Official Register Supplement 393 of July 31, 2008.

###### Art. 57

A.- Article 57 as substituted of the Penal Code shall be interpreted as follows: "Correctional prison" and "prison house" shall be understood as specialized places for the rehabilitation of older adults, which shall be administered by the Ministry of Economic and Social Inclusion in coordination with the Ministry of Justice and Human Rights.

Note: Article given by Art. 6 of Law No. 1, published in Official Registry Supplement 393 of July 31, 2008.

###### Art. 58

No pregnant woman may be deprived of her liberty, nor shall she be notified of a sentence imposing penalties of imprisonment or confinement, except 90 days after childbirth.

Note: Article substituted by Law No. 105, published in Official Registry 365 of July 21, 1998.

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 43
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK I)

, Arts. 61

###### Art. 59

The duration of a day for computing the time of the sentence is twenty-four hours; and that of a month, thirty days.
Any detention, before the judgment becomes final, shall be credited toward the duration of the penalty of deprivation of liberty, if such detention has been occasioned by the offense being punished.

Concordances:
•	CODE OF EXECUTION OF PENALTIES AND SOCIAL REHABILITATION, Arts. 56

###### Art. 60

Any sentence that condemns to confinement or imprisonment causes the suspension of citizenship rights for a time equal to that of the sentence; but in the cases expressly determined by this Code, judges and courts may impose the suspension of such rights for a term of three to five years, even if the imprisonment does not exceed six months.

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 64, 113

###### Art. 61

By virtue of subjection to special surveillance by the authority, the judge may prohibit the convicted person from appearing in the places designated to him, after the sentence has been served; for which purpose, before being released, the convicted person shall indicate the place he chooses for his residence, and shall receive a travel pass, in which the mandatory itinerary and the duration of his stay in each place of transit shall be determined.
In addition, he shall be obliged to appear before the police authority of the place of his residence within twenty-four hours following his arrival, and may not move to another place without written permission from said authority, which has the right to impose on the

person under surveillance an occupation and way of life, if he does not have them.

Concordances:

•	CODE OF EXECUTION OF PENALTIES AND SOCIAL REHABILITATION, Arts. 57

###### Art. 62

Those sentenced to a penalty of confinement may be placed, by the conviction sentence, under the surveillance of the authority, for five to ten years; and if they reoffend in the same crime or commit another that merits the penalty of confinement, such surveillance shall last for life.

###### Art. 63

Fines for crimes belong to the Treasury; and shall be imposed on each of those convicted for the same offense.
The fine shall be collected by real enforcement.

###### Art. 64

In the sentence the judge may authorize the convicted person to pay the fine in installments; the amount and date of payments being fixed according to the economic condition of the convicted person.

###### Art. 65

Special confiscation applies to: things that were the object of the offense; those that have served, or have been intended to commit it, when they are the property of the author of the punishable act, or of the accomplice; and those that have been produced by the offense itself.

Special confiscation shall be imposed for a crime, without prejudice to the other penalties established by Law; but, in the case of a contravention, it shall be imposed only in cases expressly determined by Law.

Concordances:

•	LAW ON NARCOTIC AND PSYCHOTROPIC SUBSTANCES, Arts. 83
•	INTELLECTUAL PROPERTY LAW, Arts. 330

Case Law:
•	Judicial Gazette, PENALTIES OF CONFISCATION AND FINES, 16-sep-1913

###### Art. 66

Work is mandatory in establishments intended for confinement and correctional imprisonment, and its proceeds shall be invested in the manner indicated in the respective Law.

The proceeds of the convict's work are not subject to attachment or seizure, except for the payment of mandatory alimony.

Note: Included Errata, published in Official Registry 165 of February 17, 1971
, and published again in Official Registry 173 of March 3, 1971.

Concordances:
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK I)

, Arts. 349
•	CODE OF EXECUTION OF PENALTIES AND SOCIAL REHABILITATION, Arts. 39, 58

###### Art. 67

The sentence to the penalties established by this Code is independent of the compensation for damages according to the rules of the Civil Code and Code of Civil Procedure. Once the amount of compensation is determined, it shall be collected by real enforcement.

The injured party or whoever exercises his legal representation may claim before the criminal jurisdiction the compensation for damages caused by the crime, through the corresponding private accusation brought for such purpose. The liquidation of compensations declared in final judgment shall be carried out in summary verbal proceedings, as prescribed by the Code of Criminal Procedure.

Collection shall be carried out by real enforcement against the debtor or the civilly liable party.

In case of proven insolvency, there shall be no enforcement for procedural costs.

Concordances:

•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 31, 159, 176, 186, 245, 309, 312, 391, 402
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK II)

, Arts. 879, 972, 974
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

, Arts. 1453, 1572, 2218, 2229, 2231, 2232, 2233, 2234, 2351

Case Law:

•	Judicial Gazette, DAMAGES, 11-jan-1886
•	Judicial Gazette, COMPENSATION FOR DAMAGES, 31-jul-1929

###### Art. 68

When the property of the convicted person is not sufficient to pay the damages, the fine and the restitutions, the first two sentences shall be preferred; and in concurrence of fine and costs owed to the Treasury, the payments made by the convicted persons shall be credited first to the costs.

###### Art. 69

No penalty may be executed while an appeal or clarification of the sentence is pending.

###### Art. 70

Civil obligations arising from offenses are not extinguished by the death of the offender.

Concordances:
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

, Arts. 2216

###### Art. 71

The guilty party is obliged to publish, at his expense, the conviction sentence, when the publication constitutes the means of repairing the non-pecuniary damage caused by the crime.

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

, Arts. 2231

#### CHAPTER II

On the application and modification of penalties


This excerpt sits under: PENAL CODE > BOOK I)

###### Art. 72

When there are two or more mitigating circumstances and no aggravating circumstance, not constitutive or modifying of the offense, the penalties of imprisonment shall be reduced or modified in the following manner:

Special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years shall be replaced with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years.  

Extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years shall be replaced with ordinary major imprisonment of eight to twelve years.  
Ordinary major imprisonment of eight to twelve years shall be replaced with ordinary major imprisonment of four to eight years.  

Ordinary major imprisonment of four to eight years shall be replaced with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.  

Extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years shall be replaced with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years.  

Ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years shall be replaced with correctional prison of two to five years.  

Ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years shall be replaced with correctional prison of one to three years.  

Note: Final paragraph added by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.  
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article replaced by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.  

Case Law:
•	Judicial Gazette, MITIGATING CIRCUMSTANCES, 18-Nov-1963
•	Judicial Gazette, AGGRAVATING CIRCUMSTANCE OF THE OFFENSE, 10-Nov-1993
•	Judicial Gazette, PENALTY OF ORDINARY MAJOR IMPRISONMENT, 28-Jan-1994
•	Judicial Gazette, MITIGATING CIRCUMSTANCES IN THE PENALTY, 15-Feb-1995
•	Judicial Gazette, MODIFICATION OF THE PENALTY, 29-Sep-2000

###### Art. 73

If there are two or more mitigating circumstances and no aggravating circumstance not constitutive or modifying of an offense, the correctional penalties of prison and fine shall be reduced, respectively, to eight days and six dollars of the United States of North America, and judges may apply only one of these penalties, separately, or replace that of prison with a fine, up to twelve dollars of the United States of North America, if only the former is prescribed by Law.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 1 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.  

Case Law:
•	Judicial Gazette, EXISTENCE OF A SINGLE MITIGATING CIRCUMSTANCE, 26-Jul-1993

###### Art. 74

When there is in favor of the accused a single mitigating circumstance of a transcendental nature and it concerns a subject whose background does not reveal dangerousness, there being no aggravating circumstances not constitutive or modifying of an offense, judges may consider it for the modification of the penalty, in accordance with the rules of the preceding articles.

###### Art. 75

When any of the excusing circumstances determined in Arts. 25, 26 and 27 exists, the penalties shall be reduced in the following manner:
If it concerns a crime that merits extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years, the penalty shall be replaced by correctional prison of one to five years and a fine not exceeding thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America;  

If it concerns an offense punished with ordinary major imprisonment of eight to twelve years, the penalty of correctional prison of one to four years and a fine not exceeding nineteen dollars of the United States of North America shall be applied;  

If the offense is punished with ordinary major imprisonment of four to eight years, this penalty shall be replaced with correctional prison of one to three years and a fine not exceeding sixteen dollars of the United States of North America;  

If the penalty prescribed for the offense is extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years, it shall be replaced with correctional prison of six months to two years and a fine not exceeding twelve dollars of the United States of North America;  

If the offense is punished with minor imprisonment of six to nine years, the penalty of correctional prison of three months to one year and a fine not exceeding nine dollars of the United States of North America shall be applied;  

If the penalty to be applied is minor imprisonment of three to six years, it shall be replaced with correctional prison of one to six months and a fine of six dollars of the United States of North America; and,

If it concerns a crime punished with correctional prison, the penalty shall be reduced to prison of eight days to three months and a fine of five dollars of the United States of North America, or only one of these penalties.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 2 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 76

The reduction of the penalty of imprisonment, by virtue of mitigating circumstances, does not prevent the convicted person from being placed under special surveillance of the authority for at least three years, and at most six.

###### Art. 77

There is recidivism when the guilty party commits a crime again after having committed a previous one for which he received a conviction sentence.  

Case Law:

•	Judicial Gazette, CRIMINAL RECIDIVISM, 22-May-1925
•	Judicial Gazette, FIGURE OF SPECIFIC RECIDIVISM, 28-Jul-1994

###### Art. 78

In contraventions there is recidivism when the same contravention or a greater one is committed, in the ninety days following the conviction for the first offense.  

Recidivism, in the case of the contravention established in numeral 1 of article 607, shall be considered as a crime, in accordance with Chapter I of Title X of Book II of this Code.  

Note: Second paragraph added by Law No. 0, published in Supplemental Official Register

160 of March 29, 2010.  

Case Law:

•	Judicial Gazette, CRIMINAL RECIDIVISM, 23-Jul-1952

###### Art. 79

Conviction sentences issued abroad shall be taken into account for recidivism.  

Likewise, conviction sentences pronounced by military tribunals shall be taken into account but only when dealing with crimes of the same nature; and, in this case, only the minimum penalty that could have been imposed in the first conviction and not actually applied shall be considered.

###### Art. 80

In case of recidivism, the penalty shall be increased according to the following rules:
1a.- Anyone who, having been previously sentenced to imprisonment, commits a crime punishable by major imprisonment of four to eight years, shall suffer the same penalty, but from eight to twelve;  
2a.- If the new crime is punishable by major imprisonment of eight to twelve years, the offender shall be sentenced to extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years; 2b.- Anyone who, having been previously sentenced to imprisonment, commits a crime punishable by extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years, the penalty shall be special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years; and, if the new crime is punishable by special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years, the penalty shall be twenty-five years, not subject to modification.  

3a.- If an individual, after having been sentenced to imprisonment, commits a crime punishable by minor imprisonment of three to six years, shall suffer the same penalty, but from six to nine;  
4a.- If the new crime committed is one of those punished by law with minor imprisonment of six to nine years, the transgressor shall be sentenced to extraordinary minor imprisonment;  
5a.- If anyone who was sentenced to extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years commits another offense punishable by the same penalty, shall be sentenced to major imprisonment of twelve years;  
6a.- If anyone who has been sentenced to imprisonment commits a crime punishable by correctional prison, shall be punished with the maximum penalty for the newly committed crime; and, in addition, shall be subjected to the surveillance of the authority for a time equal to that of the sentence;  
7a.- If anyone who has been sentenced to correctional penalty reoffends in the same crime, or commits another that also merits correctional penalty, shall be punished with the maximum penalty established for the most recently committed crime; and,
8a.- If an individual sentenced to correctional penalty commits a crime punishable by imprisonment, the penalty established for the last offense shall be applied, without being able to

recognize mitigating circumstances.  

Nota: Agregado numeral por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .  
Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo reformado por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .

###### Art. 81

In case of concurrence of several offenses, the following rules shall be observed:

1.- If several crimes punishable by correctional penalties concur, or one or more of these crimes with one or more contraventions, all fines and correctional and police prison penalties shall be accumulated; but in such a way that the fine cannot exceed double the most severe; and correctional prison, six years;  
2.- When a crime punishable by imprisonment concurs with crimes punishable by correctional prison or one or more contraventions, the penalty established for the most serious crime shall be imposed;  
3.- When several crimes punishable by imprisonment concur, the greater penalty shall be imposed.  

When several crimes punishable by special major imprisonment concur, the penalties shall be accumulated for a maximum of thirty-five years;  

4.- The penalties of special confiscation by virtue of several concurrent offenses shall always be accumulated;  
5.- When there is concurrence of several contraventions, all penalties deserved by the contravener shall be accumulated, but they may not exceed the maximum police penalty; and,
6.- When a single act constitutes several offenses, only the most severe penalty shall be imposed, which shall be up to thirty-five years, if they are offenses punishable by special major imprisonment.  
Nota: Artículo sustituido por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .  
Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo sustituido por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .

###### Art. 82

In cases of conviction for the first time, if it is caused by a crime punishable by a penalty whose maximum does not exceed six months of correctional prison or by a crime to which only a fine applies, judges may order in the same sentence that the execution of the penalty be suspended. This decision shall be based on the criterion regarding the integral personality of the convicted person, the nature of the crime and the circumstances that have surrounded it, insofar as they may serve to assess said personality. Judges shall request the information they deem pertinent to form a criterion.  

Concordancias:

•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 170, 174, 312

###### Art. 83

In the case of concurrence of offenses, conditional sentencing shall proceed if the maximum penalty applicable to the offender does not exceed six months of prison or is only a fine.

###### Art. 84

The sentence shall be considered as not pronounced if within the time established for the prescription of the penalty and two more years, the convicted person does not commit a new offense.

###### Art. 85

If the convicted person, during the time indicated in the previous article, commits a new offense, shall suffer the penalty imposed in the first conviction and that which corresponds to the new act committed.

###### Art. 86

Conditional sentencing does not suspend the reparation of damages and losses caused by the crime, the payment of procedural costs, nor special confiscation.

###### Art. 87

Any convicted person who has served three-quarters of the sentence, in the case of reclusion, and two-thirds, in the case of correctional imprisonment, may be placed on conditional release, by resolution of the corresponding authority, provided that he has regularly complied with prison regulations and observed very good conduct, revealing repentance and reform, under the following conditions:

1a.- To reside in the place determined in the respective order, not being able to leave that residence except with permission from the authority that granted him freedom;  
2a.- That, when he obtains said permission, upon moving to another place, he makes the permission known to the first police authority of his new residence;  
3a.- That he proves having a profession, art, trade or industry, or fortune, or other means that allows him to live honestly;  
4a.- That the time remaining to complete the sentence does not exceed three years;  
5a.- That, having been sentenced to the payment of civil indemnities, he proves having fulfilled this obligation, unless he has proven impossibility to do so; and, 6a.- That the Institute of Criminology in the Capital of the Republic or the institution or authority designated, in the Provincial Capitals, grants a favorable report for conditional release.  

Nota: Numeral sexto sustituido por Decreto Supremo 1458, publicado en Registro Oficial 323 de 4 de octubre de 1971 .  
Nota: Inciso final agregado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .  
Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior.

•	REGLAMENTO AL CODIGO DE EJECUCION DE PENAS Y REHABILITACION SOCIAL, Arts. 40, 41

###### Art. 88

If the person who obtained conditional release, during the time remaining to complete the sentence and up to two more years, observes bad conduct, or does not live from honest work if he lacks property, or frequents gambling houses or taverns, or ordinarily associates with vicious people or people of bad reputation, the respective authority shall revoke the conditional release, so that he completes the part of the sentence that remained upon obtaining freedom, whatever the time elapsed since then.  

If he commits a new crime, in addition to the time remaining from the first sentence, he shall suffer the penalty for the newly committed crime.

###### Art. 89

Once the time of the sentence and two more years have elapsed, without the conditional release having been revoked, the penalty shall be extinguished.

###### Art. 90

No convict whose conditional release has been revoked may obtain it again.

###### Art. 91

When notifying the accused of the conviction sentence, the provisions contained in the preceding articles shall be read to him in all cases.

###### Art. 92

The accused who obtains conditional release shall remain subject to special surveillance by the authority for the time remaining to complete the sentence and two more years.

###### Art. 93

The discovery of a prior crime, duly proven, suspends the effects of the conditional sentence.

#### CAPITULO III

Of the exercise of actions and of the extinction and prescription of the same and of penalties

###### Art. 94

The pardon of the offended party or the transaction with the latter, does not extinguish the public action for an infraction that must be prosecuted ex officio.  

Concordancias:
•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 32, 34, 36

###### Art. 95

The crime committed to the detriment of several persons shall be punished even if the accusation or complaint is brought by only one of them.  

Concordancias:

•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 58

###### Art. 96

The death of the accused, occurring before conviction, extinguishes the criminal action.

###### Art. 97.- Every penalty is personal and is extinguished with the death of the convict

###### Art. 98

The criminal action is extinguished by amnesty, or by remission of the offended party in crimes of private action, or by prescription.  

The waiver by the offended party of the exercise of the criminal action only prejudices the person waiving and his heirs.  

Having been brought accusation or complaint, as the case may be, by several persons offended by the same crime, the remission of one of them shall not prejudice the others.

###### Art. 99

Amnesty shall not only terminate the criminal action but also the conviction and all its effects, with the exception of civil indemnities.  

Concordancias:

•	CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 66, 120

###### Art. 100

The penalty is also extinguished by declaration of the Senate Chamber rehabilitating the honor and establishing the innocence of those unjustly convicted, in accordance with the provisions of the Political Constitution of the State and the Code of Criminal Procedure.  

Concordancias:

•	CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 11, 120
•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 416, 417, 420

###### Art. 101

Every criminal action prescribes within the time and under the conditions established by Law.

In the exercise of the right established by prescription, the following rules shall be observed:

In both crimes of public action and crimes of private action, a distinction shall be made above all as to whether, once the crime has been committed, prosecution has been initiated or not.

With the exception of cases of imprescriptibility of actions and penalties provided for in the last paragraph of number 2 of article 23 and in the second paragraph of article 121 of the Political Constitution of the Republic, in other crimes punished with imprisonment, the exercise of which action is public, if there is no prosecution, the action to prosecute them shall prescribe in ten years; in the case of crimes punished with special major imprisonment, the action to prosecute them shall prescribe in fifteen years. In the case of crimes punished with prison, the action to prosecute them shall prescribe in five years. Time shall be counted from the date on which the offense was perpetrated.

In the same crimes of public action, if prosecution has been initiated before those periods are completed, the action to continue the case shall prescribe in the same periods, counted from the date of the opening order of the proceeding.
If the accused appears voluntarily before justice within a maximum period of six months after the commencement of the investigation, the respective periods shall be reduced to ten

years in crimes punished with special major imprisonment; to eight years in other crimes punished with imprisonment; and to four years in crimes punished with prison. In these cases, the periods shall be counted from the date of commencement of the investigation. This rule shall have no effect in case of recidivism.

In crimes of private action, the action to prosecute them shall prescribe within a period of one hundred eighty days, counted from when the offense was committed.

Once the action has been initiated and the defendant has been summoned, before the expiration of that period, prescription shall occur after two years have elapsed from the date of service of the complaint.

The criminal action for crimes punished only with a fine shall be extinguished at any stage of the trial, by voluntary payment of the maximum fine corresponding to the crime, and of compensation, in cases where applicable.

If prescription has occurred due to lack of timely action by the judges, they shall be punished by the superior with a fine of forty-four to four hundred thirty-seven dollars of the United States of North America, without prejudice to the action for damages that may lie against said officials, in accordance with the provisions of the Code of Civil Procedure.

Officials of the public ministry and secretaries of courts and tribunals through whose negligence prescription has occurred shall incur the same penalty.

If there is a private prosecutor, or if it is a complaint, the fine shall be divided in equal parts between the administration of justice and the prosecutor. The part of the fine corresponding to the administration of justice shall be invested by the Supreme Court in its case, or by the respective Superior Court that imposed the fine, in general expenses of the administration of justice.
Note: Fourth and sixth paragraphs substituted by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Fourth and sixth paragraphs substituted by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.
Note: Article amended by Art. 3 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 102

If, after the judgment handed down by the superior becomes final, the case is not sent to the judge responsible for executing it within a time equal to or greater than that necessary for prescription of the penalty, the employees or officials responsible for the delay shall incur the sanction established in the preceding article.

###### Art. 103

The incapacities attached to certain convictions, by Law or judicial sentence, do not cease by pardon granted in accordance with the Constitution and the laws, unless expressly stated in the decree of grace.

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 120

###### Art. 104

Every person sentenced to major or minor imprisonment who obtains a pardon or commutation of sentence shall remain under special surveillance by the authority, for up to a term of ten years, if the decree of grace does not provide otherwise.

###### Art. 105

Civil interdiction shall cease when the convicted person has obtained a pardon of the penalty, or when it has been commuted with another that does not carry such interdiction.

Concordances:

## BOOK I)

, Arts. 367, 371

###### Art. 106

The authority designated by the Constitution may pardon, or commute, or reduce penalties applied by final judicial sentence, subject to the special provisions of the Constitution and the Law of Grace.

The pardon, commutation, or reduction of the penalty shall not extend to exonerate the guilty party from payment of damages and costs to the Treasury, or to interested third parties.

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 147
•	LAW OF GRACE, Arts. 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7

###### Art. 107

Penalties depriving liberty, for crime, prescribe in a time equal to that of the sentence, and in no case may the time of prescription be less than six months.

Prescription of the penalty shall begin to run from midnight of the day on which the judgment became final and the time that the offender has been confined, imprisoned or detained by reason of the same crime shall be credited to the time necessary for prescription.

Cases in which constitutional guarantees have been violated by public officials or employees are excepted, in accordance with the provisions of the Political Constitution.

###### Art. 108

Both prescription of action and prescription of penalty are interrupted by the fact that the offender commits another offense that merits equal or greater penalty, before the time for prescription expires.

###### Art. 109

The action and penalty for police offenses prescribe within the periods indicated in Book III of this Code.

###### Art. 110

Every person sentenced to a penalty of imprisonment that has prescribed shall be, de facto and for ten years, subject to special surveillance by the authority, and may not reside in the place where the crime was committed, if the aggrieved party or their relatives live there.


This excerpt sits under: CRIMINAL CODE > BOOK IV) > CHAPTER III

###### Art. 111

The penalties of fine and special confiscation shall prescribe within the time periods established for the prescription of the principal penalties; and civil judgments imposed for an

infraction shall prescribe according to the rules of the Civil Code.  

When only a fine or special confiscation has been imposed, it shall prescribe in one year.

, Arts. 2392, 2393, 2394, 2395, 2396, 2397

###### Art. 112

The prescription shall run or be interrupted, separately, for each of the participants in a crime.

###### Art. 113

By pardon of the offended party the penalty ceases in the case of infractions of adultery and serious slanderous and non-slanderous injury.  
If there are several participants, pardon in favor of one of them benefits the others.  
Note: Adultery as a crime was abolished by the final article of the Code of Criminal Procedure, Law No. 143, published in Official Registry 511 of June 10, 1983.

###### Art. 114

Prescription may be declared at the request of a party, or ex officio, necessarily, when the conditions required in this Code are met.

###### Art. 114

A.- Persons who have remained detained without having received an order of dismissal or opening to plenary trial for a time equal to or greater than one-third of that established by the Penal Code as the maximum penalty for the crime for which they are prosecuted, shall be immediately released by the judge hearing the case.  

Likewise, persons who have remained detained without having received a sentence, for a time equal to or greater than half of that established by the Penal Code as the maximum penalty for the crime for which they are prosecuted, shall be immediately released by the criminal court hearing the case.  

Those who are prosecuted for crimes punished by the Law on Narcotic and Psychotropic Substances are excluded from these provisions.  
Note: Article added by Law No. 4, published in Supplementary Official Registry 22 of September 9, 1992.  
Note: To declare unconstitutionality on substantive grounds and suspend the effects of the last sentence of this Article, which states: "Those who are prosecuted for crimes punished by the Law on Narcotic and Psychotropic Substances are excluded from these provisions." Given by Constitutional Court Resolution No. 119, published in Supplementary Official Registry 222 of December 24, 1997.  

Note: Article 32 of the Constitutional Control Law, published in Official Registry 99 of July 2, 1997, establishes the Habeas Corpus remedy before the Mayor of the Canton, to enforce the right of the detainee regulated by this Article added to the Code, with appeal remedy to the Constitutional Court against the Mayor's denial.  

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 77

###### Art. 114

B.- In either case, the director of the social rehabilitation center where the detainee is located shall communicate to the judge or court of the case, the day following that on which the time periods established in the preceding article are completed, such circumstance, so that the immediate release of the detainee may be ordered.  
In the event that the release order issued by the judge or court is not received within twenty-four hours following the notice given to them, the director of the rehabilitation center shall immediately release the detainee, which shall be communicated in writing to the criminal judge or court and to the President of the Superior Court of the district.  
Note: Article added by Law No. 4, published in Supplementary Official Registry 22 of September 9, 1992.  

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 77

###### Art. 114

C.- The Supreme Court of Justice in accordance with budgetary provisions, within a period of six months, counting from the effective date of this Law, based on the statistics and resources available to it, shall create, in the judicial districts where necessary, as many criminal judges and courts as required so that each of those offices has to handle at most four hundred cases annually.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 4, published in Supplementary Official Registry 22 of September 9, 1992.

###### Art. 114

D.- Judges and members of criminal courts who do not dispatch the cases under their charge within the time periods established in articles 231, 251, 260, 271 and 324 of the Code of Criminal Procedure, shall be immediately sanctioned by the President of the respective Superior Court of Justice with a fine equal to fifty percent of the value of the minimum wage for workers in general in each of the first three times they incur such delay and with dismissal from office the next time, being disqualified from reinstatement for a period of five years.  

These sanctions shall be imposed on the same day that the President of the Superior Court learns of the delay, under his personal and pecuniary responsibility.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 4, published in Supplementary Official Registry 22 of September 9, 1992.

### Título V

Common Rules for the Application of Penalties in Offenses of Function Committed by Military or Police Servers

Note: Title added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(114.3).- Military and police server.- A military server is considered to be permanent personnel in active service listed in the rosters of the Armed Forces, reservists incorporated into active service; and citizens who perform voluntary military service.

A police server is considered to be anyone who has acquired the police profession and is in active service.

In no case may female or male civilian citizens be tried under these rules.
Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.
Art.	(114.4).- Offenses of military or police function.- Offenses of military or police function are the actions or omissions typified in this Code, committed by a military or police server in active service, which are in direct, concrete, proximate and specific relation to their function and legal position according to the mission established in the Constitution and other applicable laws, which affect persons, property or operations of the Armed Forces or the National Police. Offenses of function may be committed in peacetime or during international or non-international armed conflict.

Military or police servers shall be tried and sanctioned with strict observance of constitutional rights and guarantees.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(114.5).- Joint operations of military and police servers.-
When, for reasons of non-international or international conflict, states of exception or

any other exceptional situation duly established by law, military and police services operate jointly and an offense of function is committed, the penalty corresponding to the function or service ordinarily responsible for responding in the situation shall apply.
