Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.  (114.6).- Subsidiary liability of the State.- For purposes of the
subsidiary and civil liability of the State in offenses of function committed by military or police servers, the general provisions provided in the Constitution and the law shall apply.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(114.7).- Due obedience.- The commanders of the Armed Forces and the National Police
shall be responsible for the orders they issue. Obedience to superior orders shall not exempt from responsibility those who execute them. No male or female subordinate may evade their criminal responsibility with the obedience given to their superior in the perpetration of a crime.

Every superior is imputable for the responsibility for the orders they issue and the consequences of omission in the fulfillment of their duties.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.
Art.	(114.8).- Imprescriptibility.- The actions and penalties provided for the crimes of
genocide, crimes against humanity, war crimes, forced disappearance, extrajudicial execution, torture, or crimes of aggression against a State are imprescriptible.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

BOOK TWO
OF CRIMES IN PARTICULAR

### TITLE I

OF CRIMES AGAINST THE SECURITY OF THE STATE

#### CHAPTER I

Of crimes that compromise the External Security of the Republic

###### Art. 115

Anyone who within the territory of the Republic conspires against its external

security, inducing a foreign power to declare war on Ecuador, shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment, from twelve to sixteen years, subjected to special surveillance by the authority, for ten years, and disqualified for the same time from exercising the rights of citizenship.

If the machinations were not followed by the outbreak of hostilities, the offender shall be punished with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years.

These penalties shall apply to Ecuadorians, even if the machinations to declare war on the Republic took place outside its territory.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978.
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text.

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 5, 6, 83
•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 255

###### Art. 116

Ecuadorians who, under enemy flag, take up arms against the Republic, shall be punished with the same penalties established in the preceding article.


This excerpt sits under: CODIGO PENAL > LIBRO IV)

###### Art. 117.- Shall incur the same penalties

1o.- Ecuadorians who facilitate for the enemies of the Republic entry or passage through the territory of the State;  
2o.- Ecuadorians who have delivered to them cities, fortresses, plazas, ports, forces, warehouses, arsenals, military plans or designs, ships, vessels, or aircraft belonging to Ecuador;  
3o.- Ecuadorians who supply to an enemy power aid in the form of soldiers, men, guides, money, provisions, horses or vehicles, arms, munitions or other objects known to be useful to the enemy;  
4o.- Ecuadorians who have favored the progress of enemy arms in the Republic, against Ecuadorian land, sea or air forces, by corrupting the loyalty of officers, soldiers, sailors, or other citizens; or by giving notice regarding the number, state or strategic movements of Ecuadorian forces; or by directing, as pilots, the enemy army, air forces or navy; or by intentionally giving false direction or false information to the Armed Forces of the Republic;  
5o.- Ecuadorians who have hidden or caused to be hidden spies or enemy soldiers, knowing them as such;  
6o.- Every Ecuadorian who, being officially charged or instructed, by reason of his employment or office, with the measures taken against the enemy, the secret of a negotiation, or of an expedition, has maliciously revealed it to an enemy power or its

agents;  
7o.- Ecuadorians who, with the purpose of favoring the enemy, destroy or set fire to warehouses, depots, arms, munitions, ships, aircraft, fortresses, crops or other objects that could be used by the forces of the Republic;  
8o.- Ecuadorians who prevent the troops of the Republic, in time of international war, from receiving aid in the form of funds, arms, munitions of war and provisions, equipment, vessels or aircraft, or plans, instructions or information convenient for the better progress of the war; and,
9o.- Ecuadorians who deliver to a bordering power or its agents, maps or documents, proof of the Republic's dominion, over disputed border lands.  

In the cases of numbers 2o. and 9o. of this article, if the fraudulent delivery of plans, military designs, maps or documents has been made to a power other than the enemy, the penalty of eight to twelve years of major imprisonment shall be imposed on the guilty party.  

If said plans, designs, maps or documents are delivered to foreign powers or their agents, by culpable act of an Ecuadorian, the penalty shall be one to five years of imprisonment.  

If the revelation contemplated in number 6o of this article, made fraudulently, were carried out to a non-enemy power, it shall be punished with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years; and if it were culpable, with imprisonment of one to five years.

###### Art. 118

Conspiracy to commit any of the infractions detailed in the three preceding articles, shall be punished with eight to twelve years of major imprisonment, in the event that some act has been carried out to prepare the execution of said infractions; and in the contrary case the penalty shall be four to eight years of the same imprisonment.

###### Art. 119

The penalties indicated in the four preceding articles, shall also apply if the mentioned infractions were committed against a nation allied with Ecuador.

###### Art. 120

If the acts mentioned in Arts. 115, 117, 118 and 119, were committed by foreigners, in the territory of the Republic, the penalties established in said articles shall be applied to them.  

The foreigner convicted of espionage shall be punished with the penalty indicated in Art.

###### 115. Art. 121

Every individual who has maintained with subjects of another nation a correspondence that, without having in view any of the infractions determined in Arts. 115 and 117, has had, nevertheless, as a result the supplying to the enemies of Ecuador or its allies acting against the common enemy, of instructions prejudicial to their military situation, shall be punished with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years.

###### Art. 122

In every conviction sentence for treason to the Homeland, the obligation to compensate the nation for the damages and losses caused by the perpetration

of the crime being punished shall be imposed.  

The sentence referred to in the preceding paragraph, carries with it the loss of Ecuadorian nationality.  

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 8

#### CHAPTER II

Of crimes that compromise the peace and dignity of the State

###### Art. 123

Whoever, fraudulently, violates a truce or armistice celebrated with the enemy, after it has been published in due form; or violates, in the same manner, any treaty in force between Ecuador and another nation, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year.  

Jurisprudence:

•	Judicial Gazette, WOUNDS, 08-may-1906

###### Art. 124

Whoever commits hostilities against any foreign power or its subjects, without the knowledge or authorization of the Government of the Republic, if said hostilities occasion a declaration of war or reprisals, shall be punished with eight to twelve years of major imprisonment.  

If the hostilities committed are such that they may produce reprisals or a declaration of war, even if this effect does not follow, the author of said hostilities shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years.

###### Art. 125

Shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years whoever improperly draws up plans of fortifications, ships, aircraft, establishments, roads or other military works, or enters with such purpose, clandestinely or deceitfully, into such places, when their access has been prohibited to the public.

###### Art. 126

Anyone who in the territory of Ecuador attempts against the life, against the immunity or against the personal liberty of the Head of a foreign State, shall be punished: in case of attempt against life, with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years; and in the other cases, with major imprisonment of four to eight years. If the act results in the death of the Head of the foreign State, the guilty party shall be punished with special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years.  

Anyone who in the territory of Ecuador offends the honor or prestige of the Head of a foreign State visiting the country, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two

years.  

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.  
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.

###### Art. 127

The provisions of the preceding article shall apply to the acts provided for therein, when they are committed against representatives of foreign States accredited in Ecuador, in the capacity of heads of diplomatic mission.

###### Art. 128

Whoever publicly, and outside the cases provided for in this Code, incites or foments by any means separatism, or whoever offends or vilifies public institutions or the Public Force, whoever commits any mockery or contempt, with words or actions, against the Flag, the Coat of Arms or the Anthem of the Homeland, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of forty-four to eighty-seven dollars of the United States of North America.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 4 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.  

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 2

###### Art. 129

Whoever illegally impedes the free transit of vehicles, persons or merchandise on the public roads of the country, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years and a fine of forty-four to eighty-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 5 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

#### CAPITULO III

Crimes against the Internal Security of the State

###### Art. 130

Whoever in any form or by any means rises up against the Government, with the purpose of disregarding the Constitution of the Republic, of deposing the constituted Government, preventing the meeting of Congress or dissolving it, or provoking civil war, shall be punished with major imprisonment of four to eight years.

The act exists from the moment there is punishable attempt.

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 4, 424

###### Art. 131

Conspiracy aimed at achieving any of the purposes mentioned in the preceding article, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years.

The guilty party shall, in addition, be subjected to the surveillance of the authority for a time equal to that of the sentence.

###### Art. 132

Whoever in word or in writing attacks in a subversive manner the Constitution or the laws of the Republic, or incites their non-observance, shall be punished with six months to three years of imprisonment.

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 426

###### Art. 133

The authors of pastoral lessons, preachings or sermons, whatever the form in which they are given to the people, if they are aimed at discrediting the authority, presenting it as contrary to the dogmas, or to the discipline, or to the religious interests of any church or cult, accepted or tolerated in the Republic, shall be punished with six months to two years of imprisonment.

###### Art. 134

If the author of the pastoral lessons, preachings or sermons referred to in the preceding article, intends with them to inculcate disobedience to the Constitution, or to the laws, or to the orders of the authority, the penalty shall be one to five years of imprisonment.
If the purpose intended by the author is to incite the people to revolt, or to put in arms one part of the citizens against the other, the penalty shall be imprisonment of three to five years.

In this case, if the revolt or civil war takes place, the guilty party of having provoked them shall suffer the penalty of minor imprisonment of three to six years.

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 426

###### Art. 135

Those who promote discord among citizens, arming or inciting to arm themselves against each other, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years, even if they do not intend, in any way, to alter the constitutional order.

Conspiracy to perpetrate these offenses, if it has been followed by some preparatory act, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to two years.

###### Art. 136

If the attack has as its purpose to bring devastation, carnage or pillage to one or many places, it shall be punished with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years.

Conspiracy to execute such attacks, if it has been followed by some preparatory act, shall be punished with four to eight years of major imprisonment.

###### Art. 137

Those who, armed and militarily organized, alter by force the constitutional order, disregarding the Government, the National Congress, or the Constitution of the Republic itself, shall be punished with minor imprisonment of six to nine years.

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 426

###### Art. 138.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years

1o.- Those who have taken command of an army corps, of a troop, of a warship, of an aircraft, of a square, of a guard post, of a port, of a city, without right or legitimate reason;
2o.- Those who have retained any military command, against the order of the Government; and,
3o.- Commanders who have kept their army or troop assembled after having knowledge that the order to discharge that force has been issued.

###### Art. 139

Any individual who, whether to seize public funds; or to invade properties, squares, cities, fortresses, guard posts, warehouses, arsenals, ports, ships, vessels or aircraft belonging to the State; or to attack or resist the public force that acts against the authors of these crimes, has placed himself at the head of armed factions or has exercised in them any function or command whatsoever, shall be punished with the maximum penalty established in Art. 137.

If these factions have had as their purpose to plunder and distribute among themselves public or national properties, or those of a generality of citizens, or to attack or resist the public force that pursues the authors of these crimes, those who have placed themselves at the head of those factions, or have exercised in them any employment or command whatsoever, shall also be punished with the preceding penalty.

###### Art. 140

The penalties established in the two paragraphs of the preceding article shall be applicable to those who have directed the association, raised or caused to be raised, organized or caused to be organized the factions.

###### Art. 141

In case one of the crimes mentioned in Art. 130 has been

committed by a faction, the penalties established by that article shall be applied to all individuals who form part of the faction and who have been apprehended at the place of the seditious gathering.

###### Art. 142

Outside the case in which the seditious gathering has had as its purpose or result one of the crimes enumerated in Art. 130, the individuals who form part of such factions, without exercising in them any command or employment, and who have been apprehended at the same place, shall be punished with the penalty immediately inferior to that which should be imposed on the directors or commanders of said factions.

###### Art. 143

Those who, having formed part of a faction, without exercising in it employment or command, have separated themselves spontaneously, or at the first admonition of the authority, shall not be punished.

###### Art. 144

In every conviction sentence for the offenses determined in this chapter, the obligation to compensate for the damages and losses caused to the Treasury, with the perpetration of the punished acts, shall also be imposed.

###### Art. 145

Conspirators who reveal to the authority the existence of the conspiracy are exempt from penalty, provided that no punishable preparatory act has been executed.

###### Art. 146

Whoever incites rebellion or indiscipline of the Public Force, shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years and a fine of seventeen to forty-four dollars of the United States of North America.
If as a consequence of the incitement a conflict results in which injuries to persons are produced, the penalty shall be minor imprisonment of three to six years and a fine of forty-four to eighty-seven dollars of the United States of North America; and if the death of one or more persons is produced, the penalty shall be six to nine years of ordinary minor imprisonment and a fine of eighty-seven to one hundred seventy-five dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 6 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 147

Whoever promotes, directs or participates in guerrilla organizations, commandos, combat groups or terrorist groups or cells, intended to subvert public order, replace the Public Force, attack it or interfere with its normal performance, shall be punished with ordinary major imprisonment of four to eight years and a fine of eighty-seven to one hundred seventy-five dollars of the United States of North America.

If these activities are carried out with weapons, or obeying foreign instructions, or with the intervention, support or economic aid from abroad, the penalty shall be eight to twelve years of ordinary major imprisonment and a fine of one hundred seventy-five to four hundred thirty-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Two paragraphs added by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Art. 7 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 148

Whoever disseminates by any means or sends abroad propaganda, news or false information intended to alter public order or that affects national honor, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of forty-four to eighty-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 8 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 149

Whoever establishes or maintains deposits of weapons or ammunition for military or police use, and of any other similar type, without legal authorization from competent authority, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years and a fine of one thousand to five thousand dollars of the United States of America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 9 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.
Note: Article replaced by Law No. 31, published in Official Register 231 of March 17, 2006.

###### Art. 150

A deposit is deemed to be the existence of three or more of said weapons, whatever their model or class, even when they are in disassembled pieces.

###### Art. 151

Whoever has introduced into the country money or securities for subversive purposes or to alter public order, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to two years and a fine of seventeen to eighty-seven dollars of the United States of North America, and the money or securities shall be confiscated for the benefit of National Defense.

Note: Article amended by Art. 10 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 152

Whoever facilitates the entry into the country, the permanence or concealment therein or the evasion of foreign subversive agents, knowing their condition, shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years and a fine of twenty-six to seventy dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 11 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 153

Whoever promotes, directs or organizes public parades or demonstrations

in streets, squares or other open places, provided they are carried out without written permission from competent authority, in which the purpose of the gathering, the site, day and time at which it is to take place are determined, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three months and a fine of nine to twenty-six dollars of the United States of North America.

Directors, promoters and organizers shall also be punished, those who appear as such, by the speeches they deliver, by the printed materials they have published or distributed, by the command words they pronounce, by the insignia they display or by the initial voluntary contribution to the funds of the parade or demonstration or by any other significant act.

The penalty shall be three to six months of imprisonment and a fine of seventeen to thirty-five dollars of the United States of North America, when the parade or demonstration is carried out against the prohibition issued by competent authority.

Note: Article amended by Art. 12 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

•	ORGANIC ELECTORAL LAW, CODE OF DEMOCRACY, Arts. 288

###### Art. 154

Those who attend the demonstration referred to in the preceding article, carrying weapons, shall be punished with deprivation of liberty of three months to one year and a fine of forty-four to eighty-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 13 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 155

Those who, with the purpose of altering public order, invade public or private buildings, facilities or lands, or those who in committing such acts with the same proposed purposes seize property belonging to others, shall be punished with minor imprisonment of three to six years and a fine of forty-four to one hundred seventy-five dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 14 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

#### CAPITULO IV

Crimes of sabotage and terrorism

###### Art. 156

Doctors, nurses, pharmacists, practitioners, employees of health facilities or owners of pharmacies or drugstores who, disobeying orders from competent authority, paralyze services or refrain from providing their collaboration to those who

need them, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of thirty-five to seventy dollars of the United States of North America.

The maximum penalties provided in this article shall be applied to members of professional organizations who have incited the commission of such acts, if these have been consummated.

Note: Article amended by Art. 15 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.
See CRIME OF INTERRUPTING OR PARALYZING PUBLIC SERVICES, Judicial Gazette. Year I. Series XVII. No. 5. Page 1. (Quito, March 28, 2001).

###### Art. 157

Whoever removes, conceals or renders useless on the occasion of a fire, flood, shipwreck or other calamity, any material object or other means intended for relief, rescue or to combat the danger, shall be punished with minor imprisonment of three to six years and a fine of nine to twenty-six dollars of the United States of North America. The same penalty shall be incurred by whoever hinders the service provided for such reason.

If injured persons result from the act, the penalty shall be six to nine years of ordinary minor imprisonment and a fine of eighty-seven to two hundred sixty-two dollars of the United States of North America; and if the death of one or more persons results, the penalty shall be special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years and a fine of two hundred sixty-two to eight hundred seventy-three dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.
Note: Article amended by Art. 16 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 158

Anyone who, outside the cases contemplated in this Code, destroys, damages, renders useless, interrupts or paralyzes public services, industrial or manufacturing installations, commercial centers, ports, canals, reservoirs, mines, powder magazines, vehicles or any other element of transport, public or private installations of electric energy, drinking water, gas or other similar services, or installations of radio, telephone, telegraph, television or any other transmission system; deposits of merchandise, explosives, lubricants, fuels, raw materials destined for production or national consumption, or any other type of similar supply, with the purpose of producing collective alarm, shall be punished with ordinary major imprisonment of eight to twelve years and a fine of eighty-seven to one hundred seventy-five dollars of the United States of North America.

If, as a consequence of the act, injuries to persons are produced, the penalty shall be the maximum indicated in the preceding paragraph; and if the death of one or more persons results, the penalty shall be sixteen to twenty-five years of special major imprisonment, and a fine of one hundred seventy-five to four hundred thirty-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978.
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Registry 422 of September 28, 2001.
Note: Article amended by Art. 17 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 326

###### Art. 159

Anyone who, outside the cases contemplated in this Code, impedes, disorganizes or disturbs the collection, production, transport, storage or distribution of raw materials, manufactured or extracted products, machinery or any other means necessary for production, with the purpose of producing collective alarm, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years and a fine of seventeen to forty-four dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 18 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 160

Anyone who, with the purpose of committing crimes against the common security of persons or property, manufactures, supplies, acquires, removes, throws, uses, or introduces into the country weapons, ammunition or explosive bombs, explosive, asphyxiating or toxic materials, or substances or materials destined for their preparation, shall be punished with ordinary major imprisonment of 4 to 8 years and a fine of four hundred forty-two to eight hundred eighty-four dollars of the United States of North America.

If, by effects of the indicated acts, injuries to persons are produced, the maximum of the penalty indicated in the preceding paragraph shall be imposed; and, if one or more persons result dead, the sanction shall be special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years and a fine of eight hundred eighty-four to one thousand seven hundred sixty-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

If the criminal acts affect exclusively property, in addition to the penalty indicated in the first paragraph, the perpetrator shall be condemned to indemnification for the damages and losses caused.

Note: Article substituted by Supreme Decree No. 1273, published in Official Registry 705 of December 19, 1974.
Note: Supreme Decree No. 1273 amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978.
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text and substitutes, in addition, the first paragraph given by Supreme Decree No. 1273.
Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Registry 422 of September 28, 2001.
Note: Article amended by Art. 19 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.
Note: Article amended by Law No. 31, published in Official Registry 231 of March 17, 2006.

###### Art. 160

A.- Those who, individually or forming associations, such as guerrillas, organizations, gangs, commandos, terrorist groups, armed bands or some other similar form, armed or not, pretexting patriotic, social, economic, political, religious, revolutionary, vindicatory, proselytizing, racial, localist, regional purposes, etc., commit crimes against the common security of persons or human groups of any kind or their property: whether by assaulting, forcing or destroying buildings, banks, warehouses, storage facilities, markets, offices, etc., whether by breaking into or invading domiciles, dwellings, colleges, schools, institutes, hospitals, clinics, convents, installations of the public force, military, police or paramilitary, etc., whether by removing or seizing property or assets of any nature and amount; whether by kidnapping persons, vehicles, ships or airplanes to demand ransom, pressure and demand the change of laws or legally issued orders and provisions or require competent authorities to release persons prosecuted or sentenced for common or political crimes, etc.; whether by occupying by force through threat or intimidation, places or public or private services of any nature and type; whether by raising barricades, parapets, trenches, obstacles, etc. with the purpose of confronting the public force in support of their intentions, plans, theses or proclamations; whether by attacking, in any form, against the community, its property and services, shall be punished with ordinary major imprisonment of four to eight years and a fine of one thousand seven hundred sixty-seven to four thousand four hundred eighteen dollars of the United States of North America.

If by the enumerated criminal acts, injuries to persons are produced, the maximum of the penalty indicated in the preceding paragraph shall be imposed on their perpetrators and, if the death of one or more persons is produced, the penalty shall be special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years and a fine of four thousand four hundred eighteen to eight thousand eight hundred thirty-five dollars of the United States of North America.

If the acts referred to in the first paragraph of this article affect only property, in addition to the sanction imposed therein, the perpetrator or perpetrators shall be condemned to compensation for damages and losses they may have caused.

Note: Article added by Supreme Decree No. 1273, published in Official Registry 705 of December 19, 1974.
Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978.
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Registry 422 of September 28, 2001.
Note: Article amended by Art. 20 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 161

Whoever unjustifiably enters premises whose access to the public or to private individuals has been prohibited, or military or police bases, ships, aircraft, transports, barracks, factories or depots, or, in general, security zones determined by the competent authority, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of forty-four to eighty-seven dollars of the United States of North America

If injuries to persons result from the act, the penalty shall be ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years and a fine of one hundred seventy-five to four hundred thirty-seven dollars of the United States of North America, and if the death of one or more persons occurs, the penalty shall be extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years and a fine of four hundred thirty-seven to eight hundred seventy-three dollars of the United States of North America.  
Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .  
Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 21 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 162

Private individuals who, without the necessary permit and without due explanation, carry weapons for military or police use and of any other similar type, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of nine to forty-four dollars of the United States of America.  

The repeated willful commission of this type of conduct shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment of three to six years.  

It is the responsibility of the Ministry of National Defense, through the Joint Command of the Armed Forces, to issue permits to carry weapons; this power may be delegated in accordance with the regulations on the matter.  

This obligation extends to weapons used in industries and trades.  

Military and Police authorities duly authorized are obligated to seize and remit, after preparing the corresponding report of the action taken, to the Logistics Directorate of the Joint Command of the Armed Forces, any weapon or ammunition of national or foreign origin that does not have legally granted permits.  

All seized weapons shall be registered in the Logistics Directorate of the Joint Command of the Armed Forces and if the owner does not justify their origin within a period of thirty days, they shall be delivered to the weapons depots of the Joint Command of the Armed Forces to be destroyed.  

Seized weapons that serve as evidence of the commission of criminal offenses shall be maintained as such under the custody of the Judicial Police and once the respective criminal trial is concluded, they shall be registered in the Logistics Directorate of the Joint Command of the Armed Forces and subsequently delivered to the competent authority.  

In the Provincial Police Commands throughout the country, a register shall be kept in which the type, caliber and characteristics of the weapon or weapons seized and sent to the corresponding military authority are recorded.  

Manufacturers of weapons, explosives and ammunition of any type must register their factories or workshops in the Logistics Directorate of the Joint Command of the Armed Forces, and report monthly to this entity on the quantity, type, caliber and characteristics of the weapons produced and the code assigned to each of them, which must also be registered.  
Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 22 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .  
Nota: Artículo sustituido por Ley No. 31, publicada en Registro Oficial 231 de 17 de Marzo del 2006 .

###### Art. 163

Whoever imparts or receives military instruction without permission from the competent authority shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to one year and a fine of thirty-five to seventy dollars of the United States of North America.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 23 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 164

Terrorist aggression, provided that the act does not constitute a more serious crime, perpetrated against officials or employees of public institutions or against their properties, shall be punished with three to six years of ordinary minor imprisonment and a fine of eighty-seven to one hundred seventy-five dollars of the United States of North America.  

If injuries to persons are caused, the penalty shall be minor imprisonment of six to nine years and a fine of eighty-seven to four hundred thirty-seven dollars of the United States of North America. If the death of one or more persons results, the penalty shall be sixteen to twenty-five years of special major imprisonment and a fine of two hundred sixty-two to eight hundred seventy-three dollars of the United States of North America.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .  
Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo reformado por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .  
Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 24 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 165

Outside the cases contemplated in this Code, terrorist threat, by whatever means it is made, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year and a fine of nine to forty-four dollars of the United States of North America.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 25 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 166

When the crimes provided for in this Chapter are committed by foreigners naturalized in Ecuador, in addition to the imposed penalty, the naturalization certificate shall be cancelled and they shall be expelled from the country after serving the sanction imposed upon them.  
Art. ...- Those who willfully, individually or collectively, directly or indirectly, by any means, provide, offer or collect funds or assets to finance in whole or in part, with lawful or unlawful resources, the commission of the crimes typified in this Chapter, shall be punished with the same penalties established for the financed crime.  

The crime typified in this article shall be investigated, prosecuted, adjudicated or sentenced by the competent court or authority as a crime autonomous from other crimes typified in this Chapter, committed within or outside the country.  

Nota: Artículo agregado por Ley No. 00, publicada en Registro Oficial Suplemento 352 de 30 de Diciembre del 2010 .

### TITULO II

DE LOS DELITOS CONTRA LAS GARANTIAS CONSTITUCIONALES Y LA IGUALDAD RACIAL

Nota: Encabezado del Título II, reformado por Decreto Supremo No. 3194, publicado en Registro Oficial 769 de 8 de Febrero de 1979 .

#### CAPITULO I

De los delitos relativos al ejercicio del sufragio

###### Art. 167

Those who, by means of riots, violence or threats, have prevented one or more citizens from exercising their political rights, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years and a fine of six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 26 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .  

Concordancias:
•	LEY ORGANICA ELECTORAL, CODIGO DE LA DEMOCRACIA, Arts. 122, 290

###### Art. 168

Members of Electoral Boards and other officials or corporations who, by Law, are charged with verifying the scrutiny of an election, and who subtract or falsify ballots, or annul partially or totally an election, contrary to express laws, shall be punished with imprisonment of three to five years and deprivation of political rights for two years.

###### Art. 169

If the offenses provided for in the two preceding articles have been committed pursuant to an agreement to extend and execute them throughout the Republic, or in several cantons, the penalty shall be ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years and interdiction of political rights for two years.

###### Art. 170

Any individual who is caught subtracting ballots from voters, by means of cunning or violence, or fraudulently substituting another ballot for the one held by the voter, or who presents himself to vote under a false name, or who votes in two or more parishes, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to one year and with one year of interdiction of political rights.

Concordancias:

•	LEY ORGANICA ELECTORAL, CODIGO DE LA DEMOCRACIA, Arts. 288

###### Art. 171

Those who disturb a popular election by alleging religious motives, whether in favor of their candidates, recommending them; or discrediting opposing candidates, shall be punished with imprisonment of thirty to ninety days.

Concordancias:
•	LEY ORGANICA ELECTORAL, CODIGO DE LA DEMOCRACIA, Arts. 49, 50

###### Art. 172

Anyone who has received something in exchange for his vote, or who has given or promised something for the vote of another, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to one year and interdiction of political rights for an equal time.

#### CAPITULO II

Of crimes against freedom of conscience and thought

###### Art. 173

Those who, employing violence or threats, prevent one or more individuals from exercising any cult permitted or tolerated in the Republic, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years.

Concordancias:
•	CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 66

###### Art. 174

Private individuals or ministers of a cult who provoke riots, or tumults against the followers of another cult, whether by word or in writing, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to six months and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 27 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 175

If the offenders exercise ecclesiastical, political, civil or military authority, the penalty shall be six months to two years of imprisonment and a fine of sixteen to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 28 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 176

Those who have prevented, delayed, or interrupted the exercise of a cult, or its public ceremonies, not expressly prohibited by Law, by means of disorder or tumult promoted in the place designated for said cult, but without committing violence or uttering threats against anyone, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year and a fine of twelve to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 29 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 177

Those who offend the corpse of a person, with actions, words, emblems or writings, shall be punished with imprisonment of two months to one year and a fine of six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

The civil or ecclesiastical authority that denies burial to a corpse, in public cemeteries, alleging religious motives, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three months and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Those who place on the tomb of a person injurious emblems or writings, shall be punished with imprisonment of thirty to ninety days and a fine of six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 30 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 178

The authority that, by arbitrary or violent means, restricts the faculty of freely expressing thought, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and interdiction of political rights for a time equal to that of the sentence.

Concordancias:

•	CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 66

###### Art. 179

Whoever prevents or hinders the free circulation of a book, newspaper or printed matter, that are not anonymous, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years.

Concordancias:

•	CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 16

#### CAPITULO III

Of crimes against individual liberty

###### Art. 180

Public employees, custodians and agents of the authority or of the public force who, illegally and arbitrarily, have arrested or caused to be arrested, detained or caused to be detained one or more persons, shall be punished with six months to two years of imprisonment and a fine of twelve to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

They may, in addition, be sentenced to interdiction of the rights of citizenship for two to three years.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 31 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

Concordancias:
•	CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 77

•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 166

Jurisprudencia:

•	Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE PRISION ARBITRARIA, 01-jun-2001
•	Gaceta Judicial, ARRESTO, 30-ene-2009

###### Art. 181

The authority that orders the confinement of a person in contravention of constitutional precepts, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years.

Concordancias:

•	CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 77

###### Art. 182

The official who retains a detainee or prisoner whose liberty he should have decreed or executed shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years; and he who unduly prolongs the detention of a person, without placing him at the disposal of the competent judge.

Concordancias:
•	CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 89
•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 165

###### Art. 183

Those who, without order of the constituted authorities and outside the cases in which the Law and regulations permit or order the arrest or detention of private individuals, have arrested or caused to be arrested, detained or caused to be detained any person, provided that this arrest or detention does not constitute a crime more severely punished, shall be punished with imprisonment of two months to two years and a fine of six to twelve dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 32 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

Concordancias:
•	CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 77

Jurisprudencia:

•	Gaceta Judicial, ARRESTO ARBITRARIO, 10-abr-1889

•	Gaceta Judicial, ARRESTO ILEGAL Y ARBITRARIO, 20-dic-1920

###### Art. 184

The imprisonment shall be six months to three years and the fine six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America, if the illegal and arbitrary detention has lasted more than ten days.
Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 33 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 185

If the illegal and arbitrary detention has lasted more than one month, the guilty party shall be punished with imprisonment of one to four years and a fine of sixteen to forty-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 34 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 186

The penalty of minor imprisonment from three to six years shall be applied if the arrest had been executed with a false order from the public authority, or with the uniform or under the name of one of its agents, or if the arrested or detained person had been threatened with death.  

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 77

###### Art. 187

When the arrested or detained person has suffered bodily torments, the guilty party shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment.  

The penalty shall be minor imprisonment from six to nine years, if the torments have resulted in any of the permanent injuries detailed in the chapter on injuries.  

If the torments have caused death, the guilty party shall be punished with special major imprisonment from sixteen to twenty-five years.  

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.  
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.  

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66

###### Art. 188

The crime of kidnapping is committed by seizing another person by means of violence, threats, seduction or deceit, whether to sell them or place them against their will in the service of another, or to obtain any benefit, or to force them to pay ransom or deliver a movable thing, or extend, deliver or sign a document that has or may have legal effects, or to force them to do or omit to do something, or to force a third party to execute one of the indicated acts aimed at the liberation of the kidnapped person.  

Note: Article substituted by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.  

Jurisprudence:
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF KIDNAPPING, 31-May-1977
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF KIDNAPPING OR ATTEMPT, 04-Apr-1983
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF KIDNAPPING, 19-Feb-1998
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF KIDNAPPING, 27-Apr-1998

###### Art. 189

Kidnapping shall be punished with the penalties indicated in the following numbers:

1.- With imprisonment from six months to two years, if the victim is returned to liberty spontaneously by the kidnapper, before judicial proceedings are initiated, without having suffered mistreatment, nor any of the conditioning acts determined in the previous article being carried out;  
2.- With imprisonment from one to three years, if the return of liberty, with the conditions of the preceding number, has been carried out after prosecution has been initiated with the kidnapper not being detained or imprisoned;  
3.- With ordinary minor imprisonment from three to six years, if the liberation is carried out under the terms of number 2 of this article, with the kidnapper being detained or imprisoned;  

3-A.- With ordinary minor imprisonment from three to six years to whoever, by means of threats, violence, seduction, deceit or other illegitimate means, seizes a motor vehicle; retaining against their will its driver and/or its occupants, to ensure the commission of the crime;  
3-B.- With ordinary minor imprisonment from six to nine years, when the offender, in the case and circumstances established in the previous numeral, starts the vehicle or forces the driver or another of the retained persons to do so, in order to, in the company of these persons, although without their participation, use the motor vehicle to commit or attempt to commit other crimes.  
4.- With ordinary minor imprisonment from six to nine years, if in the case of number 1, the victim has suffered mistreatment;  
5.- With extraordinary minor imprisonment from nine to twelve years, in the case of number 2, if the victim has suffered mistreatment;  

6.- With ordinary major imprisonment from eight to twelve years, in the case of number 3, if there has been such mistreatment; and,
7.- With special major imprisonment from sixteen to twenty-five years, when the victim has not regained liberty until the date of the sentence, the maximum penalty being imposed if before the conviction the victim appears raped, dead or dies as a consequence of the kidnapping.  

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.  
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article substituted by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.  
Note: Numerals 3-A and 3-B, added by Law No. 21, published in Official Register 154 of November 28, 2005.  


This excerpt sits under: CODIGO PENAL > LIBRO IV) > TITULO II > CAPITULO III

###### Art. 190

Even if there is a final conviction, in the case of number 7 of the preceding article, the penalty shall be reduced by half if the kidnapper restores the freedom of his victim.

Art. ... .- The following shall be considered aggravating circumstances of the crime of kidnapping, in addition to the circumstances indicated in article 30, when one or more of the following circumstances concur:

Prolonging the deprivation of liberty of the kidnapped person for a period greater than fifteen days.

Committing the offense using a false detention order, or simulating having one or abusing authority in the case of members of the public force or judges.
Note: Article added by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.

CHAPTER ....
OF THE CRIME RELATING TO TRAFFICKING IN PERSONS

Art. ..- It constitutes the crime of trafficking in persons, even with the consent of the victim, to promote, induce, participate in, facilitate or favor the recruitment, transfer, harboring, reception or delivery of persons by resorting to threat, violence, deception or any other fraudulent form, for purposes of illegal exploitation, with or without profit motive.

For purposes of this offense, exploitation is considered to be any form of forced labor or services, labor slavery, sale and/or use of persons for begging, armed conflicts or recruitment for criminal purposes.

Note: Chapter and Article added by Law No. 2, published in Official Register 45 of June 23, 2005.

Art. ..- Trafficking in persons shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years, provided it does not constitute sexual exploitation. If the victim is a person under eighteen years of age, the penalty shall be extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 2, published in Official Register 45 of June 23, 2005.

Art. ..- The penalty shall be extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years when in the commission of the crime established in the preceding article one or more of the following circumstances concur:

1.	That the victim is under fourteen years of age;
2.	That, as a consequence of the crime, the victim suffers serious or permanent bodily injury, or irreversible psychological damage;
3.	If the offender is the spouse, cohabitant or relative up to the fourth degree of consanguinity or second degree of affinity, ascendant or descendant of the victim; and,
4.	When the offender takes advantage of the vulnerability of the victim or the victim is incapacitated to resist the aggression.

Note: Article added by Law No. 2, published in Official Register 45 of June 23, 2005.

Art. ..- Whoever sells, buys or carries out any transaction, by virtue of which a person is delivered, by payment or any other means, for purposes of exploitation, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years. An offer for sale constitutes an attempt.
If the victim is a person under eighteen years of age, the penalty shall be extraordinary major imprisonment of nine to twelve years; and, if under twelve years of age, the penalty shall be twelve to sixteen years of extraordinary major imprisonment.

Note: Article added by Law No. 2, published in Official Register 45 of June 23, 2005.

CHAPTER ...
OF CRIMES RELATING TO ILLEGAL EXTRACTION AND TRAFFICKING OF ORGANS

Art.	(1).- Whoever intentionally fails to comply with the obligations or violates the prohibitions
provided in articles 11, 19, 58, 69, 74, 76, 77, 78 and 79 of the Organic Law on Donation and Transplantation of Organs, Tissues and Cells; whoever falsifies or adulterates a donation and transplantation protocol, a document defined in the same Law, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three (3) to six (6) years.

In case of non-compliance or non-observance of the obligations or prohibitions mentioned

above, when referring to institutions or legal persons, the criminal responsibility of the highest authority of the institution or legal representative of the legal person shall be presumed.

Whoever intentionally trades or traffics in organs, tissues, bodily substances or any anatomical material from human cadavers shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine (9) to twelve (12) years. The same penalty shall be imposed on whoever intentionally selects, extracts, evaluates, prepares, obtains, stores, transports, assigns, distributes or transplants them.

The penalty shall be extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve (12) to sixteen (16) years, if the activities referred to in the preceding paragraph are carried out with organs, tissues, bodily substances or any anatomical material that come from living persons.

A penalty of extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve (12) to sixteen years shall be imposed on persons who extract or transplant any human anatomical component in medical centers not accredited by the National Health Authority.

If the anatomical components extracted or transplanted intentionally come from children or adolescents or from persons with disabilities, the penalty shall be special major imprisonment of sixteen (16) to twenty-five (25) years.

Note: Chapter and Article added by Law No. 2, published in Official Register 45 of June 23, 2005.
Note: Article replaced by Law No. 0, published in Official Register 398 of March 4, 2011.
Art.	(2).- Whoever transfers organs, donors, recipients or health professionals
specialized in transplants, outside their original jurisdictional borders, with the purpose of performing one or more transplants in the Republic of Ecuador, without having immigrant status in the country or the corresponding permits from the National Health Authority, shall be punished with the penalty of extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve
(12) to sixteen (16) years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register 398 of March 4, 2011.

CHAPTER...
OF HATE CRIMES

Note: Chapter added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 555 of March 24, 2009.

Art. ...- Whoever publicly or by any means suitable for public dissemination incites hatred, contempt, or any form of moral or physical violence against one or

more persons by reason of the color of their skin, their race, sex, religion, national or ethnic origin, sexual orientation or sexual identity, age, marital status or disability, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 555 of March 24, 2009.

Art. ...- Whoever commits acts of moral or physical violence of hatred or contempt against one or more persons by reason of the color of their skin, their race, religion, national or ethnic origin, sexual orientation or sexual identity, age, marital status or disability, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years.

If from the acts of violence referred to in this article, any person is injured, the perpetrators shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years. If such acts of violence produce the death of a person, their perpetrators shall be punished with imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 555 of March 24, 2009.

Art. ...- Whoever, in the exercise of their professional, commercial or business activities, denies a person a service or benefit to which they are entitled, or excludes a person or denies or violates or restricts the rights enshrined in the Constitution, by reason of the color of their skin, their race, religion, national or ethnic origin, sexual orientation or sexual identity, age, marital status or disability, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years.
Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 555 of March 24, 2009.
Art. ...- Whoever, being a public servant, engages in any of the conducts provided for in this chapter or denies or delays to a person a procedure or service to which they are entitled, shall be punished as provided in the preceding article. In these cases the official shall be disqualified from holding any public office, employment or commission for up to the same period as the deprivation of liberty imposed.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 555 of March 24, 2009.

#### CHAPTER IV

Of crimes against the inviolability of the domicile

###### Art. 191

Employees of the administrative or judicial order, officers of justice or police, commanders or agents of the public force who, acting as such, have entered the domicile of an inhabitant, against the latter's will, outside the cases provided for and without the formalities prescribed by Law, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of six to sixteen dollars of the United States

of North America.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 35 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.  

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66
•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 194
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK I)

, Arts. 45

###### Art. 192

Whoever, without order of the authority and outside the cases in which the Law permits entering the domicile of private individuals, against their will, has entered a house, apartment, room or dwelling, inhabited by another, or its enclosed dependencies, whether by means of threats or violence, or by means of breaking, scaling or lock picks, shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to two years and a fine of six to twelve dollars of the United States of North America.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 36 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.  

Jurisprudence:
•	Judicial Gazette, VIOLATION OF DOMICILE, 09-Apr-1907
•	Judicial Gazette, VIOLATION OF DOMICILE, 06-Sep-1949

###### Art. 193

The imprisonment shall be six months to five years and the fine twelve to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America, if the act has been committed with a false order of the public authority, or with the uniform or under the name of one of its agents or with one of the following three circumstances:
If the act has been executed at night;  
If it has been executed by two or more persons; and, If the guilty parties or any of them carried weapons.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 37 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 194

Those guilty of the infractions provided for in the two preceding articles shall be placed under the surveillance of the authority for a time equal to that of the sentence.

###### Art. 195

Whoever has entered, without the consent of the owner, or of the lessee, but without violence or threats, in the

places designated in Art. 192, and has been found therein during the night, shall be punished with imprisonment of fifteen days to six months and a fine of five to twelve dollars of the United States of North America.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 38 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 196

In the violation of domicile it is presumed that there is no consent of the owner or his representative when they are not present in the act that constitutes the violation.  

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66, 355
•	CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 32

Jurisprudence:

•	Judicial Gazette, VIOLATION OF PRIVATE DOMICILE, 07-Oct-2003

#### CHAPTER V

Of crimes against the inviolability of secrecy

###### Art. 197

Those who intercept without judicial order, telephone conversations or those made by similar means and those who remove or open envelopes of correspondence belonging to another without express authorization shall be punished with penalties of 2 months to one year of imprisonment.  

The responsibility of whoever did so is exempted when the telephone interception or the opening of envelopes occurs by mistake, accidentally or fortuitously.  
Note: Article amended by Art. 39 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.  
Note: Articles 197 and 198 unified and replaced by Law No. 0, published in Supplementary Official Register 555 of March 24, 2009.  

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66
•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 150, 151

###### Art. 199

Whoever, being in possession of correspondence not intended for publication, causes it to be published, or presents it in court without judicial order, even if it has been addressed to him, shall be punished with a fine of six to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America, if the act may cause harm to third parties; unless it is correspondence in which obligations appear in favor of the holder thereof, in which

case it may be presented in court.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 41 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.  

Concordances:
•	CODE OF CIVIL PROCEDURE, Arts. 199

###### Art. 200

The same penalty shall be incurred by whoever, without being a public employee, divulges proceedings or procedures of which he has had knowledge and which, by Law, must remain reserved.  

Concordances:

•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 158

###### Art. 201

Whoever, having knowledge, by reason of his status or office, employment, profession or art, of a secret whose disclosure may cause harm, reveals it without just cause, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of eight to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 42 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.  

Concordances:

•	LABOR CODE, Arts. 45
•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 36

###### Art. 202

Those who remove letters entrusted to the mail shall be punished with imprisonment of fifteen to sixty days, except parents, husbands or guardians who take the letters of their children, spouses or wards, respectively, who are under their dependence.  

Art. ...- Whoever, using any electronic, computer or similar means, violates passwords or security systems, to access or obtain protected information, contained in information systems; to violate secrecy, confidentiality and reserve, or simply to violate security, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to one year and a fine of five hundred to one thousand dollars of the United States of North America.  

If the information obtained refers to national security, or to commercial or industrial secrets, the penalty shall be one to three years of imprisonment and a fine of one thousand to one thousand five hundred dollars of the United States of North America.  

The disclosure or fraudulent use of protected information, as well as of commercial or industrial secrets, shall be punished with a penalty of ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years and a fine of two thousand to ten thousand dollars of the United States of North America.  

If the disclosure or fraudulent use is carried out by the person or persons in charge of the custody or legitimate use of the information, they shall be punished with a penalty of minor imprisonment of six to nine years and a fine of two thousand to ten thousand dollars of the United States of North America.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 67, published in Supplementary Official Register 557 of April 17, 2002.  

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66
•	LABOR CODE, Arts. 45, 310

Art. ...- Obtaining and unauthorized use of information.- The person or persons who obtain information about personal data to later transfer it, publish it, use it or transfer it for any consideration, without the authorization of its owner or owners, shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment of two months to two years and a fine of one thousand to two thousand dollars of the United States of North America.  
Note: Article added by Law No. 67, published in Supplementary Official Register 557 of April 17, 2002.  

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66

#### CHAPTER VI

Of crimes relating to the declarations of the accused or their relatives

###### Art. 203

The judge or authority who compels a person to testify against themselves, against their spouse, their ascendants, descendants, or relatives within the fourth civil degree of consanguinity or second degree of affinity, in matters that may result in criminal liability, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years.  

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66, 77

•	CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 22, 23
•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 126, 143

Case Law:

•	Judicial Gazette, PRESUMPTIONS OF CRIMINAL LIABILITY, 27-Jan-1914

###### Art. 204

The judge or authority who extracts statements or confessions from the persons indicated in the preceding article, by means of whipping, imprisonment, threats or torture, shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years and deprivation of citizenship rights for a time equal to that of the sentence.  

The same penalty shall be imposed on police agents or members of the public force who commit the offense indicated in the preceding paragraph.  

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66

#### CAPITULO VII

Offenses against prisoners or detainees

###### Art. 205

Those who issue or execute the order to torture prisoners or detainees, with incommunicado detention for a longer time than that established by Law, with shackles, stocks, bars, handcuffs, ropes, unhealthy cells, or other torture, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and interdiction of political rights for an equal time.  

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 76, 77
•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 72, 211

###### Art. 206

Neither the insecurity of the prisons, nor the dangerousness of the detainee or prisoner, nor the rebellious conduct of the latter, may serve as an excuse in the cases of the preceding article.  

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66

###### Art. 207

The head of a prison or other penal establishment, or whoever replaces him, who receives any convict without a copy of a final sentence imposing the

penalty, or without the constitutional order or warrant, in case of detention, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to six months.  

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 77
•	CODE OF EXECUTION OF SENTENCES AND SOCIAL REHABILITATION, Arts. 38

###### Art. 208

Judges and other employees who have detained or caused to be detained a person, in places other than those determined by Law, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 43 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

#### CAPITULO VIII

Offenses against freedom of work, association and petition

###### Art. 209

The political, civil, ecclesiastical or military authority who demands services not imposed by Law, or compels to work without prior stipulation, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to six months.  

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66
•	LABOR CODE, Arts. 3

###### Art. 210

Whoever exercises violence against another, or threatens him to compel him to take part in a strike or boycott, shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to one year. The same penalty shall be suffered by the employer, entrepreneur or employee who, by himself or on behalf of someone, suspends work in whole or in part in his establishments, agencies or offices, with the purpose of imposing modifications to the established agreements on his dependents; and those who, out of solidarity, do the same in other establishments.  

Concordances:

•	LABOR CODE, Arts. 467, 513, 534, 536

###### Art. 211

The same penalty shall be applied to the employer, entrepreneur or employee who, by himself or on behalf of someone, exercises coercion to compel another to participate in any of the acts determined in the second part of the preceding article, or to abandon, or join

a specific workers' association.  

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 326
•	LABOR CODE, Arts. 440, 510, 513, 534

###### Art. 212

The authority who, in any manner, prevents the free exercise of the right of petition, shall be punished with a fine of six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America and imprisonment of one to six months.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 44 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.  

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 3, 19, 66, 326

#### CAPITULO VIII-A

Offenses Relating to Racial Discrimination

Note: Chapter added by Supreme Decree No. 3194, published in Official Register 769 of February 8, 1979.

###### Art. 212-A.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years

1)	Whoever, by any means, disseminates ideas based on racial superiority or racial hatred;  
2)	Whoever incites, in any form, to racial discrimination;  
3)	Whoever commits acts of violence or incites to commit them against any race, person or group of persons of any color or ethnic origin; and,
4)	Whoever finances, assists or aids any kind of racist activities.  

If the offenses specified in this article are ordered or executed by public officials or employees, the penalty shall be imprisonment of one to five years.  

Note: New article inserted by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.  
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article added by Supreme Decree No. 3194, published in Official Register 769 of February 8, 1979.  

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 11, 416

###### Art. 212

B.- If from the acts of violence referred to in numeral 3 of the preceding article, any person is injured, the perpetrators shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years. If such acts of violence produce the death of a person, their perpetrators shall be punished with reclusion of 12 to 16 years.  
Note: Article added by Supreme Decree No. 3194, published in Official Register 769 of February 8, 1979.

###### Art. 212

C.- Organizations as well as all propaganda and dissemination activities that promote racial discrimination or incite to it are declared illegal, and consequently prohibited in the Republic. Therefore, whoever participates in such organizations or in such activities shall be punished with imprisonment of two months to two years.  

Note: Article added by Supreme Decree No. 3194, published in Official Register 769 of February 8, 1979.

###### Art. 212

D.- Authorities and national, regional or local public institutions are prohibited from promoting or inciting racial discrimination. For the violation of this prohibition, the aforementioned authorities, legal representatives or directors of such institutions shall be held responsible, who shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years and loss of political rights for a time equal to that of the sentence.  

Note: Article added by Supreme Decree No. 3194, published in Official Register 769 of February 8, 1979.

###### Art. 212

E.- Public officials or employees who commit any of the crimes of racial discrimination typified in this Decree shall be subject to the special rules provided in the Political Constitution for the case of violation of the guarantees declared therein.

Note: Article added by Supreme Decree No. 3194, published in Official Register 769 of February 8, 1979.

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 11, 46

Jurisprudence:

•	Judicial Gazette, TYPIFICATION OF SMUGGLING BY THE EXECUTIVE, 08-May-1914

#### CHAPTER IX

Provisions common to this title

###### Art. 213

Any other arbitrary act that violates the freedoms and rights guaranteed by the Constitution, ordered or executed by a public employee or official, by a depositary or agent of the authority or of the public force, shall be punished with imprisonment of three to six months.

With respect to the accusation; pardon, reduction and commutation of the penalty; prescription of actions, and civil liability, the rules specially specified by the Constitution shall also be applied with respect to officials or employees who violate any of the guarantees declared in the Constitution.

Note: Article substituted by Supreme Decree No. 2060, published in Official Register 495 of December 30, 1977.
Note: Supreme Decree No. 2060, repealed by Supreme Decree No. 2124, published in Official Register 508 of January 18, 1978.

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 3, 11, 66

###### Art. 214

Disciplinary obedience may exempt from liability the person who has executed an order contrary to the rights guaranteed by the Constitution, provided that such order, emanating from the respective hierarchical superior and in a matter within his competence, could not have been disobeyed by the subordinate without breach of discipline.

Once these circumstances are proven, all responsibility for the act shall fall upon the superior who issued the order to execute it.

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66, 80, 159

###### Art. 215

If any of the arbitrary acts mentioned in this Chapter has been committed by means of the forged signature of a public employee, the authors of the forgery and those who, maliciously and fraudulently, have used it shall be punished with major imprisonment of four to eight years.

###### Art. 216

Judges and other employees who, without the authorizations prescribed by the Constitution, have requested, issued or

signed an order or judgment against the President of the Republic or his substitute, or against the Ministers of State, the Magistrates of the Supreme Court, or the Members of the Constitutional Guarantees Tribunal; or an order whose purpose is to prosecute them or bring them to trial; or who have given or signed the order or mandate to apprehend or arrest them, shall be punished with a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America and imprisonment of one to three years.

Note: "Councilors of State" is replaced by "Members of the Constitutional Guarantees Tribunal".
Provision given by Legislative Decree No. 104, published in Official Register 315 of August 26, 1982.
