Note: The name is changed to "Constitutional Tribunal". Given by First Transitory Provision of the Constitutional Control Law, Official Register No. 99 of July 2, 1997.
Note: Article amended by Art. 45 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 129, 130

###### Art. 217

Judges and other employees who proceed in the manner indicated in the preceding article with respect to Senators and Deputies, while they enjoy immunity, shall incur the same penalties, except in the case of flagrant crime provided for by the Constitution.

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 77, 128

### TITLE III

OF CRIMES AGAINST PUBLIC ADMINISTRATION

#### CHAPTER I

Of rebellion and attacks against officials

###### Art. 218

Rebellion is any attack, any resistance made with violence or threats against public employees, depositaries or agents of the public force, commissioners for the collection of taxes and contributions, executors of decrees and judicial rulings, guards of customs and collection offices and police agents, when they act in execution of the laws, or of the orders or regulations of the public authority.

Likewise, rebellion is any attack, any resistance with violence or threats, by individuals admitted to hospices, not being deprived of knowledge, or by

prisoners or detainees in jails and other places of correction or repression.

Jurisprudence:

•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF REBELLION, 22-Oct-1889
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIMES AGAINST PUBLIC ADMINISTRATION, 12-Jun-2007

###### Art. 219

Rebellion is also any attack, any resistance made with violence or threats against employees or agents of the telegraph service, when they transmit dispatches from the public authority.

###### Art. 220

Rebellion committed by a single person armed shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to two years. If it has taken place without arms, with imprisonment of eight days to six months.

###### Art. 221

If the rebellion has been committed by many persons and as a consequence of prior agreement, the rebels who carry arms shall be punished with minor imprisonment of three to six years; and the others, with imprisonment of one to three years.

If the rebellion has not been the result of prior agreement, the guilty parties who carry arms shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year; and the others, with imprisonment of fifteen days to three months.

Jurisprudence:
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF REBELLION, 23-Oct-1964

###### Art. 222

In case of rebellion in a gang or mob, no penalty shall be applied to the rebels who did not exercise functions or positions in the gang, if they withdrew at the first warning from the public authority, or if they were apprehended outside the place of the rebellion, without new resistance and without arms.

###### Art. 223

In all cases where, for the act of rebellion, the penalty of deprivation of liberty is applied, the guilty parties may be placed under the surveillance of the authority for up to one year, and fined six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

The leaders of the rebellion, and those who provoked it may be sentenced to surveillance by the authority for two years, at least, and five, at most.

Note: Article amended by Art. 46 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 224

The attempted assassination against the President of the Republic or the person exercising the Executive Function shall be punished with major imprisonment of eight to

twelve years, even if no harm is inflicted.

Note: Last paragraph added by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text.

###### Art. 225

The perpetrator of the same attempt against a Senator or Deputy, Minister of State, Magistrate or Judge, Governor or any other public official who exercises civil or military jurisdiction or authority, when in the actual exercise of his functions, or by reason of his ministry, shall be punished with four to eight years of major imprisonment, even if no harm is inflicted.

If the attack is committed against the life of any other public official who does not exercise jurisdiction, the perpetrator shall be punished with minor imprisonment of three to six years.

###### Art. 226

The provocation to a duel directed at the public officials referred to in the two preceding articles shall be punished with the penalty immediately inferior to that established for attempted assassination against said officials, and according to the distinctions established in the same articles.

###### Art. 227

Whoever wounds or strikes, or physically mistreats, or commits other material violence against the President of the Republic or against whoever acts in his stead, shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment.

###### Art. 228

Whoever wounds, or strikes or physically mistreats any of the officials enumerated in Art. 225, when the latter is in actual exercise, or by reason of the exercise of his functions, shall be punished with one to three years of imprisonment.  
Whoever, in the same case, commits this offense against any other public official who does not exercise jurisdiction, or civil or military authority, shall be punished with imprisonment of two months to one year.

###### Art. 229

If the wounds, blows or mistreatment, by their nature and according to the provisions of this Code, merit another penalty, the penalty of the immediately superior degree shall be applied to the guilty party.  

Jurisprudencia:

•	Gaceta Judicial, HERIDAS, 08-mar-1946
•	Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE HERIDAS, 28-feb-1994

###### Art. 230

Whoever with threats, menaces or insults, offends the President of the Republic or whoever exercises the Executive Function, shall be punished with six months to two years of imprisonment and a fine of sixteen to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North

America.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 47 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 231

Whoever with threats, insults, menaces or violence, offends any of the public officials enumerated in Art. 225, when they are exercising their functions, or by reason of such exercise, shall be punished with imprisonment of fifteen days to three months and a fine of eight to forty-seven dollars of the United States of North America.  

Those who commit the offenses detailed in the preceding paragraph against another official who does not exercise jurisdiction, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to one month.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .  
Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 48 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .  

Jurisprudencia:

•	Gaceta Judicial, AMENAZAS E INJURIAS CONTRA AUTORIDADES PUBLICA, 22-nov-2002

###### Art. 232

Whoever fails to show respect to any tribunal, corporation or public official, when in the exercise of their functions, with words, gestures or acts of contempt, or disturbs or interrupts the act in which they are engaged, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to one month.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .  
Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior.

###### Art. 233

The same penalty shall be applied to whoever insults or offends any person who is present and in the presence of the tribunals or public authorities.

###### Art. 234

Those who, outside the cases expressed in this Code, disobey the authorities when they order something for the better public service, in matters of their respective jurisdiction and in accordance with their legal powers, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to one month.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial

621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .  
Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior.

###### Art. 235

Those who, without legitimate cause, refuse to provide the service required of them in the profession, art or trade they practice, or in any other manner necessary for the administration of justice, or public service, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to two months and a fine of six dollars of the United States of North America, without prejudice to being compelled to provide the service that had been required of them.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 49 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .  

Concordancias:
•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 96
•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO CIVIL, Arts. 254, 255, 261
•	CODIGO DEL TRABAJO, Arts. 514, 515, 516

#### CAPITULO II

Of the usurpation of functions, titles and names

###### Art. 236

Whoever, without legitimate title, pretends to be a civil, military or ecclesiastical public employee, Government agent or commissioner, and exercises as such any function, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of sixteen to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 50 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 237

Whoever, having been appointed as a public official, has entered to exercise his functions without having taken the oath that the Law provides, shall be punished with a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 51 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 238

The public official dismissed, suspended or legally declared under interdiction, who continues in the exercise of his functions after having been notified of the dismissal, suspension or interdiction, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to one year and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.  

The public official who has continued exercising his functions after the period for which he was appointed, except in legal cases, shall be punished with the same penalties.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 52 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .  

Concordancias:
•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO CIVIL, Arts. 9
•	CODIGO CIVIL (

## LIBRO I)

, Arts. 367, 371

###### Art. 239

Whoever has publicly taken a name that does not belong to him shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to three months and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America, or with only one of these penalties.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 53 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .  

Concordancias:
•	LEY GENERAL DE REGISTRO CIVIL IDENTIFICACION Y CEDULACION, Arts. 83, 116

###### Art. 239

A.- Whoever improperly uses military ranks, insignia, uniforms, denominations or distinctive marks shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years.  

The maximum penalty shall be imposed on the head of any Department or Institution who authorizes such use to his subordinates.  

Nota: Artículo agregado por Decreto Supremo No. 1114, publicado en Registro Oficial 159 de 5 de Octubre de 1972 .

###### Art. 239

B.- If the improper use of military ranks, insignia, uniforms, denominations or distinctive marks is done with criminal purpose or as a facilitating means for the commission of a crime, the responsible party shall be imposed imprisonment of three to five years. If the crime has been committed under these circumstances, the offender shall be given the maximum penalty of the most serious crime that he has perpetrated.  

Nota: Artículo agregado por Decreto Supremo No. 1114, publicado en Registro Oficial 159 de 5 de Octubre de 1972 .

#### CAPITULO III

Of the violation of seals and documents

###### Art. 240

When seals placed by order of public authority have been broken, the guardians shall be punished, for simple negligence, with imprisonment of eight days

to six months.

###### Art. 241

Those who have intentionally broken the seals shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years; and if the guilty party is the guardian himself or the public official who ordered or executed the affixing, he shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years.

Concordancias:
•	CODIGO CIVIL (

## LIBRO IV)

, Arts. 2128, 2129

###### Art. 242

If the broken seals are those affixed on papers or effects of an individual accused of a crime that has the penalty of major imprisonment or extraordinary minor imprisonment assigned, or of an individual sentenced to any of these penalties, the negligent guardian shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year.

###### Art. 243

Whoever has intentionally broken the seals placed on papers or effects of the nature stated in the preceding article, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years; and if the guilty party is the guardian or the public official who ordered or executed the affixing, he shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years.

###### Art. 244

If the breaking of the seals has been committed with violence, the guilty party shall be punished with the maximum of the penalties assigned for the infraction.

###### Art. 245

In the cases of Arts. 241, 242, 243 and 244, the guilty party may be sentenced, in addition, to a fine of six to sixty-two dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 54 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

#### CAPITULO IV

Of obstacles placed to the execution of public works

###### Art. 246

Whoever has opposed, by acts of fact, the execution of public works ordered by the competent authority, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to three months.

###### Art. 247

Those who, by means of riot, violence, acts of fact or threats, have opposed the execution of said works, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to two years.

The leaders and promoters shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years.

###### Art. 248

In the cases provided for by the two preceding articles, the guilty parties may in addition, be sentenced to a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of

North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 55 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

#### CAPITULO V

Of the violation of the duties of public officials,
of the usurpation of powers and of abuses of authority

###### Art. 249

The public official to whom corresponds, as such, the compliance and execution of a law, regulation or superior order that is legally communicated to him, does not comply with and execute it, or does not have it complied with and executed, as the case may be, by delay, omission or negligence, shall be punished with a fine of sixteen to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 56 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

Concordancias:

•	LEY ORGANICA DE REGIMEN TRIBUTARIO INTERNO, LORTI, Arts. 109

Jurisprudencia:

•	Gaceta Judicial, FUNCIONARIOS PUBLICOS, 22-nov-1920

###### Art. 250

The same penalty shall be imposed on whoever defers executing or having executed the superior order, even if under pretext of making representations concerning it, except in the following cases:
1o.- When the superior order is manifestly contrary to the Constitution;
2o.- When it is not communicated with the formalities required by the Constitution and the laws, or there is some reason to prudently doubt the authenticity of the order; 3o.- When it is a resolution obtained by deceit, or given against Law and with prejudice to third parties; and,
4o.- When from the execution of the order result or are feared, probably, serious evils that the superior could not have foreseen.

Although in these cases the executor of the order may suspend, under his responsibility, the execution, to make representations to the one who gave it, he shall be punished with the respective penalties, according to the two preceding articles, if he does not adduce in the same representation, the founded reasons that he alleges.

If the superior, after becoming aware of the representation, repeats the order, the inferior must

comply with and execute it immediately, except for the sole case of it being manifestly contrary to the Constitution and the laws.

Concordancias:
•	CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 3, 11, 80, 171

###### Art. 251

When, two or more public officials or bodies depositary of some part of the public authority colluding, whether in a meeting, or by deputation, or correspondence between them, concert some measure to prevent, suspend or hinder the execution of a Law, regulation or superior order, they shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to five years.

If the concert has taken place between the civil authorities and the military corps or their chiefs, those who have provoked it shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment; and the others, with three to five years of imprisonment.

###### Art. 252

In case the civil authorities have formed with the military corps or their chiefs, a conspiracy attacking the security of the State, the provokers shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years, and the others, with six to nine years of the same imprisonment.

###### Art. 253

Those officials who, as a consequence of the agreement, have resigned with the purpose of preventing or suspending, whether the administration of justice, or the fulfillment of a legitimate service, shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to two years and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 57 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 254

The public employee who issues regulations or provisions, exceeding his powers, shall be punished with a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 58 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

Jurisprudencia:

•	Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE USURPACION DE FUNCIONES, 23-ago-1884

###### Art. 255

The judge who arrogates to himself powers proper to the political or administrative authorities, or prevents them from the legitimate exercise of theirs, shall be punished with a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

The same penalty shall be incurred by any employee of the political or administrative order who exercises judicial powers, or prevents the execution of a provision or decision issued by the competent judge.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 59 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 256

The public employee or judge, who legally required to inhibit himself, continues proceeding before the competence or recusation is decided, shall be punished with a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 60 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

Concordancias:
•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO CIVIL, Arts. 21, 848, 856

###### Art. 257

Public sector officials and employees and any person entrusted with a public service who, for their own benefit or that of third parties, has misused public or private funds, instruments representing them, items, titles, documents, movable or immovable property that were in their possession by virtue or reason of their office, whether the misuse consists of embezzlement, arbitrary disposition or any other similar form, shall be punished with ordinary major imprisonment of eight to twelve years. The penalty shall be extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years if the offense relates to funds destined for national defense.

Misappropriation shall be understood as the application of funds to purposes other than those provided for in the respective budget, when this act also implies abuse for personal benefit or that of third parties, for purposes unrelated to the public service.

This provision includes officials who handle funds of the Ecuadorian Social Security Institute or of state and private banks. Also included are officials of the Office of the Comptroller General and the Superintendency of Banks who have participated in audits, examinations or special reviews, provided that the reports issued imply complicity or concealment in the crime being investigated.

Those convicted against whom a conviction is issued shall also be perpetually disqualified from holding any public office or function; for this purpose, the first instance judge shall immediately communicate the judgment, once it becomes final, to the National Personnel Directorate and to the appointing authority of the respective official, and also to the Superintendency of Banks if it concerns a bank official. The National Personnel Director shall refrain from registering appointments or

contracts granted in favor of such disqualified persons, for which purpose a register shall be kept in the National Personnel Directorate containing their names.

The criminal action shall prescribe in double the time specified in article 101.

Officials of the General Revenue Directorate and customs officials who have participated in Determination Acts shall be sanctioned with the same penalty.

Also included in the provisions of this article are the officers, administrators, executives or employees of the institutions of the national private financial system, as well as the members or directors of the boards of directors and boards of administration of these entities, who have contributed to the commission of these offenses.

Note: Article substituted by Art. 396 of the Organic Law of Financial Administration and Control, No. 1429, published in Official Register 337 of May 16, 1977.  
Note: By numeral 1) of article 99 of Law No. 73, published in Supplementary Official Register 595 of June 12, 2002, which contains the Organic Law of the Office of the Comptroller General, Title XI of the Organic Law of Financial Administration and Control is repealed, a title that contained article 396 which substituted article 257 of the Penal Code, the latter being repealed together with Title XI.  
Note: Art. 16 of Supreme Decree No. 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978, amends Art. 257, regarding the duration of penalties, and eliminating "misappropriation" as a crime. By Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, the aforementioned Art. 16 is repealed, however, in Art. 1 it is not restored to the original text, but rather it is addressed in Art. 3, restoring the original penalties. Consequently, there was apparently an intention in the Legislative Decree to eliminate "misappropriation" as a classification of crime. We point this out as a concern for the User.  
Note: Article amended by Law No. 93, published in Supplementary Official Register 764 of August 22, 1995.  
Note: Article amended by Art. 19 of Law No. 26, published in Official Register 190 of May 13, 1999.  
Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.
