Art. 257A.- The persons described in the preceding article who, abusing their positions, have acted fraudulently to obtain or grant related, connected or intercompany loans, expressly violating

legal provisions regarding this type of operation, shall be punished with imprisonment of four to eight years. The same penalty shall apply to beneficiaries who have fraudulently intervened in the commission of this offense and to those who have lent their name for their own benefit or that of a third party.

Note: Article added by Art. 20 of Law No. 26, published in Official Register 190 of May 13, 1999.

###### Art. 257

A.- Persons elected by popular vote, representatives or delegates of the President of the Republic and of other fiscal or municipal officials in State agencies, autonomous or semi-autonomous; officials, employees or public servants who in any way use for their own benefit or that of third parties when this signifies profit or patrimonial increase, workers remunerated by the State or by public sector Entities or public sector goods, shall be punished with a penalty of one to five years of imprisonment and a fine of one hundred ninety to nine hundred fifty-two dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article added by Law No. 6, published in Official Registry Supplement 260 of August 29, 1985.  
Note: Article amended by Art. 61 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.  

Cross-references:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 231, 232

###### Art. 257

B.- With the same penalty shall be sanctioned the persons indicated in the preceding article, who have taken economic advantage for their own benefit or that of third parties, of studies, projects, reports, resolutions and other documents, classified as secret, reserved or of restricted circulation, that are or have been within their knowledge or under their responsibility by reason of or on the occasion of the position they hold or have held.

Note: Article added by Law No. 6, published in Official Registry Supplement 260 of August 29, 1985.

###### Art. 257

C.- The same penalty indicated in the preceding articles shall be imposed on persons elected by popular vote, on representatives or delegates and on officials, employees or public servants who, taking advantage of the popular representation or the position they hold, favor or have favored natural or legal persons so that, contrary to express legal or regulatory provisions, they have granted them contracts or permitted the conduct of business with the State or with any other public sector agency.

Included in the same preceding provision and subject to the same penalty are the directors, members or members of the administrative agencies of the State or of the public sector

in general, who, with their vote, have cooperated in the commission of the crime referred to in the preceding paragraph.

Note: Article added by Law No. 6, published in Official Registry Supplement 260 of August 29, 1985.

###### Art. 257

D.- It corresponds to the Criminal Court to try the crime of embezzlement typified in article 257 of the Criminal Code, subject to the procedure indicated in the current Code of Criminal Procedure and not to that determined in Decree No. 618 published in Official Registry 105 of July 19, 1972, in what remained in force according to the provisions of Supreme Decree No. 888 published in Official Registry No. 631 of September 4, 1974, and then repealed since the Code of Criminal Procedure published in Official Registry No. 511 of June 10, 1983 entered into force.

Note: Article given by Resolution in Plenary of the Supreme Court of Justice, published in Official Registry 572 of November 26, 1986.

###### Art. 258

Note: Article deleted by Art. 396 of the Organic Law of Financial Administration and Control, No. 1429, published in Official Registry 337 of May 16, 1977.

###### Art. 259

Note: Article deleted by Art. 396 of the Organic Law of Financial Administration and Control, No. 1429, published in Official Registry 337 of May 16, 1977.

###### Art. 260

They shall be punished with imprisonment of two to four years, if the case is not included in Art. 257, the public officials and employees or their agents who, in charge of the acquisition or purchase of articles and supplies for the administration, receive commissions or premiums, alter the prices in the articles, the payrolls, etc., and the sellers or suppliers who take part or lend themselves to these illicit combinations.

Case law:

•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF EMBEZZLEMENT, 21-Aug-2007

###### Art. 261

Officials who collect for themselves, when they should do so through another, the fines they impose; or who do not issue a receipt for payment; or do not leave a record of the fine in the corresponding book, shall be punished with fifteen days to one year of imprisonment and four times the fine improperly collected.

Note: Supreme Decree No. 380, of May 27, 1966, published in Official Registry No. 46, of June 2 of the same year, grants popular action to report to the corresponding authority cases of direct collection of fines by officials who impose them and sanctions, in addition, such procedure, with the cancellation of the position and loss of remuneration.

###### Art. 262

They shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment, every public employee and every person in charge of a public service, who has maliciously and fraudulently, destroyed or suppressed documents, titles, programs, data, databases, information or any data message contained in an information system or electronic network, of which they were depositaries, in their capacity as such, or that had been entrusted to them without reason of their position.

Note: Article replaced by Law No. 67, published in Official Registry Supplement 557 of April 17, 2002.  

Cross-references:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 11

###### Art. 263

When pieces or criminal proceedings or other papers, records or documents contained in archives, offices or public deposits, or delivered to a public depositary, in his capacity as such, have been removed or destroyed, the depositary guilty of negligence shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to one year.

Case law:

•	Judicial Gazette, REMOVAL OF PROCEEDINGS, 06-Nov-1951

###### Art. 264

Public employees or persons in charge of a public service who have been guilty of extortion, ordering to collect, demanding or receiving what they knew was not due for rights, quotas, contributions, rents or interest, salaries or gratuities, shall be punished with imprisonment of two months to four years.  
The penalty shall be imprisonment of two to six years, if the extortion has been committed with violence or threats.

This penalty shall be applicable to prelates, priests or other ecclesiastics who demand from the faithful, against their will, tithes, first fruits, parochial rights, or any other obligations that are not authorized by civil Law.

The infractions provided for in this article and in the three preceding ones shall be punished, in addition, with a fine of forty dollars of the United States of North America and with the restitution of four times what they have collected.

These penalties shall also be applied to the agents or official descendants of public employees and of any person in charge of a public service, according to the

distinctions established above.

Note: Penultimate paragraph amended by Law No. 105, published in Official Registry 365 of July 21, 1998.  
Note: Article amended by Art. 62 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.  

Cross-references:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 233
•	LABOR CODE, Arts. 523

Case law:

•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF EXTORTION, 16-May-1930


This excerpt sits under: CRIMINAL CODE > BOOK IV)

###### Art. 265

The public employee who, openly or through an act simulated by him, or through an intermediary, takes for himself, in whole or in part, property or assets in whose auction, lease, adjudication, seizure, sequestration, judicial partition, deposit or administration, he intervenes by reason of his position or office; or any of the referred persons who enters equally in any negotiation or speculation for profit or personal interest, relating to the same properties or assets, or relating to anything in which he has equal official intervention, shall be punished with a fine of six to twelve percent of the value of the property or of the negotiation and with correctional imprisonment of six months to three years and the enforcement of the penalty may not be suspended.  

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

, Arts. 1737

###### Art. 266

Criminal judges, treasurers, administrators and other employees of Customs and of the Guard, who engage in commerce themselves, within the district where they respectively perform their functions, whether openly or through simulated acts, shall be punished with the loss of what is seized from them pertaining to this illicit commerce.  

The same penalty shall be imposed on the President of the Republic, Ministers of State, Governors, General Commanders, Military Zone Chiefs and Magistrates of the courts, if they engage in commerce.  

The provision of this article does not include the sale of products from estates that are owned by the employees, or that they manage as lessees, usufructuaries or users, nor of the products from branches of their own industry in which their family members or their agents are

engaged.  

Nor is this provision applicable to those who place their funds in bank shares or in any enterprise or company, provided that they do not exercise any position in them nor have direct, administrative or economic intervention, nor to those who lend their capital at interest.  

Concordances:
•	COMPANIES LAW, Arts. 101
•	COMMERCIAL CODE, Arts. 7

###### Art. 267

The Magistrate or Judge who, fraudulently and while the lawsuit, proceeding or matter of which he has knowledge is pending, becomes a debtor of one of the parties, or makes one of them his guarantor, or contracts with them any pecuniary obligation, shall be punished with a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America and suspension for three years of citizenship rights.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 63 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.  

Concordances:
•	CODE OF CIVIL PROCEDURE, Arts. 856

###### Art. 268

Note: Article repealed by Law No. 105, published in Official Register 365 of July 21, 1998.

###### Art. 269

Note: Article repealed by Law No. 105, published in Official Register 365 of July 21, 1998.

###### Art. 270

The public official, agent or commissioner of the Government, whatever his employment or rank, who has required or ordered, caused to be required or ordered, the action or use of public force against the execution of a Law or executive decree, against the collection of a legally established tax, or against the execution of a decree, order or judicial sentence, or of any order emanating from authority, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years.

###### Art. 271

If the requirement or order has taken effect, the guilty party shall be punished with three to six years of minor confinement.

###### Art. 272

If the orders or requirements have been the direct cause of other crimes that must be punished with more severe penalties than those expressed in the preceding articles, these more severe penalties shall be applied to the officials, agents or commissioners guilty of having given said orders or made said requirements.

###### Art. 273

When a public official, of whatever nature, an agent of the Government or of the Police, an executor of judicial orders or sentences, a Commander of the Public Force has, without legitimate motive, used or caused to be used violence against any person, in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of his functions, the minimum of the penalty prescribed for these acts shall be increased in accordance with Art.

###### 275. Art. 274

Any commander, officer or subordinate of the Public Force who, after having been legally required by the civil authority, has refused to provide the assistance that it requests from him, shall be punished with imprisonment of fifteen days to three months.  

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 159
•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 269

###### Art. 275

Outside the case in which the Law specifically fixes the penalties with which crimes committed by public officials or employees must be punished, those who have made themselves guilty of other crimes that they were charged with preventing, pursuing or punishing shall be sanctioned with the same penalties prescribed for these crimes, doubling the minimum if the penalty is imprisonment, and increasing it by two years if it is major or minor confinement.

###### Art. 276

In dealing with the crimes incriminated and sanctioned in Arts. 257 to 261 inclusive, 264 and 265, conditional release may not be granted.  

Concordances:

•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 233

#### CHAPTER VI

On malfeasance

###### Art. 277.- Those who commit malfeasance and shall be punished with one to five years of imprisonment are

1o.- Judges of law or arbitrators juris who, by personal interest, by affection or disaffection to any person or corporation, or in prejudice of the public cause, or of an individual, rule against express Law, or proceed criminally against someone, knowing that he does not deserve it;  
2o.- Judges or arbitrators who give advice to one of the parties litigating before them, with prejudice to the opposing party;  
3o.- Judges or arbitrators who in the processing of cases proceed maliciously against express laws, doing what they prohibit or failing to do what they command;  

4o.- Public employees of any class who, exercising any judicial, governmental or administrative authority, by personal interest, affection or disaffection to any person or corporation, deny, refuse or delay the administration of justice, or the protection or other remedy that is legally requested of them or that the public cause requires, provided that they are obliged to do so; or who, required or warned in legal form, by any legitimate authority or legitimate interested party, refuse or delay providing the cooperation or assistance that depends on their powers, for the administration of justice, or any necessity of the public service;  
5o.- Other employees, officials and court clerks who, for any of the causes mentioned in the first paragraph, fraudulently abuse their functions, prejudicing the public cause or any person; and,
6o.- Judges or arbitrators who hear cases in which they represented one of the parties as lawyers or attorneys.  

Concordances:
•	TAX CODE, Arts. 278
•	LAW OF CONTENTIOUS ADMINISTRATIVE JURISDICTION, Arts. 64

Jurisprudence:
•	Judicial Gazette, TRIAL FOR MALFEASANCE OF MAGISTRATES, 01-Jul-1993
•	Judicial Gazette, POLICE OR CIVIL JURISDICTION OF THE CRIME OF MALFEASANCE, 18-Aug-1993
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF MALFEASANCE, 31-Oct-2001

###### Art. 278

If the infractions detailed in the preceding article have been committed in criminal matters, the maximum penalty shall be applied.

###### Art. 279

Lawyers, defenders or attorneys in trial, who disclose the secrets of their client to the opposing party; or who, after having undertaken to defend one party and learning of its claims and means of defense, abandon it and defend the other; or who, in any other way, fraudulently, prejudice their client to favor the opponent, or obtain some personal benefit, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years.  

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

## LIBRO IV)

, Arts. 1737
• CIVIL PROCEDURE CODE, Arts. 38
• LAW OF THE FEDERATION OF LAWYERS OF ECUADOR, Arts. 23, 49

###### Art. 280

Court clerks who, in cases in which they intervene, defend or advise any of the litigants, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year, a fine of

eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America and loss of employment.

Note: Article amended by Art. 64 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 281

Those who maliciously exercise the functions of a judge, verbally or in writing, in a civil or criminal case in which they have an interest, or in which a relative of theirs has an interest in the degree prohibited by Law; or in which they have any other legal impediment to exercise them, shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to one year.

Concordances:
• CIVIL PROCEDURE CODE, Arts. 856

###### Art. 282

Any public official who, without legal order from the competent superior, discloses or reveals any secret entrusted to them by reason of their position, or exhibits any document that should be kept confidential, shall be punished with one to five years of imprisonment.

Concordances:

• LABOR CODE, Arts. 546
• COMPANIES LAW, Arts. 442
• CRIMINAL PROCEDURE CODE 2000, Arts. 151, 154, 158
• SECURITIES MARKET LAW, Arts. 214, 222
• ORGANIC LAW OF FINANCIAL SYSTEM INSTITUTIONS, Arts. 88, 90, 92, 93, 94

###### Art. 283

Lawyers who favor the actions of shysters by authorizing with their signature the writings of the latter shall be punished with imprisonment of two months to one year and suspension from professional practice for the same period.

Concordances:
• CIVIL PROCEDURE CODE, Arts. 213

###### Art. 284

Physicians, surgeons, pharmacists, obstetricians, or any other professional who is the depositary of a secret by reason of the profession they exercise and who reveals it even when testifying in court, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to six months and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America and the execution of the sentence may not be suspended. The case in which the Law obliges them to make said secret known is excepted.

Note: Article amended by Art. 65 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

#### CAPITULO VII

Of bribery

###### Art. 285

Any public official and any person in charge of a public service who accepts an offer or promise, or receives gifts or presents, to execute an act of their employment or office, even if it is just, but not subject to remuneration, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America, in addition to the restitution of double what they have received.

They shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of six to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America in addition to restoring triple what was received, if they have accepted offers or promises, or received gifts or presents either to execute in the exercise of their employment or office a manifestly unjust act; or to refrain from executing an act of their obligation.

Note: Article amended by Art. 66 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

• CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 131, 233
• ORGANIC LAW OF FINANCIAL SYSTEM INSTITUTIONS, Arts. 189

Jurisprudence:

• Judicial Gazette, BRIBERY OF SOCCER MATCH REFEREE, 03-mar-2004
• Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF BRIBERY, 30-jun-2004

###### Art. 286

Any public official and any person in charge of a public service who, by accepted offers or promises, by gifts or presents received, have executed, in the exercise of their position, an unjust act, or have refrained from executing an act that fell within the order of their duties, shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment and with a fine of sixteen to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America, in addition to triple what they have received.

Note: Article amended by Art. 67 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 287

The guilty party shall be sentenced to major imprisonment of four to eight years and a fine of sixteen to one hundred fifty-six dollars of the United States of North America, if they have accepted offers or promises, or received gifts or presents to commit, in the exercise

of their position, a crime.

Note: Article amended by Art. 68 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 288

The judge, arbitrator or mediator, the jury who has allowed themselves to be bribed or suborned shall be punished with four to eight years of major imprisonment and deprivation of the exercise of the legal profession, as applicable.

###### Art. 289

The judge, arbitrator or jury guilty of bribery shall be sentenced, in addition to the penalties mentioned above, to a fine equal to triple the money or value of the reward. In no case may this fine be less than eight dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 69 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

• LAW ON NARCOTIC AND PSYCHOTROPIC SUBSTANCES, Arts. 77

###### Art. 290

Those who have compelled by violence or threats, corrupted by promises, offers, gifts or presents, a public official, a jury, arbitrator or mediator, or a person in charge of a public service, to obtain an act of their employment or office, even if it were just, but not subject to remuneration, or the omission of an act corresponding to the order of their duties, shall be punished with the same penalties as the official, jury, arbitrator or mediator guilty of having allowed themselves to be bribed.

Concordances:

• LAW ON NARCOTIC AND PSYCHOTROPIC SUBSTANCES, Arts. 77, 78

###### Art. 291

In no case shall the things delivered by the corruptor, nor their value, be returned to them; and they shall be confiscated and placed at the disposal of the President of the Republic, so that they may be allocated to the public assistance establishments that they deem appropriate.

#### CAPITULO VIII

Of crimes against judicial activity

###### Art. 292

Any official or any Police agent who, having had notice of the perpetration of a crime, does not immediately bring it to the attention of an investigating judge, shall be punished with imprisonment of fifteen days to six months.

Concordances:

• CIVIL CODE (

## LIBRO III)

, Arts. 1011
• CRIMINAL PROCEDURE CODE 2000, Arts. 26, 209

###### Art. 293

Any physician, surgeon, dentist, obstetrician, or any other person who, in the exercise of a health profession, when providing professional services, discovers a fact that presents the characteristics of a crime and does not report it to the police or to an investigating judge, shall be punished with a fine of eight to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America, unless the report may result in criminal liability for the person assisted.
Note: Article amended by Art. 70 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 294

Anyone who by accusation or denunciation, or by anonymous means, or with a false name affirms that a crime has been committed that has not existed, or who simulates the traces of an offense, to procure a criminal prosecution aimed at obtaining a certification in their favor, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year.

Concordances:

• CRIMINAL PROCEDURE CODE 2000, Arts. 46, 51, 61

###### Art. 295

Anyone who, within proceedings before the examining judge or the trial judge, or extrajudicially, before judicial authorities or police agents, declares himself the author of a crime that has not been committed, or of a crime in which he has had no participation, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to two years.

The foregoing crime shall not be punished when it has been committed to favor an ascendant or descendant, spouse or sibling, or a relative by affinity within the second degree.

###### Art. 296

Anyone who in the course of a civil or administrative proceeding, or before or during a criminal proceeding, in order to mislead the judge, artificially alters the state of things, places or persons, and if the act does not constitute another offense punished more severely by this Code, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 71 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

#### CAPITULO VIII

Of Illicit Enrichment

Note: Chapter incorporated by Art. 2 of Law No. 6, published in Supplementary Official Register 260 of August 29, 1985.

Art. ...- Illicit enrichment constitutes the unjustified increase in the patrimony of a person, produced on the occasion of or as a consequence of the performance of a public office or function, which is not the result of his legally received income.

Note: Article incorporated by Art. 2 of Law No. 6, published in Supplementary Official Register 260 of August 29, 1985.
Note: Article replaced by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 131, 233

Case Law:

•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF ILLICIT ENRICHMENT, 18-Apr-2007

Art. ... .- Illicit enrichment shall be punished with a penalty of two to five years of imprisonment and the restitution of double the amount of the illicit enrichment, provided it does not constitute another crime.

Note: Article incorporated by Art. 2 of Law No. 6, published in Supplementary Official Register 260 of August 29, 1985.
Note: Article replaced by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.

###### Art. 296

C.- The two preceding unnumbered articles are applicable to those who, as officials or employees, manage funds of the Central Bank, the Development and Commercial Credit System Banks and the Ecuadorian Social Security Institute.

Note: Article incorporated by Art. 2 of Law No. 6, published in Supplementary Official Register 260 of August 29, 1985.

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 131, 231, 233

#### CAPITULO IX

Of the publication and distribution of anonymous writings or writings without imprint

###### Art. 297

Anyone who has contributed to the publication or distribution of any printed matter, mimeographed or typewritten or handwritten materials, that do not bear the name of the author or the printer, or of the printing house, known, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year and a fine of twelve to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 72 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:
•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 146, 384

Case Law:

•	Judicial Gazette, ANONYMOUS PRINTED MATTER, 17-Jan-1934

###### Art. 298

The following shall be exempt from the penalty provided by the preceding article: Those who have disclosed the printer, the author or the printing house; and,
The sellers or distributors who have disclosed the persons from whom they received the printed, mimeographed, or written material.

###### Art. 299

Once the printing house or mimeograph in which the anonymous publication was made is discovered, they shall be seized and assigned to a public establishment.

Concordances:

•	INTELLECTUAL PROPERTY LAW, Arts. 324, 325

#### CAPITULO X

Of the crimes of suppliers

###### Art. 300

Persons charged with supplying provisions for the Armed Forces who, voluntarily and maliciously, have failed in the service to which they are obligated, shall be punished with minor imprisonment of three to six years if the offense was committed during a campaign; and if in time of peace, with imprisonment of one to five years.

The same penalties shall be applied, as the case may be, to the agents of the suppliers, if these agents have caused the service to fail deliberately and with malice.

###### Art. 301.- Public officials or agents commissioned or paid by the

Government, who have provoked or assisted the guilty parties in causing the service to fail in time of war, shall be punished with minor imprisonment of six to nine years; and, in time of peace, with imprisonment of one to five years.

###### Art. 302

When the cessation of service is the result of negligence on the part of the suppliers, their agents, public officials, or agents commissioned or paid by the Government, the guilty parties shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to two years in time of war; and, otherwise, with a fine of six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 73 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 303

Even if the service has not failed, if the deliveries have been voluntarily delayed, the guilty parties shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years in case of war; and with a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America, if the Republic is at peace.

Note: Article amended by Art. 74 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 304

In the cases provided for by Art. 302, no prosecution may be brought except by complaint of the Minister of State to whom the matter concerns.

###### Art. 305

If there has been fraud regarding the nature, quality or quantity of the things supplied, the guilty parties shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years.

###### Art. 306

Public officials, or agents or commissioners of the Government who have participated in this fraud shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years.

#### CAPITULO XI

Of escape

###### Art. 307

In case of escape of detainees or prisoners, those charged with conducting or guarding them shall be punished in accordance with the following articles.

Concordances:

•	LAW ON NARCOTIC AND PSYCHOTROPIC SUBSTANCES, Arts. 76

Case Law:

•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF ESCAPE, 26-May-1942
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF ESCAPE OF DETAINEE BY COMPLICITY, 06-Feb-2001

###### Art. 308

If the fugitive is being prosecuted, or has been convicted of a crime that merits imprisonment, said persons in charge shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to three months, in case of negligence and with imprisonment of six months to two years, in case of connivance.

Concordances:
•	CODE OF EXECUTION OF SENTENCES AND SOCIAL REHABILITATION, Arts. 55

###### Art. 309

If the fugitive is being prosecuted, or has been convicted of a crime that merits confinement, said persons in charge shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to one year, in case of negligence; and with three years of minor imprisonment, in case of connivance.

###### Art. 310

Those who, not being charged with guarding or conducting the detainee or prisoner, have procured or facilitated the escape, shall be punished, in the case of Art. 308, with imprisonment of fifteen days to six months; and in the case of Art. 309, with imprisonment of three months to one year.
Ascendants, descendants, spouses and siblings of the fugitive, and their relatives by affinity in the same degrees, are excepted from this provision.

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 22, 23


This excerpt sits under: CODIGO PENAL > LIBRO III)

###### Art. 311

If the escape has taken place or has been attempted with violence, threats, or prison break, the penalties against those who have favored it by providing instruments suitable for carrying it out shall be:
In the circumstances set forth in Art. 308, imprisonment of one to five years against those in charge of guarding or transporting the fugitive, and three months to one year against other persons; and in the circumstances set forth in Art. 309, minor imprisonment for four years against those in charge of the fugitive, and imprisonment of six months to two years against other persons.

###### Art. 312

If the escape has taken place or has been attempted with violence, threats, or prison break, the penalties against those who have favored it with weapons shall be:

In the circumstances set forth in Art. 308, minor imprisonment of three to six years against those in charge of the fugitive, and imprisonment of one to five years against other persons.  

In the circumstances set forth in Art. 309, major imprisonment of four years

against those in charge, and imprisonment of three years against other persons.

#### CAPITULO XII

Of prohibited games and raffles

###### Art. 313

Those who establish houses or tables of prohibited games, without permission from the respective authority, shall be punished with imprisonment of three to six months and a fine of sixteen to sixty-two dollars of the United States of North America.  

The guilty parties may, in addition, be placed under special surveillance of the authority for at least six months and at most one year.  

In all cases, the funds and effects that have been found exposed to the game shall be confiscated, as well as the furniture, instruments, utensils and apparatus intended for the service of the games.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 75 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .  

Concordancias:

•	CODIGO CIVIL (

## LIBRO IV)

, Arts. 2164, 2165, 2168

###### Art. 314

Those who in gaming houses under their charge consent to sons of family, dependents of stores or other commercial or industrial establishments, domestic servants or notoriously vagrant individuals shall be punished with imprisonment of four months to one year and a fine of sixteen to sixty-two dollars of the United States of North America.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 76 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 315

The promoters, entrepreneurs, administrators, commissioners or agents of raffles not authorized by the Police shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to three months and a fine of sixteen to sixty-two dollars of the United States of North America.  

The movable objects placed in the raffle and those used or intended for its service shall be confiscated.  

When real property has been placed in the raffle, confiscation shall not apply, which shall be replaced by a fine of sixty-two to one hundred twenty-five dollars of the United States of North America.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 77 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 316

Those who have placed, announced, or distributed tickets for raffles not permitted by the Police, which may only permit those determined by Law, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to one month and a fine of six to twenty-five dollars of the United States of North America, or with only one of these penalties; and,

Those who by notices, announcements, posters, or by any other means of publication have made known the existence of these raffles.  

In all cases, the tickets, as well as the notices, announcements or posters shall be rendered useless.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .  
Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 78 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 317

Town criers and bill-posters who have made known the person from whom they received the tickets or the writings mentioned above shall be exempt from the penalties established by the preceding article.

### TITULO IV

OF CRIMES AGAINST PUBLIC FAITH

#### CAPITULO I

Of the counterfeiting of coins, bank notes, bearer instruments and credit documents

###### Art. 318

Those who counterfeit gold or silver coins that have legal tender in the Republic, or those who introduce, issue or put such coins into circulation, shall be punished with major imprisonment of four to eight years and a fine of sixteen to one hundred fifty-six dollars of the United States of North America.  

The penalty shall be minor imprisonment of three to six years and a fine of sixteen to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America, if it concerns gold or silver coins that do not have legal tender in the Republic.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 79 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .  

Concordancias:
•	CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 303

•	CODIGO DE COMERCIO, Arts. 573

Jurisprudencia:

•	Gaceta Judicial, INFRACCION PENAL. ELEMENTOS ESENCIALES, 26-feb-1951
•	Gaceta Judicial, ESTAFA CON BILLETES FALSOS, 03-jul-1996

###### Art. 319

If the crime mentioned in the preceding article is committed with coins of other metal that have legal circulation in the Republic, the penalty shall be imprisonment of one to three years and a fine of eight to sixty-two dollars of the United States of North America.  

The penalty shall be three months to one year of imprisonment and a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America, when dealing with coins of other metal that do not have legal tender in the Republic.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 80 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 320

Those in charge of the minting of coins who exceed the quantity for which they were authorized shall be punished as counterfeiters, in accordance with the distinctions established in the preceding articles.

###### Art. 321

Whoever by clipping, drilling, filing, or otherwise alters the value of gold or silver coins that have legal circulation in the Republic shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of eight to forty-seven dollars of the United States of North America.  

When dealing with coins of other metal, the penalty shall be imprisonment of fifteen to ninety days and a fine of six to twelve dollars of the United States of North America.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 81 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 322

The alterations mentioned in the preceding article, of gold or silver coins that do not have legal circulation in the Republic, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of eight to forty-seven dollars of the United States of North America; and if it concerns coins of other metal, the penalty shall be imprisonment of eight to sixty days and a fine of six to nine dollars of the United States of North America.  
Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 82 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 323

Those who commit fraud in the selection of the standards intended, according to the monetary Law, for the verification of the fineness and weight of gold and

silver coins shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.

###### Art. 324

Those who have committed such fraud in the selection of the standards for coins of other metal shall be punished with one to five years of imprisonment.

###### Art. 325

Anyone who, having received counterfeit or altered coins as genuine, has put them back into circulation after having recognized or had their defects recognized, shall be punished with a fine of six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America and imprisonment of one to six months, or with only one of these penalties.

Note: Article amended by Art. 83 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 326

Those who imitate or counterfeit checks, bank notes whose issuance has been authorized by Law; debt securities of the Treasury, of the Provincial Councils and of the Municipal Councils or of any other Institution legally authorized to issue them; securities, certificates or bearer shares legally issued by Banks or Companies authorized to do so and the corresponding interest coupons, as well as those guilty of fraud in the issuance of notes and securities or interest coupons referred to in this article, shall be punished with major imprisonment of four to eight years and a fine of nine to eighty-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 84 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

, Arts. 2355
•	GENERAL LAW ON CHECKS, Arts. 9

Jurisprudence:

•	Judicial Gazette, COMPLICITY IN CASHING FORGED CHECK, 09-Dec-1993

###### Art. 327

Those who, having procured by any means bank notes, put them into circulation without such circulation being legally authorized, shall be punished as counterfeiters.

###### Art. 328

Those who issue notes, bills of exchange, or tokens as conventional currency, or who, in the same manner, use any other object destining it for circulation, shall be punished with a penalty of eight days to six months of imprisonment and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 85 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

#### CHAPTER II

On the counterfeiting of seals, stamps and marks

###### Art. 329.- Those who imitate or counterfeit national seals or stamps, adhesive or fixed, of any kind or value whatsoever, shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years

Those who imitate or counterfeit national seals or stamps, adhesive or fixed, of any kind or value whatsoever; and,

Those who, fraudulently, offer for sale or put into circulation these imitated or false stamps.

If the imitation or counterfeiting has been done in foreign territory, the penalty shall be six to nine years of minor imprisonment.

###### Art. 330

Those who imitate or counterfeit punches, dies, plates, printing plates or any other objects used for the manufacture of stamps, shares, bonds, coupons and bank notes whose issuance has been authorized by Law, shall be punished with the penalties and according to the distinctions established in the preceding article.

###### Art. 331

Anyone who, fraudulently, makes use of imitated or false seals or stamps, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.
Note: Article amended by Art. 86 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Jurisprudence:

•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF FALSIFICATION AND USE OF FALSE DOCUMENT, 16-Dec-2002

###### Art. 332.- The following shall be punished with three months to one year of imprisonment

Anyone who has counterfeited tickets for the transport of persons or goods, or fraudulently made use of a false ticket; and,

Anyone who has counterfeited the seal, stamp or mark of any authority whatsoever, of a private establishment, of a bank, of industry, of commerce or of a private individual, or has fraudulently made use of these false seals, marks or stamps.

###### Art. 333

Anyone who, having procured from any official or from a private individual the true punches, plates,

printing plates or any other tools used for the manufacture of the objects mentioned in Art. 330, has made an application or use of them prejudicial to the rights or interests of the State, shall be punished with minor imprisonment of six to nine years.

###### Art. 334

Those who have imitated or counterfeited the official seals or stamps of foreign nations shall be punished with one to five years of imprisonment.

###### Art. 335

Those who, fraudulently, make use of these imitated or false foreign seals and stamps, shall suffer the penalty of six months to one year of imprisonment.

###### Art. 336

The following shall be punished with a fine of six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America:

Those who have caused to disappear from a postage stamp or other adhesive stamp, the mark indicating that it has already been used; and,

Those who have fraudulently made use of a stamp from which said mark has been caused to disappear.

Note: Article amended by Art. 87 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

#### CHAPTER III

On the falsification of documents in general

###### Art. 337

Public officials who, in the exercise of their functions, have committed a falsification consisting of:
False signatures;
Alteration of records, deeds or signatures; Impersonation;
Writings made or inserted in registers or other public documents, in writings or other judicial proceedings, after their formation or closure, shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

, Arts. 1716, 1717
•	CODE OF CIVIL PROCEDURE, Arts. 164, 165, 180

Jurisprudence:

•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF FALSIFICATION, 20-Jan-2004

###### Art. 338

The public official who, when drafting documents corresponding to his employment, has distorted their substance or their details, shall be punished with the same penalty:

Either by writing stipulations different from those agreed upon or dictated by the parties; Or by establishing as true, facts that were not.

###### Art. 339

Any other person who has committed a falsification in public instruments, in commercial or bank writings, agricultural or industrial pledge contracts or special commercial pledge contracts, in writings or in any other judicial proceeding, shall be punished with a penalty of six to nine years of minor imprisonment:

Either by false signatures;
Either by imitation or alteration of handwriting or signatures;
Either by having invented agreements, provisions, obligations or discharges, or by having inserted them outside the proper time in the documents;
Either by addition or alteration of the clauses, declarations or facts that those documents were intended to receive or verify.

Concordances:

•	COMPANIES LAW, Arts. 126
•	ORGANIC LAW OF FINANCIAL SYSTEM INSTITUTIONS, Arts. 80
•	SECURITIES MARKET LAW, Arts. 215

Jurisprudence:
•	Judicial Gazette, FALSE DOCUMENT, 11-May-2009

###### Art. 340

Anyone who, by any of the means indicated in the preceding article, commits falsification in private instruments, with the exception of checks, shall be punished with two to five years of imprisonment.

Concordances:

•	CODE OF CIVIL PROCEDURE, Arts. 191, 193
•	COMPANIES LAW, Arts. 126

Jurisprudence:

•	Judicial Gazette, FALSIFICATION OF PRIVATE INSTRUMENTS, 05-Mar-2004

###### Art. 341

In the cases stated in the preceding articles, anyone who has fraudulently made use of the false document shall be punished as if he were the author of the falsification.

Concordances:
•	CODE OF CIVIL PROCEDURE, Arts. 180

Jurisprudence:
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIMINAL OFFENSE, 26-Feb-1951
•	Judicial Gazette, FRAUDULENT USE OF FALSIFIED DOCUMENTS, 02-Apr-2007
•	Judicial Gazette, FRAUDULENT USE OF FALSE PRIVATE DOCUMENT, 19-Mar-2009

###### Art. 342

Anyone who counterfeits Bank notes, whose issuance is not authorized, shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment.

###### Art. 343

Whoever has imitated or falsified a passport, or any other public travel document, national or foreign, or has made fraudulent use of said documents, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.  
No liability shall attach to anyone who has been induced to use such documents, when they are a victim of illegal trafficking of migrants.  

Note: Article replaced by Law No. 91, published in Official Registry 716 of December 2, 2002.

###### Art. 344

The public employee who has delivered a passport to a person whom he did not know, without having had his name or status attested by two individuals known to him, and in cases where the Law requires these formalities, shall be punished with a fine of six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.  

If the public employee had knowledge of the false representation of name or status, when he delivered the passport, he shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years.  

He shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years, if he has acted motivated by gifts or promises.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 88 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 345

Whoever, in order to exempt himself or free another from a service legally due, or from any other obligation imposed by Law, has forged a certificate of illness or incapacity, whether with the

name of a physician, surgeon or practitioner, or with any name, falsely adding any of these qualifications, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to one year.

###### Art. 346

The physician, surgeon or practitioner who, to favor someone, has falsely certified illnesses or incapacities suitable for dispensing from a service legally due, or from any other obligation imposed by Law, or to demand or claim a right, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of six to sixty-two dollars of the United States of North America.  

If he has been motivated by gifts or promises, he shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years, in addition to the aforementioned fine.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 89 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 347

Whoever has forged, with the name of a public official, a certificate attesting to good conduct, indigence or any other circumstance suitable for attracting the benevolence of public authority or of private individuals, toward the person designated in said certificate, or to procure employment, credit or assistance for him, shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to one year.  

If the certificate has been forged with the name of a private individual, the guilty party shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to two months.

###### Art. 348

Those who have forged, with the name of a public official, any kind of certificates that may compromise public or private interests, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to five years.  
If the certificate has been forged with the name of a private individual, the guilty party shall be punished with imprisonment of two months to one year.  

Case law:

•	Judicial Gazette, USE OF FALSIFIED PUBLIC INSTRUMENT, 25-Jun-2008

###### Art. 349

Whoever has fraudulently made use of a false or forged certificate in the circumstances enumerated in Arts. 344 to 348 inclusive, shall be punished with the penalties established by these articles and according to the distinctions they establish.

###### Art. 350

The public official who, in the exercise of his functions, has delivered a false certificate, or falsified a certificate, or fraudulently made use of a false certificate, shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment.

###### Art. 351

Innkeepers or hoteliers who have fraudulently registered in their register, with false or assumed names, persons lodged in their establishment, or who in any

other manner have falsified their registers, shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to one year and a fine of six to sixty-two dollars of the United States of North America.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 90 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 352

Employees or persons in charge of a telegraph office who have committed a falsification in the exercise of their functions, by inventing or falsifying telegraphic messages, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years.

###### Art. 353

Whoever has fraudulently made use of the false message, shall be punished as if he were the author of the falsification.  

Art. ...- Electronic falsification.- Guilty of electronic falsification are the person or persons who with intent to profit or to cause harm to a third party, using any means, alter or modify data messages, or the information included therein, that is contained in any material medium, information system or telematic system, whether by:

1.- Altering a data message in any of its elements or requirements of a formal or essential nature;  
2.- Simulating a data message in whole or in part, in a manner that induces error as to its authenticity;  
3.- Supposing in an act the intervention of persons who have not participated or attributing to those who have participated in the act, declarations or statements different from those they have made.  
The crime of electronic falsification shall be punished in accordance with the provisions of this Chapter.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 67, published in Supplemental Official Registry 557 of April 17, 2002.

#### CHAPTER IV

Of false testimony and perjury

###### Art. 354

There is punishable false testimony when, in declaring, confessing, or reporting before public authority, whether the informant is a private person or authority, one knowingly departs from the truth; and perjury, when it is done under oath.  

Excepted are cases of confession and preliminary questioning of the accused in criminal proceedings, and reports by authorities when they may incur criminal liability.  

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 77
•	CODE OF CIVIL PROCEDURE, Arts. 133, 214, 215, 230
•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 117, 133, 137, 293

Case law:
•	Judicial Gazette, PERJURY, 20-May-1887
•	Judicial Gazette, PERJURY, 26-Aug-1916
•	Judicial Gazette, PERJURY, 08-Jan-1937
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF PERJURY, 03-Dec-2002
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF PERJURY, 24-Feb-2006
•	Judicial Gazette, PERJURY, 09-Jul-2009

###### Art. 355

False testimony shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years; and perjury, with minor imprisonment of three to six years.  

Case law:
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF PERJURY, 08-Nov-2001
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF PERJURY, 26-Nov-2001

###### Art. 356

If false testimony or perjury is committed in a criminal case, to the detriment of the accused, the penalty shall be eight to twelve years of major imprisonment.

###### Art. 357

The same penalty shall be imposed whenever the falsehood or perjury is committed for payment, or when the offender habitually offers himself to testify in court as a false witness. And it may be considered that this habit exists if, an individual having testified falsely in two or more proceedings, his suspicious background, his lack of lawful and known occupation or trade, or other circumstances, give reasonable grounds to suppose it.

###### Art. 358

If the judgment declares that the falsity of the testimony is not established, but the lack of lawful occupation or other background that would make the conduct of the accused suspicious is established, the latter shall remain subject to the surveillance of the authority for two to five years.

###### Art. 359

Those who bribe witnesses, experts or interpreters, or those who knowingly make use in court of false witnesses or experts, whether in their own case or that of their clients or represented parties, shall be punished as guilty of false testimony or perjury, as the case may be.

The minimum penalty shall be increased by one year if the bribed witness, expert or interpreter is a peasant or montubio.

If a lawyer commits the offense determined in this article, in the same judgment he shall also be permanently deprived of professional practice.

If a professional gives a report in which he fraudulently departs from the truth, he shall also be permanently deprived of professional practice, without prejudice to the other penalties established in this Chapter.

Concordancias:
•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO CIVIL, Arts. 250, 264

###### Art. 360

Interpreters and experts shall be considered as witnesses for the purposes of the preceding articles.

#### CAPITULO V

Of crimes relating to commerce, industries and auctions

###### Art. 361

Whoever maliciously or fraudulently has communicated the secrets of the factory in which he has been or is employed, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to three years and a fine of eight to sixty-two dollars of the United States of North America.
Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 91 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

Concordancias:

•	CODIGO DEL TRABAJO, Arts. 45, 310, 546

###### Art. 362

Whoever, with the purpose of forcing the rise or fall of wages, or of attacking the free exercise of industry or labor, has committed violence, uttered insults or threats, imposed fines, prohibitions or any interdiction, whether against those who work, or against those who make others work, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to three months and a fine of six to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America, or with only one of these penalties.

The same penalty shall be imposed on those who, by means of gatherings near the establishments where work is carried out, or near the residence of those who direct the work, have attacked the freedom of masters or workers.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 92 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 363

The following shall be punished with imprisonment of two months to two years and a fine of thirty-one to one hundred twenty-five dollars of the United States of North America:

1o.- Those who cause the price of merchandise, papers, effects or

securities to rise or fall by any fraudulent means, or by meeting or coalition among the principal holders of a merchandise or commodity, with the purpose of not selling it except at a determined price;
2o.- Those who offer public funds, or shares or obligations of a company or legal person, affirming or suggesting false facts or circumstances; and, 3o.- The founder, administrator, director, manager or trustee of a company or of a
legal person of another nature, who publishes or authorizes a false or incomplete balance sheet or any other report, whatever the purpose in doing so.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 93 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

Concordancias:
•	CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 233
•	LEY DE COMPAÑIAS, Arts. 126
•	LEY ORGANICA DE INSTITUCIONES DEL SISTEMA FINANCIERO, Arts. 125, 128, 208
•	LEY DE MERCADO DE VALORES, Arts. 52, 53

###### Art. 363

A.- Those who, without legal authorization, raise or participate in any manner in the raising of prices of staple food items intended for human consumption, whether their action consists of monopoly, concealment, hoarding, speculation, displacement or any other fraudulent form that produces disappearance, price increase or limitation of both the production and commercialization of said products, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years and confiscation of the articles.

If it is a legal person, the responsibility or sanction shall fall on its legal representatives, administrators or executives.

In case of recidivism, double the penalty and confiscation of the products or goods that are the subject of the offense shall be applied, the ownership of which shall pass to the National Enterprise of Vital Products or to the Entity that replaces it, both for the present case and for that contemplated in the first paragraph of this article.

Nota: Artículo agregado por Decreto Supremo No. 87, publicado en Registro Oficial 480 de 25 de Enero de 1974 .

Concordancias:

•	CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 284, 335

###### Art. 363

B.- The Special Courts of the Republic, within their respective jurisdictions, shall be competent to hear, process in accordance with the Law and sanction

the crimes established in the preceding article.

Nota: Artículo agregado por Decreto Supremo No. 87, publicado en Registro Oficial 480 de 25 de Enero de 1974 .

###### Art. 363

C.- The Intendants and Police Commissioners and Agents of the Public Force shall proceed to the immediate capture of persons involved in the commission of the crimes specified in the first article of this Decree and, within twenty-four hours, shall place them at the orders of the corresponding Special Court for trial.

Nota: Artículo agregado por Decreto Supremo No. 87, publicado en Registro Oficial 480 de 25 de Enero de 1974 .

Jurisprudencia:
•	Gaceta Judicial, FACULTADES DE LOS COMISARIOS DE POLICIA, 11-abr-1885

###### Art. 363

D.- Popular action is established to report the acts determined in the first article of this Decree.

Nota: Artículo agregado por Decreto Supremo No. 87, publicado en Registro Oficial 480 de 25 de Enero de 1974 .

###### Art. 364

The director, manager or administrator of a company or of a legal person of another nature who lends his assistance or consent to acts contrary to the statutes, laws or ordinances that govern them, as a consequence of which the legal person or company is rendered unable to satisfy its commitments, or in the need to be dissolved, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years.

Concordancias:

•	LEY DE COMPAÑIAS, Arts. 17, 91
•	LEY ORGANICA DE INSTITUCIONES DEL SISTEMA FINANCIERO, Arts. 132

###### Art. 365

The general commander, the head of military zone, the head of troops, the governor or political chief who, in the extent of the places where he has the right to exercise his authority, has employed the means indicated in number 1o of Art. 363, or has taken part in the speculation indicated therein, whether openly, or through simulated acts or through interposition of persons, shall incur the penalties established for the offense, the minimum being increased by one year, and the fine being seventy-seven to three hundred eleven dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 94 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 366

Those who, by means of riots, or with violence or threats, have disturbed public order in the markets, with the purpose of provoking looting, or only with that of forcing sellers to dispose of their merchandise at a price lower than that which would result from free competition, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to two years.

The leaders or promoters shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to five years and placed under the surveillance of the authority for at least two years and at most five.

Jurisprudencia:
•	Gaceta Judicial, SAQUEO, 28-oct-1912
•	Gaceta Judicial, ROBO Y SAQUEO, 07-dic-1912

###### Art. 367

Whoever has altered or damaged merchandise, or the materials or instruments used for its manufacture, shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to one year and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

The imprisonment shall be six months to three years if the crime has been committed by a person employed in the factory, workshop or commercial establishment.

The penalty shall be one to five years of imprisonment and a fine of thirty-one to one hundred fifty-six dollars of the United States of North America, if the crime has been committed by employees of the factory, workshop or commercial establishment, with the purpose of discrediting the industry, or by bribery or corruption.
Note: Article amended by Art. 95 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

•	INTELLECTUAL PROPERTY LAW, Arts. 319, 320, 321, 322, 323

CHAPTER ...
On crimes related to the illicit commercialization of fuels derived from hydrocarbons, including liquefied petroleum gas and biofuels

Note: Chapter added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

Art. (1)...- Illegal trafficking of hydrocarbons, their derivatives, liquefied petroleum gas and biofuels.- Natural persons or legal representatives of legal entities who, by fraudulent, deceitful or clandestine means, sell, offer, distribute or commercialize under any title in border zones, in maritime or river ports or in the territorial sea, for the purpose of illegally removing from the country, any hydrocarbon, its derivatives including liquefied petroleum gas and biofuels, shall be punished with a penalty of three to six years of ordinary minor imprisonment, a fine of one thousand to two thousand unified basic remunerations for workers in general and the special confiscation of the goods and means of transport that served for the commission of the crime.

The owner or administrator of supply plants, storage, packaging, distribution centers, commercializers, service stations, who are authorized for the storage, transport and commercialization of fuels, and who with their action or omission and in a fraudulent and deceitful manner, allow the perpetration of the crime indicated in the preceding paragraph, shall be punished with a penalty of ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years, a fine of one thousand to two thousand unified basic remunerations for workers in general and definitive closure of the establishment.

Note: Article added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

Art. (2)...- Storage, transportation and commercialization without proper authorization.- Those who commercialize, store, illegally transport hydrocarbon derivatives especially in border zones and territorial sea, without authorizations, remission guides and other permits required by law for the execution of such activities, shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment of one to three years and a fine of five hundred to one thousand unified basic remunerations for workers in general and the special confiscation of the goods used for the execution of the crime. With equal penalties, the Director of DIGMER or his responsible delegates who illegally and fraudulently issue such authorizations shall be punished.
Note: Article added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

Art. (3)...- Adulteration of hydrocarbon derivatives.- Whoever in a fraudulent or deceitful manner to obtain personal benefit or that of a third party, adulterates the quality or quantity of hydrocarbons, their derivatives including liquefied petroleum gas and biofuels, shall be punished with imprisonment of two to three years and the special confiscation of the goods used for the execution of the crime.

Note: Article added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

Art. (4)...- Improper use of hydrocarbon derivatives.- Those who for their own benefit or that of third parties, use hydrocarbon derivatives, including liquefied petroleum gas and biofuels, in activities other than those expressly permitted by law, shall be punished with imprisonment of one year and the special confiscation of the goods used in the execution of the crime.

Note: Article added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

Art. (5)...- Extraction of hydrocarbons, their derivatives, biofuels or mixtures containing them.- Whoever by fraudulent or clandestine means seizes hydrocarbons, their derivatives, including liquefied petroleum gas and biofuels, when they are transported through an oil pipeline, gas pipeline, polyduct or through any other means, or when they are stored in immediate supply sources or pumping plants, shall be punished with a penalty of ordinary major imprisonment of six to nine years and a fine of two to three thousand unified basic remunerations for workers in general and the confiscation of the goods used in the execution of the crime.

Note: Article added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

Art. (6)...- Damages to the State and environment.- Whoever fraudulently destroys hydrocarbon infrastructure and/or causes the destruction or deterioration of the environment, shall be punished with ordinary major imprisonment of eight to twelve years, without prejudice to the perpetrator remedying the environmental damages and the recovery of the goods by the State.

Note: Article added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.
Art. (7)...- Destination of goods subject to special confiscation.- Once the prosecutor has determined the illicit origin of the hydrocarbons, their derivatives, liquefied petroleum gas or biofuels, he shall request the judge to deliver them to PETROCOMERCIAL so that it may give them the use convenient to the interests of the State. The judge with the prosecutor's request shall order the immediate delivery to PETROCOMERCIAL of the goods used in the commission of the infraction.

Note: Article added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

Art. (8)...- Sanctions for officials and members of the public force.- If the crimes provided for in this Chapter are committed by officials, employees, public servants or members of the Public Force, who in a fraudulent and clandestine manner or by their actions or omissions allow the commission of such crimes, the maximum penalties provided for each of them shall be applied.

Note: Article added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

Art. (9)...- Whoever or those who paralyze or suspend in an unjustified manner the public service of sale or distribution of fuels, whether these be gasoline, diesel, liquefied gas and other products derived from petroleum or biofuels such as ethanol, shall be punished with a fine of five hundred to one thousand unified basic remunerations for workers in general and the definitive revocation of the sale or operation permit, granted by the National Directorate of Hydrocarbons, without prejudice to the civil and criminal actions that may arise.

Note: Article added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

General Provisions

FIRST.- Jurisdiction.- The criminal judges and courts of the jurisdiction where the infraction was committed or where the hydrocarbons, their derivatives, liquefied petroleum gas or biofuels or mixtures containing them were apprehended, shall have jurisdiction to judge the crimes provided for in this Law, as determined by Title One of the Code of Criminal Procedure.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

SECOND.- The Public Prosecutor's Office as holder of the criminal action, shall take cognizance of the cases, direct and promote the investigation of the crimes typified and punished in the preceding articles, for which it shall have the support of the Public Force, especially the National Police, who shall submit to the regulations established in Chapter One of Book Four of the Code of Criminal Procedure; and, if grounds are found, shall accuse the alleged offenders, subject to the current criminal procedure and that contemplated in this Law and its Regulations.
Note: Provision added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

THIRD.- The economic resources generated by the application of the pecuniary sanctions and fines provided for in this Law, shall be deposited into a special account that the National Directorate of Hydrocarbons shall maintain, which shall be under the control and supervision of the Office of the Comptroller General of the State. Such resources shall be exclusively used for technology, monitoring, control and supervision established in the unnumbered article contained in article 6 of this Law.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

FOURTH.- Popular action is granted for the denunciation of the infractions established in this Law, in which case the denouncer shall be recognized 25% of the fine imposed definitively on the authors of the infractions. The denunciation shall entail civil and criminal responsibilities in the event that the denounced control subject obtains a judicial declaration that qualifies it as malicious or reckless. The respective regulation shall define the requirements for the exercise of popular action, which shall be issued within a maximum period of sixty days, from the effective date of this Law.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

FIFTH.- The National Directorate of Hydrocarbons shall provide and facilitate the authorizations for distribution of hydrocarbon derivatives and liquefied petroleum gas for the rural or suburban area that commercialize domestic quantities for local consumption, and that are destined for agricultural activities, small industry and artisanal activities. Failure to comply with the provisions of this General Provision shall be grounds for immediate removal or dismissal from their functions or the immediate termination of the contractual relationship of those responsible, as applicable.

The National Directorate of Hydrocarbons shall regulate what pertains to determining the scope and quantification of the domestic quantities for local use indicated in this General Provision.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

SIXTH.- The National Directorate of Hydrocarbons, shall prepare in coordination with PETROCOMERCIAL or whoever acts in its place, the monthly program of requirements of the hydrocarbon and derivative supply commercializer, including liquefied petroleum gas and biofuels, for the entire national territory, which shall be approved by the Ministry of the sector; for this purpose, population density, the automotive, industrial and shipping fleet, including artisanal fishing in each of the territorial jurisdictions shall be considered in order to guarantee timeliness, quality, quantity and price for the benefit of consumers and to prevent smuggling.
Those responsible for non-compliance with this provision, shall be punished with a fine of one hundred unified basic remunerations for workers in general and immediate dismissal from office. The semi-annual programming reports shall be sent to the National Congress.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

Jurisprudence:
