SEVENTH.- Given that by state policy that promotes subsidies for fuel consumption, border citizens engage in selling small quantities of fuels on the southern border and on the northern border, the State shall establish an emergency program of training and support for microenterprises with promotional credit and facilities for micro-commerce and exchange in border zones.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.

Transitory Provision

The President of the Republic within ninety days, counted from the date of promulgation of this Law, shall issue the corresponding Regulation.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 85, published in Official Registry Supplement 170 of September 14, 2007.


This excerpt sits under: CODIGO PENAL > LIBRO IV) > CAPITULO V

#### CHAPTER VI

On payment with checks without sufficient funds
Note: Chapter amended by Art. 96 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.  
Note: Chapter and its article repealed by Law No. 0, published in Supplementary Official Registry 555 of March 24, 2009.

### TITLE V

ON CRIMES AGAINST PUBLIC SECURITY

#### CHAPTER I

On illicit associations

###### Art. 369

Any association formed with the purpose of attacking persons or property is a crime that exists by the mere fact of the organization of the group.  

Concordances:
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

, Arts. 1961

Case law:
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF ILLICIT ASSOCIATION, 15-Oct-2001
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIMES FOR POSSESSION OF WEAPONS AND ILLICIT ASSOCIATION, 20-Nov-2002

###### Art. 370

If the association has had as its purpose the perpetration of crimes that merit a penalty of major imprisonment, the instigators of the association, its leaders and those who have exercised any command therein, shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment.  
They shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years, if the association has been formed to commit crimes punished with minor imprisonment; and with correctional imprisonment of six months to three years, if the association has been formed to commit crimes of another nature.  

Case law:

•	Judicial Gazette, CLASSIFICATION OF CRIME DIFFERENT FROM THE OPENING OF THE PLENARY, 03-Sep-2002

###### Art. 371

Any other individuals who have taken part in the association and those who have fraudulently supplied the group or its divisions with weapons, ammunition, instruments to commit the crime, lodging, hiding place or meeting place, shall be punished:

In the first case of the preceding article, with imprisonment of one to five years; In the second case, with imprisonment of three months to three years; and,
In the third case, with imprisonment of two months to one year.

###### Art. 372

Those convicted to imprisonment by virtue of Arts. 370 and 371, may be placed under special surveillance of the authority for at least two years and at most five years.

#### CHAPTER II

On unlawful possession of explosives

###### Art. 373

It is strictly prohibited for private individuals to keep in their homes, warehouses or installations of any type materials and explosives that constitute elements of

danger to persons and property and that must be kept or stored in technically adequate places and sites.  

Note: Article replaced by Law No. 31, published in Official Registry 231 of March 17, 2006.

###### Art. 374

Those who have such explosive materials in homes, warehouses or installations of any type, are obliged to deliver them to the corresponding military or police authorities, except for artisanal activities dedicated to the manufacture of pyrotechnic devices and mining activities, provided they are stored in unpopulated places.  
Note: Article replaced by Law No. 31, published in Official Registry 231 of March 17, 2006.

###### Art. 375

Those who contravene the preceding provisions shall be punished with imprisonment of 2 to 4 years.  

Note: Article replaced by Law No. 31, published in Official Registry 231 of March 17, 2006.

###### Art. 376

Attacks against persons or property by means of explosives shall be punished with sixteen years of extraordinary major imprisonment.

#### CHAPTER III

On intimidation

###### Art. 377

Whoever by writing, anonymous or signed, threatens another with any attack against persons or property, that merits a penalty of minor imprisonment, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America, if the threat has been accompanied by an order or condition.  

Otherwise, the penalty shall be three months to one year and a fine of six to nine dollars of the United States of North America.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 97 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 378

If the threat made with an order and under condition has been verbal, the guilty party shall be punished with imprisonment of one to six months and a fine of six dollars of the United States of North America.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 98 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 379.- Whoever by writing, anonymous or signed, threatens another with an attack

against persons or property, that merits a penalty of major imprisonment, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America, if the threat has been accompanied by an order or condition; and, otherwise, with imprisonment of six months to one year and a fine of six to twelve dollars of the United States of North America.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 99 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 380

In the cases provided for in the three preceding articles, the guilty party may, in addition, be placed under special surveillance of the authority for a time not exceeding four years.

###### Art. 381

Excluded from the preceding provisions are threats made in the course of any quarrel or fight, aggression, offense, provocation or insult, which shall not be subject to any penalty other than that incurred for the aggression, offense, or quarrel itself.

###### Art. 382

Whoever, in order to instill public fear or incite tumults or disorders, makes signals, gives alarm cries, detonates bombs or explosive materials or places them for that purpose, or threatens with a disaster of common danger, when these events do not constitute a more severely punished crime, shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to two years.

#### CHAPTER IV

On vagrants and beggars

###### Art. 383

Vagrants are those who have no fixed domicile or means of subsistence, and those who, without illness or injury that incapacitates them, do not habitually exercise a trade or profession.  

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK I)

, Arts. 103

###### Art. 384.- They shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year

Vagrant beggars who have been found carrying false certificates or passports, or who feign injuries or illnesses;  

Vagrants or beggars who have been found carrying weapons, or have threatened an attack on persons or property, or exercised an act of violence against them; and,

Vagrants or beggars who are found provided with files, lock picks or other instruments suitable for committing robberies or other crimes, or for procuring the means to

enter houses.

###### Art. 385

The beggar who has been apprehended disguised in any manner, or who flees from the establishment in which the authority has placed him, shall be punished with imprisonment of two months to one year.

#### CHAPTER V

On instigation to commit crimes

###### Art. 386

Whoever publicly instigates the commission of a specific crime against a person or institution, when the instigator cannot be legally considered as an accomplice, shall be punished, for the instigation and even if the crime has not been perpetrated, with imprisonment of fifteen days to two years, according to the gravity of the instigated crime.

#### CHAPTER VI

On apology for crime

###### Art. 387

Whoever publicly and by any means makes an apology for crime, or for a person convicted of a crime, by reason of the act committed, shall be punished with a fine of eight to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America.  

The same penalty shall apply to whoever makes an apology for a suicide.  

Note: Article amended by Art. 100 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002. .

#### CHAPTER VII

On arson and other destructions, deteriorations and damages

###### Art. 388

Those who have set fire to the following shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years:

1o.- Vessels, aircraft, warehouses, shipyards, buildings or any other places that serve as dwellings and contain one or more persons at the time of the fire;  
2o.- Buildings that serve for gatherings of individuals, during the time of these gatherings; and,
3o.- Any place, even uninhabited, if it contains deposits of gunpowder or other explosive materials; and if, according to the circumstances, the perpetrator should have presumed that there were one or more persons therein at the time of the crime, or if the fire could spread to other nearby inhabited buildings.  

The penalty shall be six to nine years of minor imprisonment, if the walls of the building are made of stone, brick, or other incombustible materials, and do not contain within their enclosure deposits of explosive materials.

###### Art. 389

Those who have voluntarily set fire, either to the objects designated in the preceding article, but outside the cases provided for by said article, or to forests, groves, workshops or sown fields, shall be punished with minor imprisonment of three to six years.  

If these objects belong exclusively to those who have set them on fire, and the fire was set with fraudulent intent, the guilty parties shall be punished, in cases not covered by the preceding article, with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of six to twenty-five dollars of the United States of North America. If there is no fraud or danger that the fire may harm others, the person who sets fire to his own property shall not be punished, unless the fire causes harm to the national economy.  

If the fire has been set to harvested crops; cut wood placed in piles, the crime shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years.  

If the harvested crops or cut wood have not been gathered together, the penalty shall be imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.  

If the burned crops or wood belong exclusively to the arsonist, and if the fire has been set with fraudulent intent, the penalties shall be: in the first case provided for in this article, imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America; and in the second case, imprisonment of two months to two years and a fine of six to nine dollars of the United States of North America.  
It shall be understood that there is fraudulent intent when the burning of the objects indicated in this article is detrimental to the national economy and has no reasonable justification.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 101 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 390

In the cases provided for by the preceding articles, the convicted person may also be placed under special surveillance by the authorities for at least two years and at most five.

###### Art. 391

Whoever, with the intention of consummating any of the acts provided for in the preceding articles, has set fire to any objects placed in such a way that the fire could spread to the thing that was intended to be destroyed, shall be punished as if he had directly set or attempted to set fire to the latter thing.

###### Art. 392

When the fire has spread from the object that the guilty party wanted to burn to another object whose destruction carries a more severe penalty, the latter shall be applied if the things are placed in such a way that the fire must necessarily spread from one to the other.

###### Art. 393

When the fire has caused the death of one or more persons, the penalty shall be special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years.  

If it has caused injuries or permanent lesions, the arsonist shall be punished with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .  
Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo reformado por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .

###### Art. 394

The burning of movable or immovable property of another, which has been caused either by the age or lack of repair or cleaning of ovens, chimneys, forges, houses or nearby workshops; or by fires lit in the fields, less than one hundred meters from buildings, forests, thickets, orchards, or plantations, fences, piles of grain, straw, hay, fodder or any other deposit of combustible materials; or by fires or lights carried or left without sufficient precaution; or by fireworks lit or thrown recklessly, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to three months and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 102 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 395

Those who have destroyed or attempted to destroy, by means of an explosion, buildings, vessels, aircraft, carriages, wagons, warehouses, shipyards or other constructions, shall be punished with the penalties specified in the preceding articles and according to the distinctions established therein.  

The provision of Art. 391 is applicable to the cases provided for in this article.

###### Art. 396

The burning of huts, haystacks, uninhabited sheds, or any other object whose value, exceeding ten sucres does not reach fifty, and in which there is no danger of the fire spreading, shall be punished with imprisonment of fifteen to ninety days.

###### Art. 397

Whoever has destroyed or demolished, in whole or in part, buildings, bridges, dikes, causeways, roads, railways, aqueducts, aerodromes, or other national, municipal, or privately owned constructions, shall be punished with imprisonment of three to five years.  

The total or partial destruction of installations for fire alarm services, as well as the improper use of such services, shall be punished with the same penalty.  

Concordancias:

•	CODIGO CIVIL (

## LIBRO II)

, Arts. 608

Jurisprudencia:
•	Gaceta Judicial, JUSTICIA POR PROPIA MANO, 30-ene-1995
•	Gaceta Judicial, DESTRUCCION DE BIENES AJENOS, 22-sep-2009

###### Art. 398

Those who destroy aqueducts belonging to others that do not constitute masonry works shall be punished with imprisonment of three to six months.  

When dealing with new works that attack the property or possessory right of the person or persons who have destroyed the aqueduct or intake, the provisions of the preceding paragraph shall not be applicable.  
The same penalty as specified in the first paragraph shall be applied to those who destroy living fences or fences of any other kind, when the act does not constitute usurpation.  

Concordancias:

•	CODIGO CIVIL (

## LIBRO II)

, Arts. 903

###### Art. 399

Whoever has destroyed a machine belonging to another, whether hydraulic, steam, electric, or movable by animal power, if it is intended for industrial or agricultural use, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of sixteen to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America.  

There is destruction from the moment the action of the machinery has been paralyzed in whole or in part, whether the act affects the motor apparatus or the apparatus set in motion.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 103 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 400

When the act provided for by the preceding article has been committed in a gathering or gang, or by means of violence, assault, or threats, the guilty parties shall be punished with imprisonment of three to five years.

###### Art. 401

Whoever has destroyed, demolished, mutilated, or damaged the following objects shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to one year and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America:

1o.- Tombs, commemorative signs, or gravestones;  
2o.- Monuments, statues, or other objects intended for public utility or ornamentation and erected by the authority or with its authorization; and,
3o.- Monuments, statues, paintings, or any other object of art, placed in churches, chapels, or other public buildings.  

In case of destruction or violation of sepulchers, to steal the mortuary boxes, the objects enclosed with the corpses, or the corpse itself, the penalty shall be imprisonment of three to five years. The same penalty shall be imposed on whoever commits the infraction to take advantage of the materials of the destroyed tomb, or to satisfy an act of vengeance.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 104 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .  

Jurisprudencia:
•	Gaceta Judicial, VIOLACION DE TUMBAS, 15-dic-1943

###### Art. 402

Whoever has destroyed, in any manner, authentic records or original instruments of the public authority, civil or criminal proceedings, shall be punished with major imprisonment of four to eight years.  

The destruction of titles, bills of exchange, commercial or bank documents, or any fiduciary instrument issued by virtue of a Law, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of sixteen to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America.  

The destruction of receipts, obligations, drafts, or other private documents, that contain proof of an act or contract, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years.  

If the aforementioned infractions have been committed with one or more aggravating circumstances, the penalties shall be replaced in the following manner:

Major imprisonment of four to eight years, with an equal penalty of eight to twelve years; Imprisonment of one to five years, with minor imprisonment of three to six years; and,
Imprisonment of six months to three years, with imprisonment of two to five years.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 105 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .  

Concordancias:

•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO CIVIL, Arts. 164, 191

•	CODIGO DE COMERCIO, Arts. 410

###### Art. 403

Any destruction or damage to movable property of another, executed without violence or threats, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to one year and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.  
Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 106 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .  

Concordancias:

•	CODIGO CIVIL (

## LIBRO II)

, Arts. 585

###### Art. 404

If the act has been committed in a gathering or in a gang, the penalty shall be three months to two years of imprisonment.

###### Art. 405

The destruction or damage to movable property of another, executed with violence or threats, in an inhabited house or in its dependencies, and with the concurrence of any of the aggravating circumstances, shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment.  

The penalty shall be minor imprisonment of six to nine years, if the crime has been committed in a gathering or in a gang.  

Art. ...- The destruction or damage to movable or immovable property belonging to third parties, committed inside sports venues or in their surroundings or other places that by their capacity, can host massive gatherings of the public, shall be punished with the penalty of three to six years of ordinary minor imprisonment.  

Nota: Artículo agregado por Ley No. 63, publicada en Registro Oficial 412 de 7 de Diciembre del 2006 .

###### Art. 406

If the violence or threats with which the destruction or damage has been committed caused an incurable disease, or a permanent bodily injury, the guilty parties shall be punished with the penalty immediately superior to that which they would have incurred, according to the preceding article; and if they have caused death, they shall be punished with special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years.  
Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .  
Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo reformado por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .

###### Art. 407

Whoever has removed, cut or destroyed the moorings, or the obstacles that held a vessel, a wagon or a carriage, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to two years.

###### Art. 408

Whoever has cut or felled crops, or plantations due to the industry of man or to nature, shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to three years and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 107 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .  

Jurisprudencia:
•	Gaceta Judicial, HURTO DE SEMENTERA DE PAPAS, 10-may-1955
•	Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE DESTRUCCION DE SEMENTERAS, 20-feb-2001

###### Art. 409

Whoever has felled or destroyed a sown field; or scattered in the seeds weeds, or any harmful herb or plant; or broken or damaged agricultural instruments, animal pens, or the huts of the guardians, shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to two years and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 108 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .  

Jurisprudencia:

•	Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE DESTRUCCION DE SEMBRIOS, 09-sep-1963
•	Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE DESTRUCCION DE SEMBRIOS, 21-oct-2002

###### Art. 410

Whoever has felled, mutilated, or debarked one or more trees, so that they perish; or destroyed one or more grafts, shall be punished, for each tree, with imprisonment of eight days to one month and a fine of six to nine dollars of the United States of North America.  

In no case shall the totality of the penalty exceed three years as to imprisonment, nor thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America as to the fine.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .  
Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 109 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635

de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 411

Whoever has poisoned horses or other beasts of draft or burden, horned animals, sheep, goats or pigs, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to two years and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 110 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 412

Whoever has thrown into a river, canal, stream, pond, fish farm, or water reservoir, substances suitable for destroying fish, shall suffer the penalty of imprisonment of eight days to three months and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 111 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .  

Concordancias:

## LIBRO II)

, Arts. 612, 624, 627
•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 36


This excerpt sits under: CODIGO PENAL > LIBRO II)

###### Art. 413

Those who without justifiable cause have killed any of the animals mentioned in Art. 411, or have caused a serious wound or injury to them, shall be punished as follows:
If the offense has been committed in the houses, enclosures or dependencies, or on the lands of which the owner of the dead or wounded animal was the owner, settler or tenant, the penalty shall be imprisonment from one to six months and a fine of six to nine dollars of the United States of North America;

If it has been committed in places of which the guilty party was the owner, settler or tenant, the penalty shall be imprisonment from eight days to three months and a fine of six dollars of the United States of North America; and,

If it has been committed in another place, the imprisonment shall be from fifteen days to four months and the fine from six to nine dollars of the United States of North America.

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK II)

, Arts. 625

###### Art. 414

Whoever, without necessity, kills a domestic animal, other than those mentioned in Art. 411, or a domesticated animal, or has caused a serious wound or injury to them, in a place of which the owner of the animal is the owner, usufructuary,

user, lessee or tenant, shall be punished with imprisonment from eight days to three months and a fine of six to nine dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 113 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

, Arts. 624, 639

###### Art. 415

If in the cases provided for by the preceding articles there has been a violation of enclosure, the penalty shall be increased by double.
Art. ...- Computer damage.- Whoever willfully, in any manner or using any method, destroys, alters, renders useless, suppresses or damages, temporarily or permanently, programs, data, databases, information or any data message contained in an information system or electronic network, shall be punished with imprisonment from six months to three years and a fine of sixty to one hundred fifty dollars of the United States of North America.
The penalty of imprisonment shall be from three to five years and a fine of two hundred to six hundred dollars of the United States of North America, when it involves programs, data, databases, information or any data message contained in an information system or electronic network, intended to provide a public service or linked to national defense.

Note: Article added by Law No. 67, published in Supplementary Official Register 557 of April 17, 2002.

Art. ...- If it does not constitute a greater offense, the destruction, alteration or rendering useless of the infrastructure or physical installations necessary for the transmission, reception or processing of data messages, shall be punished with imprisonment from eight months to four years and a fine of two hundred to six hundred dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article added by Law No. 67, published in Supplementary Official Register 557 of April 17, 2002.

#### CHAPTER VII A

OF OFFENSES AGAINST CULTURAL HERITAGE

Note: Chapter added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

###### Art. 415

A.- Whoever destroys or damages property belonging to the Cultural Heritage of the Nation, shall be punished with imprisonment from one to three years without prejudice to compensation owed to third parties in good faith and to the judge, if feasible, ordering the

reconstruction, restoration or restitution of the property, at the expense of the perpetrator of the destruction or deterioration.

With the same penalty shall be punished whoever causes damage to an archive, registry, museum, library, educational center, scientific cabinet, analogous institution, archaeological sites or any property belonging to cultural heritage, without prejudice to the judge ordering the adoption of measures aimed at restoring as far as possible the damaged property at the expense of the perpetrator of the damage.

If the offense were culpable, the penalty shall be from three months to one year.

The damage shall be punishable when it does not arise from the normal use that should have been given to the property, according to its nature and characteristics.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

###### Art. 415

B.- The same penalty provided in the preceding article, if the act does not constitute a more severely punished offense, shall be applied to the public official or employee who, acting by himself or as a member of a collegiate body, authorizes or permits, against the law, modifications, alterations or demolition that cause the destruction or damage of property belonging to the Cultural Heritage of the Nation; as well as to the official or employee whose report or opinion has led to the same result.

The authorization given to intervene in the heritage property in order to ensure its conservation does not constitute an offense, if precautions are adopted so that in the execution the internationally accepted technical standards are respected.
Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

###### Art. 415

C.- The same penalty shall be applicable to those who, in violation of the laws and other legal provisions on the matter, traffic, commercialize or take out of the country archaeological pieces or objects, property of historical interest or belonging to the Cultural Heritage of the Nation.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

#### CHAPTER VIII

Of offenses against means of transportation and communication

###### Art. 416

Whoever renders useless or destroys, in whole or in part, the routes or works intended for public communication, by

land, by air or by water, or hinders the measures adopted for their safety, shall be punished with imprisonment from six months to five years and a fine of eight to one hundred fifty-six dollars of the United States of North America.

If wounds or injuries result, the penalty of four to eight years of major imprisonment; and if the death of any person results, the penalty shall be special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.
Note: Article amended by Art. 114 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Jurisprudence:

•	Judicial Gazette, MITIGATING CIRCUMSTANCES, 03-mar-1910
•	Judicial Gazette, OFFENSE OF INJURIES FROM SON TO FATHER, 09-jul-2001

###### Art. 417

Whoever employs any means to stop or hinder the movement of a train, or to cause it to derail, shall be punished:
1o.- With imprisonment from six months to three years, if no derailment or other accident occurs;
2o.- With imprisonment from one to five years, if a derailment or other accident occurs; 3o.- With major imprisonment of four years if any person is wounded or injured; and, 4o.- With extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years if the death of any person results.

Whoever executes any act tending to interrupt the operation of a telegraph or telephone intended for the service of a railway shall be punished with the penalties established in this article, and in their respective cases.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text.

###### Art. 418

Whoever throws blunt objects or projectiles at a train, streetcar or other vehicle in motion shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of eight to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America, if the act does not constitute a more severely punished crime.

Note: Article amended by Art. 115 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 419

Whoever executes any act tending to endanger the safety of a vessel, balloon, or floating construction; or to stop or hinder navigation shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of eight to one hundred fifty-six dollars of the United States of North America.

If the act produces shipwreck, damage, grounding, or any other serious accident, the penalty shall be minor imprisonment of three to six years; if any person is injured or harmed, the penalty shall be four to eight years of major imprisonment; and if it produces the death of any person, the penalty shall be special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.
Note: Article amended by Art. 116 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 420

Conductors, captains, pilots, mechanics and other employees of a train or a ship, or a balloon who abandon their posts during their respective services, before arriving at port or at the end of the railway or aircraft journey, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of eight to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America, if the act is not punished with a more severe penalty.
The imprisonment shall be three months to one year, in the case of drivers of motor vehicles intended for international, interprovincial or intercantonal transport.

Note: Article amended by Art. 117 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 421

Whoever, through imprudence or negligence, or through lack of skill in his art or profession, or through non-observance of regulations or ordinances, causes a shipwreck, derailment or other traffic accident shall be punished with imprisonment of two months to two years and a fine of eight to forty-seven dollars of the United States of North America. If any person is injured, harmed or killed as a result of the act, the penalty shall be six months to five years of imprisonment, according to the gravity of the act and its consequences.

Note: Article amended by Art. 118 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 422

Whoever interrupts postal, telegraphic, telephone, radio or other system communication, or violently resists the restoration of the interrupted communication shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years.

If the act is carried out in a gathering or gang, or the interruption is by violent means, acts of force or threats, the penalty shall be imprisonment of three to five years.

Those who offer, provide or commercialize telecommunications services, without being legally authorized, through concession, authorization, license, permit, agreements or any other form of administrative contracting, except for the use of internet services, shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years.

Included in this provision are those who are in clandestine possession of installations that, by their configuration and other technical data, give rise to the presumption that among their purposes is that of using them to offer the services indicated in the preceding paragraph, even when they are not being used.

The sanctions indicated in this article shall be applied without prejudice to the administrative and civil responsibilities provided for in the Special Telecommunications Law and its Regulations.

Note: Article amended by Law No. 38, published in Official Register 253 of August 12, 1999.

#### CAPITULO IX

Of piracy

###### Art. 423

The crime of piracy or assault committed armed on the high seas, or in the waters or rivers of the Republic, shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years.

Concordances:

•	CODIGO CIVIL (

## LIBRO II)

, Arts. 657

###### Art. 424

Those who sail in armed ships with two or more patents from different nations, or without patents or registrations, or other document proving the legitimacy of their voyage, shall be considered pirates, even if they do not commit other acts of piracy; and the commander or captain shall be punished with eight to twelve years of major imprisonment; and the crew members who are found guilty, with four to eight years of the same penalty.

###### Art. 425

Whoever, maliciously, delivers to pirates the vessel aboard which he is, shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years.

###### Art. 426.- All privateers shall be considered and punished as pirates

###### Art. 427

Whoever, fraudulently, traffics with pirates in the territory of the Republic, shall be punished as their accomplice.

#### CAPITULO X

Of crimes against public health

###### Art. 428

Whoever, with the purpose of obtaining a profit, has mixed or caused to be mixed with beverages or foodstuffs, or with substances or food articles, intended to be sold, materials of such a nature that they can harm health, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year and a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

If the materials mixed with the beverages or foodstuffs, or with substances or food articles intended for sale, could cause death, the penalty shall be imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of sixteen to sixty-two dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 119 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Jurisprudence:

•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME INVESTIGABLE EX OFFICIO, 31-Aug-1965

###### Art. 429

The following shall be punished with the same penalties and according to the distinctions established in the preceding article:

Whoever sells or offers for sale any foodstuffs, beverages, substances or food articles, knowing that they contain materials that can harm health or cause death; and,

Whoever has sold or procured those materials, knowing that they were to be used to falsify substances or food articles.

###### Art. 430

In the preceding cases, if the use of those products, altered or falsified, has caused a permanent injury of those defined in this Code, or death, the penalty shall be that determined in the articles dealing with injuries and unintentional homicide.

###### Art. 431

The foodstuffs, beverages, substances or food articles shall be confiscated and destroyed.

###### Art. 432

Whoever knowingly spreads a dangerous or contagious disease to persons shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of eight to one hundred fifty-six dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 120 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 433

Whoever intentionally poisons or infects potable water, or food or medicinal substances intended for public use or consumption by the community, shall be punished, for the sole act of poisoning or infection, with major imprisonment of four to eight years and a fine of sixteen to one hundred fifty-six dollars of the United States of North America.

If the act has produced illness, the penalty shall be major imprisonment of eight to twelve years; and if it has produced death, special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.
Note: Article amended by Art. 121 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 12, 264, 276, 411

###### Art. 434

When the acts provided for in the preceding articles are committed through imprudence, or through negligence, or through lack of skill in one's own art or profession, or through failure to observe regulations or ordinances, a fine of eight to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America shall be imposed, if no illness or death of any person results; and imprisonment of six months to five years, if illness or death results.

Note: Article amended by Art. 122 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 435

Whoever violates the measures adopted by the competent authorities to prevent the introduction or spread of an epidemic shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of eight to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 123 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 436

Physicians, pharmacists, or any person who, through lack of precaution or care, prescribes, dispenses or supplies medications that seriously compromise health, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to one year; if they have caused an illness that appears to be or is incurable, the imprisonment shall be one to three years; and in case of having produced death, the imprisonment shall be three to five years.

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 362, 363

###### Art. 437

A physician who lends his name to someone who does not have a degree to practice his profession shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to one year and a fine of eight to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America.
Note: Article amended by Art. 124 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

#### CAPITULO X-A

CRIMES AGAINST THE ENVIRONMENT

Note: Chapter added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

###### Art. 437

A.- Whoever, outside the cases permitted by law, produces, introduces, deposits, commercializes, possesses, or uses toxic hazardous waste, radioactive substances, or other similar substances that by their characteristics constitute a danger to human health or degrade and contaminate the environment, shall be punished with imprisonment of two to four years.

The same penalty shall apply to whoever produces, possesses, commercializes or introduces chemical or biological weapons.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 3, 15, 362

###### Art. 437

B.- Whoever violates the rules on environmental protection, discharging residues of any nature, above the limits established in accordance with the law, if such action causes or could cause harm or alterations to flora, fauna, genetic potential, hydrobiological resources or biodiversity, shall be punished with

imprisonment of one to three years, if the act does not constitute a more severely punished crime.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 14, 66
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK II)

, Arts. 612

###### Art. 437-C.- The penalty shall be three to five years of imprisonment, when

a)	The acts provided for in the preceding article cause damage to the health of persons or to their property;
b)	The harm or alteration caused is irreversible in nature;
c)	The act is part of activities carried out clandestinely by its author; or,
d)	The polluting acts seriously affect natural resources necessary for economic activity.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

###### Art. 437

D.- If as a consequence of the polluting activity the death of a person occurs, the penalty provided for unintentional homicide shall be applied, if the act does not constitute a more serious crime.

In case injuries occur as a consequence of the polluting activity, the penalties provided in articles 463 to 467 of the Penal Code shall be imposed.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

###### Art. 437

E.- The penalty of one to three years of imprisonment shall be applied, if the act does not constitute a more severely punished crime, to the public official or employee who, acting by himself or as a member of a collegiate body, authorizes or permits, contrary to law, the discharge of polluting residues of any kind above the limits established in accordance with the law; as well as the official or employee whose report or opinion has led to the same result.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 397, 398

###### Art. 437

F.- Whoever hunts, captures, collects, extracts or commercializes species of flora or fauna that are legally protected, contravening the legal and regulatory provisions on the matter, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years.
The penalty shall be imprisonment of two to four years when:
a)	The act is committed during the period of seed production or reproduction or growth of the species;
b)	The act is committed against endangered species; or,
c)	The act is committed through the use of explosives, toxic, flammable or radioactive substances.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

Concordances:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 3, 409

###### Art. 437

G.- Whoever extracts protected aquatic flora or fauna species, in prohibited seasons, quantities or zones, or uses prohibited fishing or hunting procedures, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

Concordances:
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK II)

, Arts. 642, 645, 646, 657

###### Art. 437

H.- Whoever destroys, burns, damages or cuts down, in whole or in part, forests or other plant formations, natural or cultivated, that are legally protected, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years, provided that the act does not constitute a more serious crime.

The penalty shall be imprisonment of two to four years when:

a) The crime results in the reduction of natural waters, soil erosion or modification of the climate regime; or,
b) The crime is committed in places where there are springs that supply water to a populated center or irrigation system.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

Concordances:
• CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 83, 406
• CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK II)

, Arts. 815, 970

###### Art. 437

I.- Whoever, without authorization or without complying with the procedures provided for in the applicable regulations, designates lands reserved for ecological protection or exclusive agricultural use to become areas of urban expansion, or extraction or production of construction materials, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years, if the act does not constitute a more serious offense.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

Concordances:
• CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 400, 409

###### Art. 437

J.- The same penalty provided for in the previous article shall apply, if the act does not constitute a more severely punished crime, to the public official or employee who, acting by himself or as a member of a collegiate body, authorizes or permits, contrary to law, that lands reserved for ecological protection or exclusive agricultural use be improperly designated for a use other than that which legally corresponds to them; as well as to the official or employee whose report or opinion has led to the same result.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

###### Art. 437

K.- The criminal judge may order, as a precautionary measure, the immediate suspension of the polluting activity, as well as the definitive or temporary closure of the establishment in question, without prejudice to what the competent authority in environmental matters may order.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

Concordances:
• CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 15, 66, 397

#### CHAPTER XI

Breach of sentence and certain concealments

###### Art. 438

The convicted person placed under special surveillance of the authority, who contravenes the provisions of Art. 61, shall be punished with imprisonment of fifteen days to six months and the execution of the sentence may not be suspended.

###### Art. 439

Those who have concealed or caused to be concealed a person, knowing that he was being pursued or convicted for a crime punished with imprisonment, shall be punished with eight days to two years of imprisonment and a fine of six to twelve dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 125 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 440

Excepted from the preceding provision are the ascendants, descendants, spouse, and siblings of the concealed fugitives, and of the accomplices or co-authors of the crime, and their relatives by affinity in the same degrees.

Case Law:

• Judicial Gazette, CO-AUTHORSHIP AND COMPLICITY IN CRIMINAL MATTERS, 14-Feb-2006

#### CHAPTER XII

ILLEGAL TRAFFICKING IN MIGRANTS

###### Art. 440

A.-Note: Chapter and Article added by Law No. 20, published in Official Register 110 of June 30, 2000. (coyote, coyotes, coyoterismo).
Note: Article repealed by Law No. 70, published in Official Register Supplement 427 of December 29, 2006.

###### Art. 440

B.-Note: Article added by Law No. 91, published in Official Register 716 of December 2, 2002.

Note: Article repealed by Law No. 70, published in Official Register Supplement 427 of December 29, 2006.

Art. ...- Whoever by illegal means, promotes, facilitates, induces, finances, collaborates, participates or assists in the migration of persons, nationals or foreigners, from the territory of the Ecuadorian State to other countries, provided that this does not constitute a more serious offense, shall be punished with the penalty of major imprisonment of four to eight years and a fine of twenty to forty unified basic remunerations.

The same sanction shall be imposed on those in charge of the protection and custody of children or adolescents, whether they be father, mother, grandparents, uncles, siblings or guardians or any other person who facilitates in any way the execution of this illicit act.

The competent Judge shall order the immediate apprehension and seizure of the clandestine transport used, when the same is involved in an accident resulting in dead or injured persons, and the proceeds from the auction thereof shall be allocated in the manner provided for in article 186 of the Code of Criminal Procedure.

For crimes concurrent with the offense of illegal trafficking in emigrants, an accumulation of penalties up to a maximum of twenty-five years of special major imprisonment shall be established.

When the acts of execution of the crime of trafficking in emigrants produce the death of the emigrant, the guilty parties shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years and if injuries provided for in articles 465, 466 and 467 of this Code are produced, the guilty parties shall be punished with the penalty of ordinary major imprisonment of eight to twelve years.

It shall be considered an aggravating circumstance to have known, or for it to be evident, that the means of transport used for the transportation of emigrants was in poor condition for use or did not have sufficient capacity for the number of persons being transported.

The same penalty shall apply to the owners of air, sea or land transport vehicles and to persons who are part of the crew or in charge of operation and driving, when their knowledge and participation in the illicit act is established.

Victims who disclose the identity of those involved in the commission of this crime shall be protected by the respective authority.

Note: Article given by Law No. 70, published in Official Register Supplement 427 of December 29, 2006.

Case Law:

• Judicial Gazette, ATTEMPTED ILLEGAL TRAFFICKING IN MIGRANTS, 16-Mar-2009

#### CHAPTER XIII

CERTAIN CRIMES PROMOTED OR EXECUTED BY MEANS OF TOURIST ACTIVITIES

###### Art. 440

C.- The person or persons who instigate, promote or carry out tourist activities with the purpose of committing or perpetrating the crime of kidnapping typified in article 188 and contemplated in Chapter III referring to "Crimes against Individual

Liberty", of Title II, Book One of the Penal Code; of crimes against persons contemplated in Title VI and particularly typified in Chapter I referred to crimes against life; in Chapter II related to "Injuries" and Chapter III relating to "Abandonment of Persons" of Book II of the Penal Code; of sexual crimes contemplated in Title VIII, in Chapter II relating to "Indecent Assault, rape and statutory rape", Chapter II pertaining to crimes of procuring and corruption of minors and Chapter IV relating to abduction; Book Two of the Penal Code, of crimes against property contemplated in Title X and particularly typified in Chapter I related to the crime of theft, Chapter II concerning the crime of robbery, Chapter IV relating to the crime of extortion and Chapter V referring to fraud and other defrauding of Book II of the Penal Code, shall be imposed the maximum penalty corresponding to the nature of the respective offense. Likewise, those who commit crimes provided for in this article against persons who have the status of tourists and knowing that they had such status shall be imposed the maximum penalty provided for the perpetrated offense.  

Note: Chapter and article added by Law No. 97, published in Official Registry Supplement 733 of December 27, 2002.  

Case Law:

•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF RAPE, 26-Jan-1914
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF INDECENT ASSAULT, 06-Sep-1993
•	Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF INDECENT ASSAULT, 14-Oct-1993

CHAPTER...  
OF THE CRIMES OF GENOCIDE AND ETHNOCIDE

Art. ...- Whoever, with the purpose of destroying totally or partially a national, ethnic, racial or religious group, perpetrates any of the following acts, shall be punished:
1.	Whoever causes the death of its members, shall be punished with a penalty of special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years.  
2.	Whoever causes serious injury to the physical or mental integrity of the members of the group, shall be punished with a penalty of ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years.  
3.	Whoever intentionally subjects the group to conditions of existence that are to bring about its total or partial physical destruction, shall be punished with a penalty of ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years.  
4.	Whoever takes measures intended to prevent births within the group shall be punished with a penalty of ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years. Information or access to family planning methods, contraceptive methods and sexual and reproductive health services, shall not be considered measures intended to prevent births.  
5.	Whoever forcibly transfers children of the group to another group, shall be punished with a penalty of ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years.  

Note: Chapter and article added by Law No. 1, published in Official Registry Supplement 578 of April 27, 2009.  

Art. ...- Whoever disrespects the self-determination of a national, ethnic, racial or religious group, or their will to remain in voluntary isolation, shall incur in the crime of ethnocide and shall be punished with a penalty of ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 1, published in Official Registry Supplement 578 of April 27, 2009.  

Art. ...- Whoever carries out, with awareness that the total or partial disappearance of human groups may occur, activities tending to influence, alter, or in any way change the culture, way of life or identity of peoples in voluntary isolation, shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment of two to four years.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 1, published in Official Registry Supplement 578 of April 27, 2009.  

Art. ...- The fact that the offenses typified in this chapter have been committed by a subordinate, shall not exempt from criminal responsibility the superior who ordered it nor the subordinate who executed it.  

In all these cases, the attempt shall be punished with half of the penalty provided for the consummated crime.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 1, published in Official Registry Supplement 578 of April 27, 2009.  
Art. ...- The actions and penalties for the crimes dealt with in this chapter shall be imprescriptible.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 1, published in Official Registry Supplement 578 of April 27, 2009.

### TITLE VI

OF CRIMES AGAINST PERSONS

#### CHAPTER I

Of crimes against life

###### Art. 441

Whoever by food, beverages, medicines, violence or any other means has, intentionally, caused a woman who has not consented to it to abort, shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment.  

If the means employed have had no effect, it shall be punished as an attempt.  

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 45
•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK I)

, Arts. 60, 61
•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 103
•	GENERAL LAW OF CIVIL REGISTRY IDENTIFICATION AND IDENTITY CARDS, Arts. 130, 132

Case Law:
•	Judicial Gazette, ATTEMPTED HOMICIDE AND INJURIES, 24-Jan-2000

###### Art. 442

When the abortion has been caused by violence committed voluntarily, but without intention to cause it, the guilty party shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years.  

If the violence has been committed with premeditation or with knowledge of the woman's condition, the imprisonment shall be from one to five years.  

Case Law:
•	Judicial Gazette, PREMEDITATION IN HOMICIDE, 22-Jun-2001

###### Art. 443

Whoever by food, beverages, medicines or any other means has caused a woman who has consented to it to abort, shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years.

###### Art. 444

The woman who has voluntarily consented to having an abortion performed on her, or causes the abortion herself, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years.  

If she consents to having an abortion performed on her or causes the abortion herself, to conceal her dishonor, she shall be punished with six months to two years of imprisonment.  

Concordances:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK I)

, Arts. 61

Case Law:

•	Judicial Gazette, ABORTION, 30-May-1997

###### Art. 445

When the means employed with the purpose of causing a woman to abort have

caused her death, whoever has applied or indicated them for said purpose, shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment, if the woman has consented to the abortion; and with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years, if the woman has not consented.  

Case Law:
•	Judicial Gazette, ACCOMPLICE TO LETHAL ABORTION, 31-Aug-2009

###### Art. 446

In the cases provided for by Arts. 441, 443 and 445, if the guilty party is a physician, obstetrician, midwife, practitioner or pharmacist, the penalty of imprisonment shall be replaced with minor imprisonment of three to six years; that of minor imprisonment, with major imprisonment of four to eight years; and that of ordinary major imprisonment with the extraordinary one of twelve to sixteen years.

###### Art. 447

Abortion performed by a physician, with the consent of the woman or of her husband or close relatives, when she is not in a position to give it, shall not be punishable:
1o.- If it has been done to avoid a danger to the life or health of the mother, and if this danger cannot be avoided by other means; and,
2o.- If the pregnancy results from a rape or statutory rape committed against an idiotic or insane woman. In this case, for the abortion the consent of the legal representative of the woman shall be required.  

Concordances:

•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66, 69


This excerpt sits under: PENAL CODE > BOOK II) > CHAPTER XIII

###### Art. 448

Homicide, wounds, blows and injuries shall be classified as voluntary unless proven otherwise, or the lack of intent is evident from the circumstances of the act, quality and location of the wounds, or the instruments with which they were inflicted.

Concordancias:
•	CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 66

Jurisprudencia:

•	Gaceta Judicial, TENTATIVA DE ASESINATO U HOMICIDIO SIMPLE, 17-mar-1886
•	Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE HOMICIDIO, 07-mar-1889
•	Gaceta Judicial, HOMICIDIO INVOLUNTARIO, 22-jun-1901
•	Gaceta Judicial, HOMICIDIO CULPOSO, 25-sep-1940
•	Gaceta Judicial, HOMICIDIO CALIFICADO, 27-ago-2001

###### Art. 449

Homicide committed with intent to kill, but without any of the circumstances detailed in the following article, is simple homicide and shall be punished with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years.

Concordancias:
•	CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 99, 100, 101
•	CODIGO CIVIL (

## LIBRO I)

, Arts. 64
•	LEY GENERAL DE REGISTRO CIVIL IDENTIFICACION Y CEDULACION, Arts. 131

Jurisprudencia:
•	Gaceta Judicial, HOMICIDIO Y ASESINATO, 25-oct-1976
•	Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE HOMICIDIO SIMPLE, 15-nov-1976
•	Gaceta Judicial, ASESINATO Y HOMICIDIO SIMPLE, 23-mar-1977
•	Gaceta Judicial, HOMICIDIO Y ASESINATO, 07-mar-1978
•	Gaceta Judicial, HOMICIDIO SIMPLE, 15-dic-1982
•	Gaceta Judicial, HOMICIDIO SIMPLE, 18-nov-1993
•	Gaceta Judicial, CUERPO DE DELITO DE HOMICIDIO SIMPLE, 23-feb-1994
•	Gaceta Judicial, HOMICIDIO SIMPLE, 20-nov-1997
•	Gaceta Judicial, HOMICIDIO SIMPLE, 08-sep-1998
•	Gaceta Judicial, INDICIOS Y PRESUNCIONES DE HOMICIDIO, 03-nov-1999
•	Gaceta Judicial, ELEMENTOS TIPICOS DEL HOMICIDIO SIMPLE, 26-feb-2002
•	Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE HOMICIDIO SIMPLE, 01-abr-2002
•	Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE HOMICIDIO SIMPLE, 07-may-2002
•	Gaceta Judicial, HOMICIDIO SIMPLE Y ASESINATO, 04-mar-2004
•	Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE HOMICIDIO SIMPLE, 24-abr-2007

###### Art. 450

It is murder and shall be punished with special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years, homicide committed with any of the following circumstances:

1a.- With treachery;  
2a.- For a price or remunerative promise;  
3a.- By means of flood, poison, fire, or derailment;  
4a.- With cruelty, deliberately and inhumanly increasing the pain of the victim; 5a.- When the victim has been rendered unable to defend themselves;  
6a.- By any means capable of causing great havoc;  
7a.- Purposely seeking nighttime or an uninhabited place to commit the homicide;  
8a.- With the purpose that the crime not be discovered, or that the offender not be detained, except when the killer is an ascendant or descendant, spouse or sibling of the offender whom they intended to favor; and,
9a.- As a means to prepare, facilitate, consummate or conceal another crime, or to ensure its results or impunity; or for not having obtained the results intended when attempting the other punishable act.  

10.	With hatred or contempt based on race, religion, national or ethnic origin, sexual orientation or sexual identity, age, marital status or disability, of the victim.  

11.	If it has been committed against members of the Armed Forces or National Police, prosecutors or criminal guarantee judges, in the performance of their duties.  

Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .  
Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo reformado por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .  
Nota: Numeral 10. agregado por Ley No. 0, publicada en Registro Oficial Suplemento 555 de 24 de Marzo del 2009 .  
Nota: Numeral 11. agregado por Ley No. 0, publicada en Registro Oficial Suplemento 160 de 29 de Marzo del 2010 .  

Concordancias:
