### Título XI

Crimes of function of police and military servants
Nota: Título agregado por Ley No. 0, publicada en Registro Oficial Suplemento 196 de 19 de Mayo del 2010 .


This excerpt sits under: CODIGO PENAL > LIBRO II)

#### Chapter I

Of common offenses of function

Art.	(602.3).- Insubordination.- The military or police officer or servant who commits any of the following acts shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year:

1.	Violently rejects, impedes, or resists compliance with a legitimate service order;  
2.	Makes requests in a violent manner to a superior;  
3.	Threatens, offends or outrages a superior;  
4.	Wounds or injures a superior, in acts of service; or,
5.	Removes armed troops from a unit, detachment or installation, without legitimate superior order.  

If the infraction is committed with weapons, publicity is made of it, it is committed in combat, state of exception or police operation respectively, the penalty shall be three to six years of ordinary minor imprisonment.  

Note: Chapter and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.4).- Sedition.- Military or police officers or servants who, through express or tacit agreement, in number of four or more, perform any of the following acts shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years:
1.	Disobey legitimate orders received;  
2.	Fail to comply with service duties;  
3.	Threaten, offend or outrage a superior;  
4.	Attempt to prevent the taking of office of a superior or remove him from his function; or,
5.	Act violently to make claims or petitions to the superior.  

If the military or police officers or servants perform any one or several of these acts in time of armed conflict, state of exception or police operation respectively, they shall be punished with three to six years of ordinary minor imprisonment.  

If the acts take place in a situation of danger to the security of the unit or of the service in the face of the seditious persons, resorting to weapons or assaulting a superior, they shall be

punished with the penalty of three to six years of ordinary minor imprisonment.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.5).- Conspiracy, proposal and apology of sedition.- Military or police officers or servants who engage in conspiracy or proposal to commit the crime of sedition and those who incite members of the Armed Forces or National Police to commit this crime or make apology of it or of those who commit it shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.6).- Failure to prevent or report sedition.- The military or police officer or servant who does not adopt the necessary measures or does not employ the rational means at his disposal to prevent sedition in the units or services under his command or who, having knowledge that an attempt is being made to commit this crime, does not report it to his superiors shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.7).- False alarm.- The military or police officer or servant who, without any justification, and with intent to cause harm, produces or disseminates false alarm for preparation for conflict shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year.  
Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.8).- Abuse of powers.- The military or police officer or servant who, in the exercise of his authority or command, commits any of the following acts shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year:

1.	Imposes punishments not established by law against his inferiors, or exceeds in their application;  
2.	Assumes, retains or illegally or improperly prolongs a military or police command, service, position or function;  
3.	Makes requisitions or imposes illegal contributions;  
4.	Orders his subordinates to perform functions inferior to their rank or employment; or foreign to the interest of the service; or urges them to commit a crime that endangers the security of the National Police or the Armed Forces;  
5.	Obtains benefits for himself or third parties, abusing hierarchy, rank, function, level or prerogatives, provided that this act does not constitute another crime;  
6.	Permits persons foreign to or unconnected with the institution to exercise functions that correspond exclusively to members of the military or police service;  
7.	Threatens, offends or outrages an inferior; or,

8.	Arbitrarily impedes the exercise of rights of an inferior.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.9).- Military or police contempt.- The military or police officer or servant who refuses to obey or fails to comply with legitimate orders or resolutions issued by civil or judicial authorities shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year, provided that the act does not correspond to a higher penalty in accordance with the provisions of this Code.  

This crime is also configured when the military or police officer or servant disobeys or resists complying with legitimate orders or resolutions of the Judicial Police, in their function as agents of authority and auxiliaries of the Attorney General's Office. The contempt or resistance offered by the military or police officer or servant based on the rank or hierarchical level held, shall be considered as an aggravating circumstance.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.10).- Destruction or rendering useless of property.- The military or police officer or servant who destroys or renders useless property intended for public security or national defense shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years.  

The penalty shall be three months to one year, when through negligence he loses, destroys or renders useless the property referred to in this article.  
Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.  (602.11).- Violation of correspondence.- The military or police officer or servant who, without due legal authorization, intercepts, examines, retains, records or disseminates correspondence or private or reserved communications of any type and by any means shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.12).- Crimes against public information not legally classified.- The military or police officer or servant who, using any electronic, computer or related means, obtains information to which he has access in his capacity as a police or military officer or servant, to later transfer, publish, divulge, use or transfer it under any title without due authorization shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year. The same penalty shall apply to whoever destroys or renders useless this type of information.  

If the disclosure or fraudulent use is carried out by the person or persons in charge of the custody or legitimate use of the information, they shall be punished with the maximum penalty.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.13).- Crimes against public information legally classified.- The military or police officer or servant who, using any electronic, computer or related means, obtains information classified in accordance with the law shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years. The same penalty shall apply to whoever destroys or renders useless this type of information.  

The disclosure or use of information thus obtained shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years, provided that another crime of greater gravity is not configured.  

If the disclosure or fraudulent use is carried out by the person or persons in charge of the custody or legitimate use of the information, they shall be punished with the maximum penalty.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.14).- Theft of property for police or military use.- The police or military officer or servant who steals war material such as weapons, ammunition, explosives or equipment for police or military use shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years. In the case of theft of medicines, clothing, food or other items that affect the employment of the National Police or the Armed Forces, he shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years.  
Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.15).- Robbery of property for police or military use.- The police or military officer or servant who robs war material, such as weapons, ammunition, explosives or equipment for police or military use shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years. In the case of robbery of medicines, clothing, food or other items that affect the employment of the National Police or the Armed Forces, he shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.16).- Purchase of stolen or robbed property for police or military use.- The

police or military officer or servant who acquires stolen or robbed property belonging to the National Police or the Armed Forces or intended for their employment shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.17).- Theft of requisitioned property.- The police or military officer or servant who, by reason of having carried out requisitions, has appropriated the requisitioned property shall be guilty of the crime of theft typified in this chapter.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

#### Chapter II

Offenses of function of police officers

Note: Chapter added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

##### Section I

Of offenses against the duties of police service

Art.	(602.18).- Acts against female or male citizens.- The police officer who commits deportations or illegal transfers, unlawful detentions, hostage-taking, coercion to serve subversive groups or deprives any person of their right to be tried in an ordinary and impartial manner shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.  
Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.19).- Evasion of responsibilities.- The police officer who evades their responsibility in acts of service, when this omission causes damages, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.  (602.20).- Alteration of evidence.- The police officer who puts at risk the obtaining or preservation of traces, material evidence or other elements of proof for the investigation of a crime shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year.  

The sanction shall be imprisonment of one to three years, if the police officer destroys or produces any alteration of the elements of proof.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

##### Section II

Of offenses against the duties of command

Art.	(602.21).- Abstention from execution of operations in internal commotion.- The police officer, service chief or police unit chief who in time of internal commotion and without justification by the situation, fails to undertake or fulfill a mission, abstains from executing an operation when required to do so, or does not employ in the course of operations all the means required by compliance with the precepts of the law and legitimate orders received, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years.  

Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

##### Section III

Of offenses against public or institutional property

Art.	(602.22).- Offenses against institutional property.- The police officer who shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year:

1.	Executes or does not prevent, in a police place or establishment, acts that may produce fire or havoc, or originate a serious risk for the security of a unit or establishment of the National Police; or,
2.	Conceals from their superiors serious damage or deterioration in installations, supplies or logistical material in their charge that is for use by police personnel or for service.  
Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.23).- Exceeding authority in the execution of an act of service.- The police officer who exceeds their authority in the execution of an act of service, and who as a consequence thereof, produces in a person injuries with an incapacity not greater than ninety days, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years. If the injuries caused generate in a person incapacity exceeding ninety days or permanent incapacity, they shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

#### Chapter III

Offenses of function of military officers

Note: Chapter added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

##### Section I

Of the particular circumstances of offenses of function of military officers

Art.	(602.24).- Territoriality.- Offenses of military function committed within the territory of the Republic or aboard military or merchant vessels or aircraft mobilized for service shall be punished.  

Offenses of function committed by military officers abroad, based on the principle of reciprocity, shall be tried by the judges or tribunals determined by Ecuadorian laws.  

Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

##### Section II

Crimes against the security, sovereignty and territorial integrity of the Republic

Art.	(602.25).- Crimes of treason against the Homeland.- Shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment
of six to nine years in time of peace; and with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years in armed conflict, the female or male military servant who commits one or more of these acts, even against allied forces:
1.	Desert to the enemy forces;  
2.	Facilitate the entry of enemy forces into national territory or onto Ecuadorian or allied ships or aircraft;  
3.	Carry out hostile actions against a foreign country with the intention of causing an international armed conflict for Ecuador;  
4.	Maintain negotiations with other States, tending to subject Ecuadorian territory in any form;  
5.	Declare themselves in rebellion while the Ecuadorian State faces international armed conflict;  
6.	Surrender to the enemy territory, plaza, post, position, construction, building, establishment, installation, ship, aircraft, armament, troops or force under their orders or defense materials; or induce or compel another to do so;  
7.	Give false or distorted news about the operations of the armed conflict;  
8.	Fail to give notice of the enemy's approach or of circumstances that directly affect the conflict, or the civilian population;  
9.	Prevent national or allied ships, aircraft or troops from receiving the aid and news sent to them, with the intention of favoring the enemy;  

10.	Lower, order to lower or force to lower the national flag, without order from the Command in armed conflict;  
11.	Fail to comply with a legitimate order or arbitrarily alter it with the purpose of harming the Armed Forces of Ecuador or benefiting the enemy;  
12.	Divulge news with the intention of instilling panic, discouragement or disorder in the troops, or execute any act that may produce the same consequences;  
13.	Maintain relations or correspondence with the enemy regarding the operations of the international armed conflict or of the Armed Forces of Ecuador or its allies; or, without due authorization, enter into understanding with the enemy to procure peace or the suspension of operations;  
14.	Release prisoners of war so that they may return to the enemy's armed forces, or return military equipment to the enemy;  
15.	Execute or order, within or outside the national territory, recruitment of troops to enlist them in the enemy's ranks, seduce Ecuadorian troops for the same purpose or provoke their desertion; or,
16.	Execute sabotage with the purpose of hindering national military operations or facilitating those of the enemy.  

Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.  (602.26).- Attempt, conspiracy and proposal of treason against the Homeland.- In
cases of treason against the Homeland, the attempt shall be punished in accordance with this Code; conspiracy and proposal shall be punished with a penalty of two thirds of that applicable to the perpetrator or perpetrators of the crime.  
Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  
Art.	(602.27).- Prolongation of hostilities.- Shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment
of three to six years, the female or male military servant who prolongs hostilities, despite having been officially notified that peace, armistice or truce with the enemy has been agreed upon.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.  (602.28).- Espionage.- Shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of
nine to twelve years, the female or male military servant who in time of peace:

1.	Obtains, disseminates, falsifies or renders useless information legally classified and whose use or employment by a foreign country would threaten the security and sovereignty of the State;  
2.	Intercepts, removes, copies information, files, photographs, films, recordings or others about troops, equipment, operations or missions of a military nature;  
3.	Sends documents, reports, graphics or objects that put at risk the security or

sovereignty of the State, without being obliged to do so; or, if obliged by force, does not bring such fact to the knowledge of the authorities immediately;  
4.	Conceals relevant information from national military commands; or,
5.	Alters, suppresses, destroys, diverts, even temporarily, information or objects of a military nature relevant to security, sovereignty or territorial integrity.  

If the female or male military servant commits one or more of these acts in time of armed conflict, they shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

##### Section III

Crimes against the security of the State

Art.	(602.29).- Rebellion.- Shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to
nine years, the female or male military servant who, in time of peace, commits one or more of the following acts:

1.	Rises up in arms, to overthrow the government or hinder the exercise of its powers;  
2.	Prevents the meeting of the National Assembly or dissolves it;  
3.	Prevents the elections called; or,
4.	Promotes, aids or sustains any armed movement to alter the peace of the State.  
If the female or male military servant commits one or more of these acts in time of international or non-international armed conflict, they shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.  

Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.30).- Omission in supply.- Shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment
of three to six years, the female or male military servant who, being obliged to do so by their function, refrains from supplying the troops for the fulfillment of military actions, putting the security of the State at risk.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

##### Section IV

Crimes against the operations and security of the Armed Forces

Art.	(602.31).- Attempt against the security of the Armed Forces.- Shall be punished
with imprisonment of one to three years, the female or male military servant who attempts against the security of the Armed Forces by breach of their duties or obligations in a willful manner, provided that the act does not constitute another crime.  

Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.32).- Attempt against the development of military operations.- Shall be
punished with imprisonment of one to three years, the female and male reservists who, in case of armed conflict, are called and unjustifiably do not appear within five days to perform military functions.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.  

Art.	(602.33).- Attempt against the security of military operations.- Shall be
punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years, the female or male military servant who, in non-international armed conflict, surrenders or flees without having exhausted the means of defense and security required by military precepts or orders received; and with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years if committed in international armed conflict.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

##### Sección V

Of the crime of desertion
Art.	(602.34).- Desertion.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year, the military servant who, in armed conflict, abandons for more than five days the post, service or function that has been assigned to them, in an illegal and unjustified manner.

Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.35).- Omission of notice of desertion.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year, the direct superiors or heads of units or detachments who do not report the desertion of their subordinates.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.36).- Special circumstances of desertion.- Deserters shall be

imposed the maximum penalty if the desertion is committed in conspiracy, in enemy territory, stealing, robbing or destroying the property of the Armed Forces.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

#### Capítulo IV

Offenses against persons and property protected by International Humanitarian Law

Note: Chapter added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.37).- Application of provisions in international or non-international armed conflict.- The provisions relating to international or non-international armed conflict are applicable from the day on which it takes place, regardless of formal declaration by the President of the Republic or of the decree of a state of exception throughout the national territory or part of it, in accordance with the Constitution and the law.

The state of international or non-international armed conflict shall be understood to have concluded once the state of exception has ended due to the disappearance of the causes that motivated it, upon expiration of the term of its declaration, by revocation of the decree that declared it; or, until the security conditions that had been affected are restored.
Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.
Art.	(602.38).- Persons protected by International Humanitarian Law.- For
purposes of this chapter, persons considered as such by the international instruments in force of International Humanitarian Law are considered protected, and in particular, the following:

1.	The civilian population;
2.	Persons not participating in hostilities and civilians in the power of the adverse party;
3.	Medical or religious personnel;
4.	Journalists on mission or accredited war correspondents;
5.	Persons who have laid down their arms;
6.	Persons who are out of combat or defenseless in the armed conflict;
7.	Those who, before the start of hostilities, belonged to the category of stateless persons or refugees;
8.	Political asylum seekers;
9.	United Nations personnel and associated personnel protected by the Convention on the Safety of United Nations and Associated Personnel; and,

10.	Any other person who has this status by virtue of the Geneva Conventions I, II, III and IV of 1949 and their additional protocols.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.39).- Homicide of protected person.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, kills a protected person shall be punished with special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.40).- Torture and cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment of protected
person.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, tortures or inflicts cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment on a protected person shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.41).- Collective punishments of protected person.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, inflicts collective punishments on a protected person shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years.
Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.
Art.	(602.42).- Mutilations and experiments on protected person.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, mutilates or performs medical or scientific experiments or extracts tissues or organs from a protected person shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.43).- Injury to the physical integrity of protected person.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, affects or injures the physical integrity of a protected person shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years, provided it does not constitute another offense of greater harm to the person.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.44).- Attack on the sexual and reproductive integrity of protected person.-
The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, injures or affects the sexual or reproductive integrity of a protected person shall be punished with special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years. This offense includes rape and other conduct that according to this Code affects sexual or reproductive integrity.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.45).- Deprivation of liberty of protected person.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, deprives a protected person of liberty shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years. This offense includes:

1.	Taking of hostages;
2.	Illegal detention;
3.	Illegal deportation or transfer;
4.	Forced displacement; or,
5.	Delay or postponement in repatriation.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.46).- Recruitment of children and adolescents.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, recruits or enlists girls, boys or adolescents in the armed forces or armed groups, or uses them to participate in the armed conflict shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.
Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.47).- Denial of judicial guarantees of protected person.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, deprives a person of due process guarantees or imposes or executes a penalty without having been tried in a judicial process shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.48).- Abolition and suspension of rights of protected person.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, declares abolished or suspended the rights, constitutional guarantees or judicial actions of protected persons

shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.49).- Attack on protected person for terrorist purposes.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, carries out any form of attack on a protected person whose object or effect is to terrorize the civilian population shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.50).- Arbitrary contributions.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, imposes arbitrary contributions shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.51).- Omission of relief measures and humanitarian assistance.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, omits relief measures and humanitarian assistance in favor of protected persons, being obliged to do so, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.
Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.
Art.	(602.52).- Omission of protection measures.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, omits the adoption of measures for the general protection of the civilian population, being obliged to do so, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.53).- Obstruction of medical and humanitarian tasks.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, obstructs or prevents medical, health or relief personnel to the civilian population from carrying out the medical and humanitarian tasks that they can and must perform in accordance with the rules of International Humanitarian Law shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.54).- Offenses against active participants in armed conflict.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, commits any of the following acts against an active participant shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years:

1.	Compelling him to serve in any way in the armed forces of the adversary;
2.	Depriving him of the right to a trial with due process guarantees; or,
3.	Preventing or unjustifiably delaying release or repatriation.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.55).- Attack on protected property.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, directs or participates in attacks against the following protected property shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years:

1.	Civilian objects that do not constitute a military objective;
2.	Property intended to ensure the existence and integrity of civilian persons, such as zones and localities intended to separate them from military objectives, and property intended for their survival or care;
3.	Property that is part of a peacekeeping or humanitarian assistance mission;
4.	Property intended for the satisfaction of the civil and political rights of the civilian population, such as those intended for religious worship, the arts, science or charity;
5.	Property that is part of the historical, cultural, or environmental heritage; and,
6.	Other property protected under International Humanitarian Law.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.56).- Use of prohibited methods in the conduct of armed conflict.-
The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, employs methods of warfare or armed conflict prohibited by International Humanitarian Law, and in particular, the following, shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years:

1.	Starvation of the civilian population, including through obstruction of supplies;
2.	The killing or treacherous injury of an enemy combatant or a member of the adverse party participating in the armed conflict;
3.	The use of the presence of a protected person as a shield to place certain points, zones or military forces safe from military operations, or to obstruct enemy actions against specific military objectives;
4.	The order to give no quarter;

5.	Attack on the civilian population as such;
6.	Attack on civilian property; or,
7.	Indiscriminate attack with the potential to cause death or injury to civilians, damage to protected property, or serious or disproportionate damage to the environment.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.57).- Destruction or appropriation of property of the adverse party.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, destroys or confiscates the property of the adverse party, without imperative military necessity, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.58).- Use of prohibited weapons.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, produces, possesses, stores, uses or distributes weapons prohibited by International Humanitarian Law, and in particular the following, shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years:

1.	Poison or poisoned weapons;
2.	Asphyxiating, toxic gases or other substances that produce the same effect;
3.	Biological, bacteriological or toxic weapons;
4.	Chemical weapons;
5.	Bullets which expand or flatten easily in the human body, such as hard-jacketed bullets which do not completely cover the interior part or which have incisions;
6.	Weapons whose primary effect is injury by fragments that cannot be located by X-rays;
7.	Mines, booby traps and other weapons with the same effect;
8.	Incendiary weapons;
9.	Blinding laser weapons;
10.	Anti-personnel mines, with the exception of those obtained by authorized personnel of the Ecuadorian Army, when they are intended to develop techniques for detection, clearance or destruction of mines;
11.	Cluster munitions; or,
12.	Other weapons which by their very nature cause superfluous injury or unnecessary suffering, or which have indiscriminate effects.
Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.59).- Environmental modification techniques for military purposes.- The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, uses environmental modification techniques for military, combat or other hostile purposes as a means

to produce widespread, long-lasting or severe destruction, damage or injury to the environment shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.60).- Use of prohibited means in the conduct of armed conflict.-
The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, employs any other method of warfare or conflict prohibited by International Humanitarian Law shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art.	(602.61).- Improper use of protective signs, distinctive emblems and national symbols.-
The military servant who, on the occasion and in the course of armed conflict, simulates the status of a protected person or improperly uses emblems, flags, insignia or other signs of protection contemplated in international instruments in force, such as, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year:

1.	White flag;
2.	National flag, military insignia or uniforms of the enemy;
3.	Insignia or uniform of the United Nations or other internationally recognized humanitarian organizations;
4.	Distinctive emblems of the Geneva Conventions of 1949; and,
5.	Emblems, denominations, distinctive signs of the Red Cross and the Red Crescent, in accordance with the Geneva Conventions of 1949 and their Additional Protocols.
Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

General Provisions

First.- The administrative disciplinary process corresponding to the same act being investigated in the criminal process may proceed independently without the need for a final judgment in the latter.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Second.- Execution of sentences.- Personal precautionary measures and sentences imposed on military or police servants shall be served in places that guarantee their safety; without prejudice to the jurisdiction of penitentiary guarantee judges.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Transitory Provisions

First.- Jurisdiction.- As of the publication of this law in the Official Registry, criminal guarantee judges and courts shall hear criminal proceedings for military and police offenses initiated prior to the entry into force of this amending law, in accordance with the jurisdictions established in the Organic Code of the Judicial Function; and shall continue to process them in accordance with the procedural rules that were in force when they were initiated and the provisions of the Code of Criminal Procedure, insofar as they are applicable. Said judges shall also hear proceedings for military and police criminal offenses that are initiated after the entry into force of this amending law, and shall process them in accordance with the Code of Criminal Procedure.

The National Court of Justice, through a resolution of the Plenary, shall regulate the transfer of pending cases to the ordinary jurisdiction.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Second.- Entry into the Judicial Function.- The members of the current military and police criminal courts and judges, as well as administrative personnel at all levels, may enter the judicial career in the Judicial Function, provided they comply with the provisions of literal e) of the fifth transitory provision of the Organic Code of the Judicial Function. In any case, no privilege shall be considered for entry into said career.
Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Third.- Collegiate bodies for military and police adjudication.- Immediately after the promulgation of this law, the Judicial Council, through the Judicial School, must establish in the initial and continuing training processes for prosecutors, female judges and male judges, training programs on police law, military law and International Humanitarian Law.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Fourth.- Personnel.- Within three hundred sixty-five days (365) counted from the entry into force of this law, the National Assembly must approve the law on police and military personnel that includes an update of the catalogs of disciplinary offenses or violations, as well as the competent authority to judge and the procedures, respectful of due process, to impose administrative sanctions.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Fifth.- Disciplinary offenses.- Until the National Assembly approves the law on police and military personnel that includes the classification and punishment of disciplinary offenses and violations, police and military servants shall be tried for the commission of disciplinary offenses under the provisions currently in force.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Repealing Provisions

First.- The Criminal Code of the National Police, promulgated in Official Registry Supplement 1202 of August 20, 1960, and all its amendments are hereby repealed.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Second.- The Military Criminal Code, published in Official Registry Supplement 356 of November 6, 1961, and all its amendments are hereby repealed.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.
Third.- All general and special provisions that are contrary to this Law are hereby repealed.
Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

BOOK THREE
OF VIOLATIONS


This excerpt sits under: CODIGO PENAL > LIBRO IV) > Título XI

### TITLE I

ON THE CLASSIFICATION OF CONTRAVENTIONS

###### Art. 603

For the purpose of procedure and imposition of penalties, contraventions are divided, according to their greater or lesser gravity, into contraventions of the first, second, third and fourth class; and the penalties corresponding to each of them are determined in the following chapters.

#### CHAPTER I

On contraventions of the first class

###### Art. 604

The following shall be punished with a fine of two to four dollars of the United States of North America:

1o.- Those who construct chimneys, stoves or ovens, in violation of the regulations; or fail to clean or maintain them, with danger of fire;  
2o.- Those who, outside the cases permitted by municipal ordinances, occupy sidewalks or portals with stoves, commercial articles and objects in general that interrupt or hinder the free transit of pedestrians, or those who transit through sidewalks or portals on horseback or in any vehicle; or who, through them, transport objects that by their form and quality, obstruct passersby;  
3o.- Those who introduce their animals into pastures, grazing lands or planted fields belonging to others that are fenced. The existence of this contravention is presumed by the fact that the animals are found in any of said places;  
4o.- Those who have left in streets, roads, plazas or other public places, pincers, crowbars, iron bars, ladders or other machines, instruments or weapons that thieves or other wrongdoers may abuse. In addition, said objects shall be confiscated;  
5o.- Those who without right have entered, or have passed or caused to pass their dogs, livestock or other animals, with or without load, through pastures or lands belonging to others that are fenced;  
6o.- Those who conceal their true name and surname from the authorities or police agents or other persons who have the right to demand that they disclose them, if the act does not constitute a crime;  
7o.- Those who refuse to receive legitimate and admissible currency, or wish to receive it for less than the legal value it has in the Republic;  
8o.- Those in charge of the custody of an insane person whom they leave abandoned in public places without proper supervision;  
9o.- Those who go out dressed in an indecorous manner or contrary to good morals;  
10o.- Those who bathe in violation of the rules of decency;  
11o.- Those who have on balconies, windows or rooftops, flowerpots or other objects, without the necessary precautions to prevent their fall or nuisance to passersby;  
12o.- Those who throw stones or other objects in public places, with danger to persons; or do so at houses or buildings, to the detriment of these, or with danger to their inhabitants;  
13o.- Those who cause any minor damage to public fountains, aqueducts, street lamps, or any other object of public service; or scratch, write on or dirty the walls of a building, without prejudice to civil compensation;  
14o.- Those who have uninhabited and open houses or shops;  
15o.-Note: Clause repealed by Law No. 31, published in Official Registry 231 of March 17, 2006.  
16o.- Those who transport objects, without police permission, during the night;  
17o.- Those who tie animals of any kind to trees or railings of garden plazas, public promenades or avenues, or to telegraph, telephone or electric light poles;  

18o.- Those who occupy any space of streets or roads when constructing their buildings;  
19o.- Those who post notices or any paper on the walls of public buildings or private houses;  
20o.- Those who do not paint or whitewash the exterior walls and balconies of their houses, in accordance with the regulations;  
21o.- Those who place awnings or curtains toward the street over the doors of their establishments, at a height less than two meters above the level of the sidewalk;  
22o.- Those who place notices or posters outside the cases provided for in municipal ordinances;  
23o.- Those who do not fence the lots or lands they have within the towns; without prejudice to the sanctions imposed in municipal ordinances;  
24o.- Those who, without being habitual drunkards, are found in any public place in a state of intoxication;  
25o.- The owners or administrators of any industrial or commercial establishment who do not post a permanent sign stating the name or business name and the purpose thereof;  
26o.- Those who in their shops, houses or properties in general, display inaccurate signs or inscriptions;  
27o.- Those who when selling an article alter its price announced in advance to the public; 28o.- Those who detain traders, or prevent the sale of any article of commerce;  
29o.- Those who perform works that hinder traffic momentarily or perpetually, except in the case of authorization or urgent repair; without prejudice to the provisions of municipal ordinances;  
30o.- Those who damage or torture an animal, even if it is to compel it to work, or for any other purpose;  
31o.- Those who kill an animal, without necessity;  
32o.- Those who govern animals with sharp or cutting instruments, capable of causing injuries;  
33o.- Those who employ wounded or mistreated animals in service;  
34o.- Those who on the walls of public establishments, such as hotels, boarding houses, cafés, casinos, spas, etc., etc., write words or phrases that offend morality, or draw obscene pictures, if the act does not constitute a crime;  
35o.- Those who publicly offend modesty, with indecent actions or words; 36o.- Those who do not aid or assist a person who in a public place is found alone, injured, mistreated or in danger of perishing, when they could do so without detriment to themselves, if the act is not punished as a crime;  
37o.- Those who tame horses within the towns;  
38o.- Those who have left wild or harmful animals loose;  
39o.- Those who have incited, or have not restrained their dogs when they attack or pursue passersby, even if they have not caused any damage; 40o.- Those who when encountering on foot, or on horseback, on the street, road or other public place, a person traveling in the opposite direction, dispute or obstruct the passage, instead of inclining to their right;  
41o.- Those who tear down, break or erase notices, mail lists and, in general, any

publication emanating from a competent authority or employee, thereby causing harm to the public, if the act does not constitute a crime;  
42o.- Those who close the doors of theaters and other public places, while there is attendance in them;  
43o.- Those who fail in the submission and respect due to authority, even when not in the exercise of its functions, provided that in this case it has been announced or made known as such, if the act does not constitute a crime;  
44o.- Those who engage in quarrels or disturbances in a public place, during the day;  
45o.- Those who, with speeches delivered in public, incite riots or rebellions, or disturb in any way the tranquility of the inhabitants, attack national prerogatives, or induce to commit any infraction, if the acts are not punished as a crime;  
46o.- Those who enter a house or dwelling belonging to another to provoke a fight or quarrel, if the act does not constitute a crime;  
47o.- Those who set off firecrackers or rockets in streets and plazas, or make bonfires, without special permission from the Police;  
48o.- Those who remain in a house or dwelling belonging to another against the will of the owner; 49o.- Those who fly balloons with flammable substances, or burn fireworks, without police permission, when the act is not a crime;  
50o.- Those who have wells without proper safeguards;  
51o.- Those who, through negligence or resistance, have not complied with the order issued by the authority to repair or demolish buildings that threaten to collapse;  
52o.- Those who do not maintain proper composure and moderation in temples, theaters and other places of assembly; and may be immediately removed by any of the police agents from the place of the contravention;  
53o.- Those who remain at the exit of temples, theaters, schools and colleges, forming groups of more than two, or cause annoyance to attendees; and,
54o.-Note: Clause repealed by Law No. 31, published in Official Registry 231 of March 17, 2006.  
Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978.  
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Art. 174 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

#### CHAPTER II

Of second class contraventions

###### Art. 605

The following shall be punished with a fine of four to seven United States dollars and imprisonment of one day, or with only one of these penalties:

1o.- Hoteliers, owners of lodging houses, lessors of furnished houses or apartments, owners and directors of gambling houses and transport operators, who have failed to record in a register kept for that purpose, the name, surname,

domicile, status, dates of entry and exit of any person who has slept, spent a night in their house, lodged, attended, or traveled, as the case may be;  
2o.- The aforementioned individuals who fail to send daily the statements that they entered the previous day in the register mentioned in the preceding number, to the first police authority of the place, or who fail to present that register when required by police employees or agents;  
3o.- Those who mock any religious act or the external demonstrations of a cult;  
4o.- Those who in any way oppose, impede or disturb the exercise and external demonstrations of a cult;  
5o.- Those who have within populated areas, factories or deposits of gunpowder or other explosive substances, or that produce mephitic, unhealthy exhalations, capable of damaging the air and making it bothersome for the inhabitants, when the action or omission does not constitute a crime and without prejudice to what fire and health laws or regulations provide;  
6o.- Those who violate the regulations and provisions of the authority regarding the custody of flammable or corrosive materials, or chemical products that may cause havoc;  
7o.- Those who in case of war, or when the competent authority so requires, travel without the corresponding passport;  
8o.- Those in charge of or committed to transporting persons or things, who refuse to do so without justifiable cause;  
9o.- Livestock or beast drivers who, through lack of precaution or foresight, are guilty of any damage; without prejudice to any other liability that may befall them on account of the damage;  
10o.- Those who, knowingly, transport in carts or other vehicles persons pursued by the authority, insane or intoxicated persons who are not heading to their domicile; unless in these last two cases they do so with permission from the authority;  
11o.- The chiefs, captains, or owners of vessels, railways or travel carriages, who do not submit to the corresponding authority a list of the passengers they transport, with indication of nationality, origin and destination;  
12o.- Those who carry out transactions on objects belonging to the State or intended for public use or service, such as weapons, military garments, or furniture from public establishments;  
13o.- Those who violate the regulations relating to the supply of towns; 14o.- Merchants who go about selling, through houses, or through streets, squares or any other public place, jewelry, clothing, or any other movable property, without prior written permission from the police. The police shall grant permission to persons who prove honesty; 15o.- Those who through lack of care, or for not having executed works, or not having repaired those made on their respective properties, do not have clear and in good service condition the public or neighborhood roads or paths, in the parts that correspond to them; 16o.- Those who when watering their properties flood public roads, or obstruct them with aqueducts or surface canals, or cause landslides of earth, stones, trees, etc., on said roads, if the act is not sanctioned by special laws;  
17o.- Those who cause any damage to a person or property through the fall or

collapse of any building or factory of their property, if the act does not constitute a crime; 18o.- Those who take as pledge movable property or livestock belonging to others to demand the fulfillment of an obligation, or the repair of damages;  
19o.- Those who do not deposit with the police, within three days, things belonging to others found in any place;  
20o.- Those who purchase outside of a fair any article of commerce or movable objects, jewelry or articles of clothing, etc., from unknown persons, or who do not have the corresponding police permission, from minors under eighteen years of age, not authorized for the sale, or from domestic servants; without prejudice to the return of the purchased objects to their owner, if the seller is not such;  
21o.- Those who practice the trade of divining, prognosticating,
explaining dreams, finding hidden treasures, or curing by means of certain artifices, without prejudice to the confiscation of the instruments or articles they use for such arts; 22o.- Those who take or remove fruits of any kind from orchards, gardens or fields belonging to others;  
23o.- Those who construct latrines, aqueducts, baths, drains, or any other similar work, without complying with the regulations on the matter;  
24o.- Those who violate the regulations issued on this matter by the authority. 25o.- Those who mistreat, insult or exercise acts of resistance against police agents in the exercise of their functions, if the act does not constitute a crime;  
26o.- Those who in their taverns accept intoxicated persons, or sell them liquors of any kind, or tolerate them to continue in them;  
27o.- The owners or administrators of taverns or gambling houses who admit minors in them;  
28o.- Those who give alcoholic or fermented liquors to a minor to drink; 29o.- Those who provide the same liquors to persons for whom there is prior prohibition, in writing, from the police or from their parents or guardians; 30o.- Those who open holes or ditches in streets, squares or roads;  
31o.- Those who publicly play carnival;  
32o.- Those who in places of which they are owners, lessees, tenants, usufructuaries or users, have maliciously killed or seriously wounded, to the detriment of another, a domestic animal other than those mentioned in Art. 411 of this Code;  
33o.- Those who reduce the weight, quantity or measure of an article at the time of sale; and,
34o.- Those who violate the regulations relating to the manufacture of fetid or unhealthy objects, to the establishment of tanneries, fireworks factories, dyeing plants and other factories that may alter the atmosphere with mephitic exhalations and corrupted vapors harmful to the health of the inhabitants, if the act is not sanctioned by special laws.  

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.  
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Art. 175 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

#### CHAPTER III

Of third class contraventions

###### Art. 606

The following shall be punished with a fine of seven to fourteen dollars of the United States of North America and with imprisonment of two to four days, or with only one of these penalties:

1o.- Those who, outside the cases provided for in Chapter VII of Title V, Book II of this Code, have voluntarily damaged or destroyed the movable property of another;  
2o.- Those who have caused the death or serious injury to animals as a result of the release of other harmful animals, or by the poor direction or excessive loading of vehicles, carriages, horses, draft animals, pack animals or riding animals;  
3o.- Those who, through lack of foresight or lack of precaution, cause the same damages by the use or employment of weapons, or by throwing hard objects or any substances;  
4o.- Those who have caused the same accidents due to the age, deterioration or lack of repair of houses or buildings, or by obstructions or excavations or other works made in or near streets, roads, squares or public thoroughfares, without the precautions or signs provided for in the regulations, or by custom;  
5o.- Those who take animals of any kind and at any time to graze in natural or artificial meadows, plantations or nurseries of fruit trees or other kinds, belonging to another, without prejudice to the corresponding civil compensation;  
6o.- Those who, finding a thing belonging to another, being authorities or police agents, do not deposit it with the police within twenty-four hours, or who, having knowledge of a finding, do not proceed in accordance with the provisions of the Civil Code;  
7o.- Those who conduct water across public roads or streets, provided they do so through uncovered pipes, without prejudice to being compelled to cover the pipes; 8o.- Those guilty of quarrels or nocturnal disturbances;  
9o.- Those who form meetings or gatherings for any political, religious, etc., demonstrations, without the corresponding written permission from the police;  
10o.- Those who construct projecting windows at a height of less than two meters above the level of the sidewalk or streets, without prejudice to demolition;  
11o.- Those who fight bulls, even in cases permitted by Law, or give any other public spectacles, even those not prohibited, without prior and special permission from the police;  
12o.- Those who remain for a long time and without any plausible purpose standing on street corners or other places not intended for the recreation of the inhabitants;  
Note: The unconstitutionality of numeral 12 of article 606 of the Penal Code is declared for contradicting the provisions of articles 76 numeral 2; 76 numeral 3; 82; 76 numeral 7; 11 numeral 2 of the Constitution. Therefore, its expulsion from the Ecuadorian legal system is determined. Given by Resolution of the Constitutional Court No. 11, published in Supplementary Official Register 641 of February 15, 2012.  

13o.- Those who spread false news or rumors relating to public order, the security of the State or national honor;  
14o.- Those who spread false news or rumors against the honor and dignity of

persons or families, or concern themselves with their intimate life, without prejudice to the action for insult;  
15o.- Those who direct at another a slight non-slanderous insult;  
16o.- Those who receive in their home, without the knowledge of the authority, runaway minors;  
17o.- Those who violate the regulations on public entertainment and spectacles; 18o.- Those who do not fence the land they have within populated areas, after having been required to do so by the authorities; and,
19o.- Those who cause any damage or harm to the installations or works intended for the provision of lighting, drinking water, or to the lights, lamps or lanterns, etc., intended for public service, if the act is not a crime.  
... Those who violate the right to privacy, under the terms established in the Law on Electronic Commerce, Electronic Signatures and Data Messages.  

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.  
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 67, published in Supplementary Official Register 557 of April 17, 2002.  
Note: Article amended by Art. 176 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

#### CAPITULO IV

Fourth class contraventions

###### Art. 607

The following shall be punished with a fine of fourteen to twenty-eight dollars of the United States of America and imprisonment of five to thirty days:
1o. Theft, provided that the value of the stolen things does not exceed fifty percent of a unified basic remuneration of the worker in general.  

2o.- Ministers of a religion who, in temples or religious places, streets or squares, preach against or in favor of a particular political party;  
3o.- Those who voluntarily wound or strike another, causing illness or incapacity for personal work that does not exceed three days;  
4o.-Note: Numeral repealed by Law No. 31, published in Official Register 231 of March 17, 2006.  
5o.- Those who establish gambling houses without permission from the authority, and those who attend such houses;  
6o.- Those who use things belonging to another without the will or consent of the owner, even when they do not have the intention of appropriating them;  
7o.- Those who have damaged urban or rural fences belonging to another, whatever the materials of which they are made, when the value of the damage does not exceed one hundred sucres;  
8o.- Those guilty of mistreatment of their domestics or servants, without prejudice to the

corresponding penalty if the mistreatment constitutes a crime;  
9o.- Those who fail, in any manner, with words, gestures, actions, etc., toward their ascendants, without prejudice to the corresponding penalty in case the act also constitutes another infraction.  

For the imposition of this penalty, the judge shall necessarily take into account the provisions of Art. 31 of this Code;  

10o.- Anyone who outrages a person by deed with slaps, kicks, shoves, whippings, stones, sticks, or in any other way, but without causing illness or injury, nor incapacitating them for work, without prejudice to the action for insult, in cases where there is ground; and,
11o.- Those who monopolize or attempt to monopolize negotiations on articles of daily consumption in butcher shops, market squares or other places, without prejudice to the confiscation of said articles and the other penalties imposed by municipal ordinances.  

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.  
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 66, published in Official Register 395 of March 14, 1990.  
Note: Article amended by Art. 177 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.  
Note: Numeral 1.- amended by Law No. 0, published in Supplementary Official Register 555 of March 24, 2009.  
Note: First paragraph and numeral 1. replaced by Law No. 0, published in Supplementary Official Register 160 of March 29, 2010.

#### CAPITULO V

ENVIRONMENTAL CONTRAVENTIONS

Note: Chapter added by Law No. 49, published in Official Register 2 of January 25, 2000.

###### Art. 607

A.- Any person who does the following shall be punished with imprisonment of five to seven days, and a fine of forty-four to eighty-eight United States dollars:

a)	Contaminates the air through emissions exceeding the permitted limits from vehicle exhausts;  
b)	Accumulates garbage on public roads, on land or in front of houses or buildings;  
c)	Makes noise due to lack of a muffler on their vehicle or through amplification equipment at high volume that disturbs public tranquility; or,
d)	Throws waste or contaminating water, destroys vegetation in parks or green spaces, in cases where such acts do not constitute a crime.  

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Registry 2 of January 25, 2000.  
Note: Article amended by Art. 178 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.  

Cross-references:
•	CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 14, 66

### TITULO II

SPECIAL PROVISIONS
REGARDING CONTRAVENTIONS SOLE CHAPTER

###### Art. 608

In everything relating to the punishability, liability or prescription of contraventions, which is not regulated in a special manner, the provisions of Book I of this Code shall be observed. However, the rules of Arts. 82 and 87 do not apply to contraventions.

###### Art. 609

Those sentenced to imprisonment, in accordance with the provisions of this Book, shall serve the sentence in the jails of their respective parishes or cantons; but in case these are lacking, they shall serve it in the jail of the provincial capital.

###### Art. 610

Minors under fourteen years of age and over seven, who are found playing, smoking or wandering in the streets, squares or any other public place, shall be immediately referred by the police to the respective Juvenile Court.

###### Art. 611

The damages caused by those over seven years of age and under eighteen shall be paid by the parents, guardians, employers or persons on whom the offenders depended, in accordance with the provisions of the Civil Code.  

Cross-references:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK IV)

, Arts. 2219, 2220, 2221, 2222

###### Art. 612

When the same action or omission constitutes two or more contraventions, the greater penalty shall be applied.  

Note: Article repealed by Supreme Decree No. 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978.  
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text.

###### Art. 613.- Reiteration shall be an aggravating circumstance

###### Art. 614

In case of recidivism, the maximum penalty established for the last contravention committed shall be applied.

###### Art. 615

For the graduation of penalties, the police judge shall take into account the aggravating or mitigating circumstances that accompany the act, as follows:

If there is one or more aggravating circumstances, the maximum;  
If there is one or more mitigating circumstances and no aggravating circumstances, the minimum.

###### Art. 616

In the duration of a police penalty, all the time that the guilty party has been detained on account of the same shall be counted.

###### Art. 617

The police action prescribes in thirty days, and the penalty in ninety days, both terms counted from the day on which the infraction was committed, or from the date on which the conviction became final, respectively.  

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978.  
Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text.

###### Art. 618

The penalty of special confiscation shall prescribe within the period established for the principal penalty; and civil convictions, according to the rules of the Civil Code.

###### Art. 619

In the event that prosecution for a contravention has already been initiated, the time of prescription shall begin to run from the last judicial proceeding.

###### Art. 620.- Prescription may be declared ex officio, or at the request of a party

###### Art. 621

The police are obligated to guarantee all kinds of civil or religious associations; but they shall prevent and dissolve those whose purpose is to disturb public tranquility or perpetrate an infraction; which is presumed if the individuals who compose them are armed or forming a disturbance.  

The police authorities shall issue the appropriate measures applicable to the case.

###### Art. 622

Whenever it comes to the knowledge of the Intendant or another police authority that an attempt is being made to commit, or that a crime or contravention is being perpetrated, they shall take adequate and timely measures to prevent the commission of the criminal act, or its continuation, even by using force; always subject to the corresponding provisions of the Code of Criminal Procedure.

###### Art. 623

Regarding the detention of a person suspected of a contravention, the corresponding

provisions of the Code of Criminal Procedure shall be observed.  

Cross-references:

•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 164, 165, 390

###### Art. 624

The use or carrying of weapons of any kind is prohibited, without prior permission legally granted by the competent authority.  

In the event that the authorities competent for the prosecution of contraventions seize weapons of doubtful origin, without valid legal permission, they must prepare the corresponding record, and shall immediately send the seized weapon to the Logistics Directorate of the Joint Command of the Armed Forces, and if the seized weapon serves as evidence of the crime, it shall be placed at the disposal of the competent authority.  
Note: According to Article 20 of the Law on the Manufacture, Import, Export, Commercialization and Possession of Weapons, Ammunition, Explosives and Accessories, of Supreme Decree No. 3757, published in Official Registry No. 311 of November 7, 1980, authorization to carry weapons corresponds to the Chief of the IV Department of the General Staff of the Joint Command of the Armed Forces.  
Note: Article replaced by Law No. 31, published in Official Registry 231 of March 17, 2006.  

Cross-references:

•	CIVIL CODE (

## BOOK II)

, Arts. 638
•	LABOR CODE, Arts. 46

###### Art. 625

To travel freely in case of war, or when the Executive requires it, the corresponding permission from the police authority, manifested in a passport, shall be indispensable.

###### Art. 626

The police and, in particular, the Criminal Investigation Service Office, are obligated to investigate and discover thefts and other infractions, as well as to ascertain the whereabouts of stolen or lost things.  

Stolen or lost things that are found in the possession of any person who does not possess them with any title of ownership, shall be secured by deposit until their owner claims them or they are auctioned in accordance with the law.

###### Art. 627

Anyone found in any public place in a state of mental derangement shall be apprehended by the police and, following examination by medical professionals, shall be admitted to a psychiatric hospital or an appropriate establishment.

###### Art. 628

The police are obligated to attend with their agents at theaters, circuses and, in general, any house, establishment or place where a public spectacle is to be presented, for the preservation of order and compliance with the respective programs.

###### Art. 629

Without written permission from the police, no public spectacle shall be held, except for those regulated by municipal ordinances.

###### Art. 630

Police penalties are independent of compensation for damages and losses caused by the offender.  

Cross-references:
•	CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 391


This excerpt sits under: PENAL CODE > BOOK IV) > TITLE I > CHAPTER V

###### Art. 631

Police authorities must prevent entry to the courts of their jurisdiction to persons who, when not acting in their own defense, attempt to proceed as shysters.  
FINAL CHAPTER

###### Art. 632

Note: Article deleted by the final article of the Code of Criminal Procedure, No. 134, published in Official Registry 511 of June 10, 1983.
